## Summary

The Item & Module Database is the game's read-only catalogue of the 23 physical things that can be cut free, carried, installed, held, spent or thrown: thirteen ship modules, three hand tools, a glowstick, five repair parts and a data core, each with a true mass, the pipes and wires it fits, and the one part that fully mends it. It exists because salvage is the game's only source of anything: nothing is bought or crafted, a salvaged module keeps the damage and the patch cap it died with, and a patch can only hold a module at 60 percent until a finite part cut from another wreck makes the repair last. Read it if you design anything that places, carries, installs, repairs or saves an item, or if you need a stable id or a weight for one. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: the count falls from 24 to 23 and the hand tools from four to three because `tool_suppressant` leaves the MVP roster (X6, D19; it stays a dormant reserved id, IDB-4); the stowed hull plate disables `run` (D18, IDB-13).)*

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Life-Support Graph (designed; hard: node kinds, port-type ids, M5/M6/M7)`, `Command & Simulation Layer (designed; soft: BOOTING order, no block)`, `Player Controller & Input (designed; soft: PC-8, V_CARRY)`, `Pixel-Perfect Pipeline and art bible (designed; soft: TILE_PX, sprite cells)`; downstream, all designed as of 2026-10-27 (each repeats its own clause in its own GDD): Suit & Equipment, Ship Section & Layout, Modular Ship Assembly, Derelict Authoring, Boarding & Salvage, Interaction, Emitter State, Save/Load *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: stale "undesigned/in flight" labels removed)*

## Overview

The Item & Module Database is the game's **static, read-only catalogue of every physical thing that can be cut free, carried, installed, held, spent or thrown** — twenty-three definitions ("defs") in the MVP *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: was twenty-four; `tool_suppressant` is removed from the MVP roster by the user ruling of X6 / D19, and stays a dormant reserved id, IDB-4)* across six classes (module, tool, consumable, repair part, curio, and a reserved junk class) — and each def fixes only what its thing *is* (a true mass, the ports it exposes, its ratings, the part that mends it) and what it *fits* (a Graph node kind and port types, a carry class). Nothing on a def says what the thing is *worth*: there is no price, rarity, recipe or loot weight anywhere in the catalogue, because salvage is the only faucet in the game and a thing's value is whatever the ship will not do without it. The catalogue is loaded once at start-up, before the kernel's BOOTING, as an immutable `ItemCatalog`; it registers **no kernel state block, no step hook, no command and no mutator** (the kernel's Interactions table has no Item DB row), so it can be called from `validate`, from P3 and from presentation as a set of pure queries. Two keys identify a def: the string `def_id` — the **only** item key that ever reaches a save, a snapshot, a `SalvageRecord` or a hash — and an append-only numeric `type_id` that exists for in-memory tables and later replication summaries and never reaches persisted data, so renumbering can never break a save. The Graph owns the loop ids, node kinds and port-type ids; the Item DB consumes them by number and never defines one. The Item DB also defines the single `ItemInstance` value schema that every holder (the Suit's per-player block, a loose-world placement, a Graph node about to be salvaged) embeds in its own block, because Suit, Assembly, Boarding and Save/Load must serialise the same shape; it stores, allocates and moves no instance itself.

The player never meets "the database"; they meet its consequences as things with a body. A Foundry scrubber cut from derelict A's dead bus arrives at the home-ship carrying the condition it died with — still dark, still FAILED — because a def never refreshes what the Graph recorded. It is hauled at the locked flat carry speed with both hands full and the chest lamp still lit, installed into a hardpoint it physically fits, and patched, which the Graph caps at `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000; only a **repair part**, itself cut from another wreck and spent once, restores the full ceiling and ends the patch's decay clock; cutting a patched module out and putting it back lifts nothing. A glowstick is one finite pool of Chemlight (`#86B896`) that counts as exposure like any other light. A carried module's mass is reported to the player by their own body (breath, strain — the Suit's consequence-channel cue), never by a number, a bar or a pack screen. And because every def carries a true mass and never a believed one, a phantom crate that looks like a data core can be caught by how it weighs (hallucination sub-class d, vertical slice) — the catalogue is the thing being lied about, never the liar. The two anchor numbers the rest of the game reads from it are small and exact: an item's `def_id` (what it is) and its `mass_g` (how much of it there is).

Without this system, salvage and welding have nothing to act on, and each neighbour (Assembly, Interaction, Suit, Derelict Authoring, Boarding, Layout) would invent its own item vocabulary until the save format broke on every module change — the concept's named technical risk ("save format not versioned from v1"). With it, the home-ship stays "a body assembled from the corpses of other ships" (concept, Core Fantasy) in the strictest sense: every object in it has a provenance, a dialect, a condition and a true weight, and none was bought, crafted or minted. The index flags this system as a **quiet bottleneck** — nine dependents (five in the MVP: Suit & Equipment, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Modular Ship Assembly, Derelict Authoring, Boarding & Salvage) — so the schema is frozen early, versioned from v1 (`catalogue_version`), and kept data-contract shaped even though `modes.workflow: full` requires the whole template.

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index rationale reads: "Definitions of salvageable modules (scrubber, reactor cell, hull plate, coolant unit, data core), tools, consumables and their dialect and weights; references graph port-type IDs that the Life-Support Graph GDD defines." Four of the five named modules do not map one-to-one onto a Graph node kind, and this GDD resolves them (IDB-10): the **scrubber** is `mod_scrubber` (node kind 20); the **reactor cell** is `mod_reactor_cell` (kind 10) and is *not* the battery cell, which is its own def `mod_battery_cell` (kind 11); the **coolant unit** is a roster family name over three defs (`mod_coolant_pump`, `mod_radiator`, `mod_coolant_reservoir`) because one def is exactly one Graph node; the **hull plate** is a *repair part* (`part_hull_plate`), not a module, because a HULL_SEGMENT has no `def_id` and `salvage_node` refuses it — a conflict with the index wording that is routed to the Graph owner (Open Questions); the **data core** is a *curio* with no Graph presence (`curio_data_core`, node kind 0), because the Graph has no data-core node kind and the concept forbids an authored story. "Dialect" is a sprite-and-port-shape field with no gameplay stat in the MVP; "weights" are true integer grams that change neither speed nor noise.

*Citation key.* `[LSG:n]` is a line of `design/gdd/life-support-graph.md`, `[KRN:n]` of `command-simulation-layer.md`, `[PCI:n]` of `player-controller-input.md`, `[PPR:n]` of `pixel-perfect-render-pipeline-camera.md`, `[IDX:n]` of `systems-index.md`, `[CON:n]` of `game-concept.md`, `[REG:n]` of `design/registry/entities.yaml`, `[ART:n]` of `design/art/art-bible.md`, all as read on 2026-10-26; rule ids (LS-n, CR-n, PC-n) are given where one exists. Tags: **[PH]** Item DB placeholder, not a Graph or registry fact, a registry candidate; **[DECL]** declared here, validated by the Graph at data load; **[INF]** inferred from the connectability rules, routed for a one-word confirmation; **(provisional — X undesigned/in flight)** a cross-system clause the named dependent must repeat when it is written. Implementation questions raised while designing are deferred to ADR candidates, marked "-> ADR candidate": the runtime object type of a def (constrained by IDB-26: a typed `RefCounted` `ItemDef`, never a `Resource`, never static state — the ADR records the choice but may not loosen the constraints), the export include-filter for `assets/data/items/*.json`, and the catalogue-hash function. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync: all three are decided by ADR-0007 (§ 1.5 runtime type, § 1.6 export filter, § 3.4 hash input; the hash encoding is ADR-0005's).)* The engine reference has **no module for this domain** (`docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/` holds animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui; there is no `scripting.md`, `core.md`, JSON, `Resource` or export file), so every engine claim here about data loading is MEDIUM confidence and spike-bound.

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: an Economy system has two fantasies, and this one leads with the direct one.** *(creative-director consulted; three framings weighed — "Organs From the Dead" leads, "The Weight Is the Truth" is the hallucination beat, "Every Part Reads Like Its Pipe" is the presentation beat.)* The direct fantasy is the one the player feels while handling a thing: **salvage-surgeon**. The indirect fantasy is what the catalogue makes possible inside other systems' moments: the home-ship becomes legible history.

> **Everything we own, we cut out of a dead ship and carried home — and it still remembers what happened to it.**
>
> The concept's Core Fantasy says the home-ship is "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships" [CON:46-48]. The Item DB is the anatomy of that sentence: a scrubber is not loot, it is a lung taken from a corpse, and it arrives with the corpse's injuries. Nothing in this world is minted, bought or crafted; every object has a provenance (the wreck it came from, its dialect) and a condition (the `hp` the Graph recorded, carried across the cut). The player's power is not owning things. It is **knowing what a thing really is** — what it fits, what it weighs, what state it is truly in — when the world, the scanner and their own eyes are under pressure to lie.

*Tone guard on the metaphor.* The body-and-organ language belongs to the design text and the player's own head only. It must never become visual or textual flesh: flesh tones and violet "appear nowhere in the sane world" [CON:333-334], no bare skin is ever shown [ART:508], and materials are cold metals only [ART:635-636]. Display names stay technician-plain (guard rail AP-6 below).

**MVP anchor moment — "the splice from the cold room"** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the review found that the headline scrubber and machine-kit loop below cannot be played in the MVP, because the `scr_a` carry has no destination and no `part_machine_kit` is placed (DA-25); the MVP experience target is re-anchored on a beat the MVP slice can play).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the "no destination" premise is superseded by user ruling D72 / DA-47, which gives `scr_a` a home slot, two vacant scrubber slots at home; the carry is now a decision with a payoff. The heater beat stays the MVP reference carry (Boarding C-BS-3); what remains Vertical Slice is only the `part_machine_kit` placement.)* It uses only MVP-placed fixtures and roster ids (Boarding BS-17, Derelict Authoring DA-25). (1) *Want and carry.* On the first sortie the reference payoff is the heater `htr_a` (`mod_heater`, 12000 g, MEDIUM) cut from derelict A and carried home at `V_CARRY`, hands full, chest lamp lit. (2) *The price shows.* Installed in the vacant home-ship socket it arms the bus overload (BS-17; DA-12): the trunk CABLE runs hot and begins to wear. (3) *Patch, hope on a clock.* A patch holds the cable at `PATCH_CAP_BP` and starts the decay clock; the player is now on the treadmill (G-0). (4) *The part, earned* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "earned" holds for the placed splice; a lamp scrapped on purpose also yields a `part_cable_splice` until Open Question 39 is decided, F-11 `supply_max`)*. In derelict A's `a_machine`, inside the lethal cold room, lies one `part_cable_splice` (DA-25): a 5.6-second crossing and out again. A full repair spends it and the cable's ceiling returns to `HP_FULL`. The alternative fix for the broken junction is the spare `mod_breaker_junction` on A (`a_life`): a module swap; the spare is placed at full condition (`condition_bp` 10000, registry `mvp_part_ledger`), so the swap itself spends no splice *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier clause said the spare's own full repair also takes a cable splice, which a spare at full `hp` never needs)*. The arc is want, exposure, patch under a deadline, then a small part that costs a risk to fetch. **Scarcity pushed inward**, with only MVP-placed content. ET-3 is measured on this beat; ET-2 is measured on this beat and, since DA-47 / user ruling D72 gave `scr_a` a home slot, on the install of derelict A's FAILED `scr_a` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Vertical Slice anchor moment — "the scrubber from derelict A".** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: relabelled; the beat needs a `part_machine_kit` placement and a destination for the `scr_a` carry, both Vertical Slice content.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the destination now exists, DA-47 / user ruling D72 supplies the home scrubber slot; only the `part_machine_kit` placement is still Vertical Slice content.)* It uses only locked fixtures and roster ids; placeholder numbers are tagged.

1. **Want.** In derelict A the beam finds a Foundry scrubber, FAILED, authored at `hp` 2000 [LSG:228]. It sits dark — no rotor turning, no medium moving. The home-ship's own scrubber is wearing.
2. **Cost before the cut.** Cutting it free (V8; the cutter lists the verb, Interaction owns the command) leaves its fluid ports `open_end` until they are capped [LSG:772]. The player hears the hiss they caused.
3. **Carry.** `mod_scrubber` is the one carried module (illustrative; in the MVP slice the `scr_a` carry has its destination, the vacant home scrubber slot of DA-47 / user ruling D72, and the reference carry used for the numbers below stays the heater `htr_a`, Boarding & Salvage C-BS-3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (`carry_ref` [PCI:131]): gait forced to `V_CARRY` 1 px/tick, **no run**, every `interact` mode except `drop` rejected `BUSY`; the chest lamp still lights the way [ART:512]. A 30-tile corridor (1,440 base px at `TILE_PX` 48) takes 1,440 ticks = 24 s carrying against 720 ticks = 12 s walking at `V_WALK` 2, and emits 1,440 `NOISE_PER_TICK` units against 720 (carry 1 per tick, walk 1 per tick, over twice the ticks [REG:5442-5457]). The module's mass (`mass_g` 40000, [PH]) is felt only as the Suit's body cue, never a number. This is the stretch where tells are heard (P2) and Attention is earned (P3).
4. **Install, then disappointment.** At home it is installed with the `hp` of the record, 2000 [LSG:184, 185]: still FAILED, still dark. The catalogue did not refresh it. The world never lies.
5. **Patch, hope on a clock.** A patch lifts `hp` toward, never past, `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000. At 6000 the Graph's `eff` (cited by name, D.4) is 750 permille and the scrubber runs DEGRADED at an output of 300 mGU/step — the figure the Graph's own tuning note describes as "equals one crew — any degradation loses the room" [LSG:927]. It runs, but with zero margin and a decay clock (`PATCH_DECAY_S` 240) ticking behind it. A bolted patch plate on its casing says "we did this in a hurry" [ART:617].
6. **The part, earned (Vertical Slice: no `part_machine_kit` is placed in the MVP, DA-25).** Only a `part_machine_kit` — a finite thing cut from another wreck — lets a full repair restore the ceiling to `HP_FULL` and end the decay clock [LSG:222]. The part, not the timer, is what makes the next sortie necessary. **Scarcity pushed inward.**

The emotional arc is want, exposure (the cut's hiss), dread (slow to flee, hands full) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "slow, loud"; F-13 shows a carried module is quieter per tile than a run and a stowed plate quieter than an empty run, so the cost is the lost escape, not volume)*, relief (home), disappointment (still dead), hope with a deadline (patch), earned competence (the part). It is the Project Zomboid "the base becomes self-sufficient" feeling [CON:52-55] with "survivable never quite certain" built into the item itself.

**Pillar alignment.** *Pillar 5 (primary), verbatim:* "The outer ring is kept unsafe by depletion and wear (scrubbers degrade, salvage runs out), so there is no safe place to farm forever." The catalogue is the vocabulary of the only faucet: a part is finite, a patch is capped, and the faucet is bounded by the authored node count. The other half of the pillar, "Deeper Is *Richer*", is delivered by placement and never by stat: no def carries a worth, dialect is a skin and `grade` has no behaviour, so richness deeper in the web is expressed by Derelict Authoring and Depth Rings & Scarcity through placement density, part supply (`part_cover_pm` by ring, F-11) and authored `hp` (better-condition modules deeper), never through def stats (binding, IDB-29 rule 5). *Pillar 1 (primary), verbatim:* "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong." Every def holds only true values; a module's condition is its output in the room and the scuffs and patch plates on its body, not a field on a card. *Pillar 4(d), verbatim:* "A crate of treasure is garbage once it is home. At least one cheaper field check exists (carried weight, a second scanner, the palette leak); the trip home is the fallback, not the only reveal." The Item DB supplies the true mass that check reads. *Pillar 3* is served by the carry gait and the glowstick pool; *Pillar 2* only indirectly, because a part or module is what surviving a tell costs and the Item DB fires nothing.

**MDA alignment** [CON:80-89]. The Item DB's felt contribution is *Challenge* (the repair economy: the part as the scarce input), *Fellowship* (the concept's rank-3 aesthetic, "crew roles (boarder vs. ship-keeper) ... a shared home-ship" [CON:84]: the haul is a division of labour. Each player has one `carry_ref` (IDB-2), so the hauler is slow to flee and hands-full (a carried module emits 1 noise unit per tick for twice the ticks of a walk, 48 per tile against 24 for a walk, and so less per tile than a run (64 per tile at 4 per tick), and a stowed plate is quieter than an empty run, 720 against 1920 units over 30 tiles: the plate's cost is the escape gait it removes, F-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier "slow, loud" claim did not match the F-13 arithmetic)* while a crewmate lights the way with glowsticks and guards the corridor; parts are spent per player (IDB-20 (7)) and move between crewmates only by being shed and picked up (States row 14), so who mends what is a crew decision *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the hand-over mechanism)*; and F-14's two-crew margin makes the second scrubber a shared crew goal), *Fantasy* ("a ship that is ours because we welded it") and *Discovery* (a module's real condition is learned by installing it and watching it, not by reading it). It must deliver *Competence* (reading what a salvaged thing is really worth from the world) and *Autonomy* ("which module", and "weigh a module's value against the noise of taking it" [CON:98]). It must never deliver Submission, which the concept deliberately omits: there is no comfortable stockpile.

**Design test (Item DB-specific, falsifiable).** *A module whose condition and worth are numbers on a card vs. a module whose condition is its own output in the room and the scuffs and patch plates on its body, and whose only cure is a finite part cut from another wreck → the module with the body and the part.* It resolves two live decisions. Should a def carry a `quality`, `rarity` or `value` field shown on pickup? No: `grade` is a reserved integer that is 0 everywhere in the MVP and has no behaviour. Should a free, infinitely repeatable action restore a module to full? No: a patch caps at `PATCH_CAP_BP`, a full repair takes a part, and cutting a patched module out and putting it back does not lift the cap or stop the decay clock either (IDB-18). *Secondary test (mirrors Pillar 4's):* **a weight that slows you vs. a weight your body reports truthfully → the truthful report.** Speed and noise never scale with mass.

**Experience targets** (playtest-checkable, not mechanics).

| # | Target | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| ET-1 | After the first salvage, **at least 6 of 8 testers** say where the home scrubber came from without prompting (forced free-text question after the first sortie, scored against a written key) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | provenance is felt |
| ET-2 | **At least 6 of 8 testers** who install a FAILED module (derelict A's FAILED `scr_a` at `hp` 2000 into a home scrubber slot, an MVP beat since DA-47 / user ruling D72, or the broken junction) reach for a patch within 30 s and none opens a menu *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "a Vertical Slice scrubber" is stale)* | the catalogue never heals |
| ET-3 | **At least 6 of 8 testers** explain in their own words why a patched module still needs a part (scored against a written key: names the cap, the decay or the finite part) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the cap reads as physical, not arbitrary |
| ET-4 | **At most 1 of 8 testers** asks "where is the shop" or "where is the crafting bench" within thirty minutes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the faucet reads as salvage-only |
| ET-5 (vertical slice, sub-class d) | **At least 6 of 8 testers** who carried a phantom crate home say "it felt too light" or "I should have checked" *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the weight is the unkind truth |

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks.** The catalogue is the thing being lied about, never the liar. "We know what our things really are" becomes harder and more valuable as sanity falls; it does not collapse, because at least one channel of truth always remains [CON:261-265]. The split is the Graph's: the **consequence channel** is a closed list (compartment temperature, O2 and pressure as the body feels them; frost, mist, scorch and drip trails; the acoustic classes at a descriptor anchor; Suit body cues — breath, visor fog, rime, shiver and the rest of Suit's SE-27 set (no vignette, SE-27); lamp brightness from `served_pm`; draught) and is never corruptible; the **module-body channel** (sprites, gauges, inspect cards, a module's own wear state) may lie [LSG:44, LSG:864].

| Hallucination class | In the MVP? | What the player sees (corruptible module-body channel) | What stays true | Effect on this fantasy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **(b) false system state** | **Yes** | The salvaged scrubber's sprite shows its rotor turning and no patch plate; its inspect card reads OK | The room still thins — breath, visor fog, draught, lamp brightness from `served_pm`; the def's `hp` and the Graph's output are what run | "My eyes say fixed; my breath says otherwise." The part you think you no longer need is the part you need. Deepens the lead framing |
| **(d) phantom salvage** | Vertical slice (VS-A) | A crate or module reads as a lure (a reactor-cell sprite) | Its true `mass_g` lies outside the lure's by at least `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` 300 [PH]; the carry body cue reports the true mass | "It breathed like a data core." The weight is the unkind truth |
| (a) phantom entity | not this system | — | carry noise is real and the Controller's | Carrying makes you slow and loud while things that may not be there move |
| (c) crewmate as horror | not this system | A crewmate carrying a module may render as a horror | the human channel (voice, their beam's colour and shape) is never faked [CON:217-219] | The crewmate hauling your scrubber home is still your crewmate |

Three rules follow, restating decisions rather than adding any. No def field is a believed, displayed or perceived value (`appears_as`, `displayed_as`, `fake_mass`, `lure_sprite` and any `hallucination_*` key are forbidden); a `junk` def holds only its own true data. There is no "unidentified item" mechanic: a def's identity is never hidden behind an identify step, and the player who learned what `part_pipe_fitting` looks like keeps that knowledge across a reload or a death, because Pillar 5's failure contract costs ship and body, never knowledge. Identification uncertainty comes only from Pillar 4 perception, which is falsifiable. **Risk carried to Suit & Equipment:** the carried-mass body cue must ride on a cue already in the closed consequence-channel list (breath, visor fog, rime, shiver and the rest of Suit's SE-27 set; no vignette, SE-27); if Suit proposes a new cue type (a shoulder-strain frame), its owner must either add it to the closed list or accept that it is module-body, corruptible, and so cannot serve as the sub-class d field check. Suit must say which. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: answered by Suit SE-27 and SE-28 — the load cue is breath with a load signature, and the HEAVY back-frame is on the Graph's closed consequence-channel list, so it is not module-body; see IDB-21.)*

**Reference techniques, taken precisely.**

| Reference | Adopt (exact technique) | Reject (exact technique, and why) |
|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid [CON:357] | Items are physical objects whose condition persists when moved; "we keep it running" is the base-building feeling; death is lethal and the world goes on (user decision) | Weight-scaled encumbrance that slows movement (conflicts with the locked single `V_CARRY` and `NOISE_PER_TICK` carry). A container screen with weight totals and capacity (no pack capacity in the MVP). Tool durability that breaks tools. Crafting recipes and recipe magazines (anti-pillar) |
| Satisfactory [CON:358] | A machine shows it works through its moving part and medium; a def's ports define what it fits, with no partial fit | Recipes, build-from-materials and tiered unlock; throughput scale-up. The word `tier` means power priority only |
| Oxygen Not Included [CON:361] | Stored quantities are real physical stock with exact conservation [LSG:203] | A build menu that materialises a machine from raw material — salvage is the only faucet |
| Barotrauma [CON:360] | Co-op hauling of physical items between hulls | Fabricators, deconstructors and outpost shops; weapon item stats |
| Pandorum [CON:364] | The hands-free chest lamp: the beam stays available while carrying; a carried module rides above the helmet and never covers the chest point [ART:512] | Any flashlight-as-held-item def — the lamp is not an item def |
| Beyond the Aquila Rift [CON:363] | "The kindness of the lie": phantom salvage is the lie that feels like relief, and the weight is the unkind truth | A lie with no check [CON:267-268] |
| Alien: Isolation (not in the concept's list; flagged as an added reference) | A scarce thrown light that changes what can be seen and what can see you: `cons_glowstick` is a pool that counts as exposure [ART:286] | Flares or noisemakers as lures with damage-adjacent effects — tool effects belong to Attention and Horror, not the def |
| Loot-driven action games (anti-reference) | — | Rarity colours, affixes, item level and "legendary" tiers; violet and flesh are reserved for sanity leaks [CON:333-334], so no rarity hue may use them |

**Anti-pillar guard rails this GDD states (concept wording, each with a lint).**

| ID | Anti-pillar [CON:285-298] | Guard rail in the Item DB | Lint or review hook |
|---|---|---|---|
| AP-1 | "NOT a freeform ship-building sandbox: welding is functional and modules are pre-shaped." | A def fixes `node_kind`, `ports[]` and its cell; none is editable, combinable or rotatable into a new shape; one def is one Graph node. No composite module | (lint) no def has `recipe`, `ingredients`, `craft*`, `build_cost` or `editable` |
| AP-2 | "NOT a combat game: no weapon tree. Tools may repel or distract ... nothing levels up from killing" | Tools list verbs only; the only charge-bearing def, `tool_suppressant`, is dormant and outside the MVP roster (X6), and any repel or distract effect is Attention's and Horror's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | (lint) no def has `damage`, `accuracy`, `armour`, `dps`, `xp` or `unlock` |
| AP-3 | No shop, no crafting; salvage is the only faucet (index brief, P5) | `salvage_yield` is the only function that turns a world event into an `ItemInstance`, beside authored placements; no currency exists | (lint) no `price`, `value_gold`, `sell`, `buy` or `vendor`; the registry `items:` entry never fills `value_gold` |
| AP-4 | "NOT intentional PvP" | No def has a friendly-harm or crewmate-targeting field | (review) no def field references another player |
| AP-5 | "NOT an authored linear story: no chapters or cutscenes; lore is found and assembled." | `curio_data_core` is a thing with mass and no behaviour in the MVP; its content is the Knowledge Codex's | (lint) no `quest`, `story_flag` or `lore_text` field; display text is a key only |
| AP-6 | Tone [CON:327-334; ART 6.2] | Display names are technician-plain nouns in the manufacturer's register ("scrubber", "battery cell", "pipe fitting"); no value adjectives, no flesh or organic naming despite the design metaphor. Final strings are the narrative-director's | (review) stale-token list adds `rare`, `legendary`, `epic`, `loot` |
| AP-7 | "NOT a game you win" | No def is a win or escape item; no "complete set" bonus | (lint) no `set_bonus`, `win` or `escape` field |

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Every rule below is a data-contract rule of the Item DB module (`src/data/items/` in the code root — location is an ADR candidate), stated so an implementer can write it and a tester can falsify it. Rules are `IDB-n`, numbered and named, as the Graph's LS-n and the kernel's CR-n are. Graph- and registry-sourced numbers are written by their locked name with a `[LSG:n]`/`[REG:n]` tag and appear once; every number this GDD introduces is tagged **[PH]** and is a registry candidate (Section G). Where a rule records an alternative that was considered and rejected, the rejection is part of the rule: a later change must answer it. Examples use only roster `def_id`s (IDB-31) or are marked "illustrative, not in the catalogue", because an example in a GDD is read as a contract.*

**IDB-1 — The Item DB is a static, immutable catalogue.** It defines what a thing *is* (mass, ports, ratings, repair part, yield) and what it *fits* (port types, node kind, carry class); nothing on a def says what it is *worth*. It registers **no kernel state block, no step hook, no command and no mutator** — consistent with the kernel's Interactions table (KRN:190-206) having no Item DB row and with CR-6 freezing the block registry at the end of BOOTING [KRN:98]. It is loaded once at start-up, before the kernel's BOOTING (ADR-0007 § 1.7; *Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync*), into an `ItemCatalog`, scene-tree free (kernel H10), read-only afterwards, constructed as `ItemCatalog.new(data, graph_tables)` with both inputs injected and no static, singleton or autoload state (so catalogue tests can build many catalogues in one process; IDB-26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, and callable from `validate`, from P3 and from presentation, because every query in IDB-28 is pure. It **rejects nothing**: every rejection reason that concerns an item (`BUSY`, `OUT_OF_CHARGES`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `NOT_REPAIRABLE`) belongs to Interaction, Suit or the Graph. *Alternative considered:* one WORLD block `items.state` owning every instance with its own id counter — rejected: it duplicates Suit's carried-module state (the index gives Suit "carried modules" [IDX:27]), adds a write path every neighbour must call, and contradicts the producer's advice to keep this system thin and data-shaped [IDX:143]. *Alternative considered:* a hybrid catalogue plus a small `items.loose` block — rejected: it invents ownership the neighbours may not want; the loose-world holder is an Open Question, not a decision made here (IDB-16).

**IDB-2 — Six def classes, one prefix and one numeric range each.** Every def belongs to exactly one class, named by its `def_id` prefix and bounded by its `type_id` range; the class fixes the default carry class.

| Class | `def_id` prefix | `type_id` range | Default `carry_class` | MVP count | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Module | `mod_` | 1000-1999 | `CARRIED` | 13 | One Graph node (IDB-6); installed, salvaged, patched, repaired |
| Tool | `tool_` | 2000-2999 | `HAND` | 3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was 4; `tool_suppressant` is dormant, X6)* | A hand-rail tool; never consumed (IDB-11) |
| Consumable | `cons_` | 3000-3999 | `STOWED` | 1 | A counted item spent on use (IDB-12) |
| Part | `part_` | 4000-4999 | `STOWED` | 5 | Consumed by a full repair (IDB-13) |
| Curio | `curio_` | 5000-5999 | `CARRIED` | 1 | Mass with no Graph presence (IDB-14) |
| Junk | `junk_` | 9000-9999 | `CARRIED` | 0 (reserved, vertical slice) | A true-mass decoy for phantom salvage (IDB-22) |

`CARRIED` means the instance occupies the single `controller.state.carry_ref` [PCI:131, PC-8]; `HAND` means it occupies the hand rail; `STOWED` means it is a counted entry in the Suit's block. The Item DB defines the class; Suit and the Controller own the slots (IDB-16). Capacity is 999 `type_id`s per class (a boot assertion): the class base (1000, 2000, ... 9000) is reserved and never assigned, so the usable range is base+1 to base+999 (orchestrator ruling, QA B8; F-8 and AC-73 pin it). The classes are fixed in the MVP; a seventh class would need a new prefix, a new range and a `catalogue_version` bump (IDB-27).

**IDB-3 — Two keys: `def_id` persists, `type_id` stays in memory.** `def_id` is a lowercase ASCII snake_case string `<class>_<family>[_<variant>]`, at most `ID_MAX_LEN` 40 characters [PH], matching `^(mod|tool|cons|part|curio|junk)_[a-z][a-z0-9]*(_[a-z0-9]+)*$` (digits are legal after the first letter: `mod_o2_tank` is a roster id). `type_id` is an unsigned 16-bit integer, append-only, never reused. **Only `def_id` ever reaches a save, a snapshot, a `SalvageRecord`, a hash or a replicated record**; `type_id` exists for in-memory tables and the vertical slice's replication summaries, so renumbering can never break a save. This mirrors the Graph's own contract: "`def_id` is a string and type ids are numbers" [LSG:832], with stable content type IDs under ADR-LSG-08 [LSG:26]. A dialect is a separate field, never an id token (IDB-23). A rename after release is an alias, not a new id (IDB-27). *Alternative considered:* a string key only (no compact in-memory key, nothing to bound a typo) and a numeric key only (contradicts the Graph's string-in-saves rule) — both rejected; the pair is kept in step by the bijection assertion of IDB-4.

**IDB-4 — The id ledger and the boot assertions.** A committed ledger `assets/data/items/ids.lock.json` lists every `(def_id, type_id)` pair ever shipped and the `retired_ids[]`. Boot refuses the build with `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(class, id)`, by analogy to `GRAPH_BOOT_TYPE_ID(table, id)` [LSG:1037], when any of these holds: two defs share a `type_id`; a `type_id` lies outside its class range; a `def_id` prefix disagrees with its class; a `type_id` is in `retired_ids`; a shipped `def_id` appears in the ledger with a different `type_id`; or a ledger pair is missing from the catalogue without a `retired_ids` entry or a `dormant` marker *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Formula F-8 is the assertion. **Dormant entries** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: a ledger pair may carry `dormant: true`, meaning the def is cut from the current build but its interface may return (X6). A dormant pair need not be in the catalogue, no other def may take its `def_id` or its `type_id`, and it may be re-shipped only unchanged (same pair). A dormant pair was never shipped to a save, so it needs no `renamed_from`. At MVP the only dormant entry is `tool_suppressant` 2004; it is distinct from `retired_ids`, which are never reused. Aliases (`renamed_from[]`, IDB-27) are resolved transitively to a depth of at most `ALIAS_DEPTH_MAX` 8 [PH]; a cycle, a self-alias, or an alias equal to a live `def_id` refuses to boot (`ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS(def_id)`), because an alias never creates a def and a retired string reused for a different def would let an old save silently load the wrong thing. The sim never iterates a Dictionary [LSG:64, LS-1 (i)]: the catalogue exposes arrays sorted ascending by `type_id` (`defs_of_class`), and a `def_id`-keyed map is for lookup only.

**IDB-5 — Fields every def carries.**

| Field | Type | Range / values | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `def_id` | string | IDB-3 grammar | Persisted key |
| `type_id` | int | IDB-2 range | In-memory key |
| `class` | enum | MOD, TOOL, CONS, PART, CURIO, JUNK | Derived from the prefix, asserted equal |
| `family` | string | snake_case | Groups dialect variants (IDB-23) |
| `dialect` | enum | `foundry` (MVP), `lattice`, `civil` (reserved) | Sprite and port-shape selector only |
| `name_key` | string | text key | Localisation key; **no player-facing string lives in a def** |
| `sprite_family` | string | art bible 8.5 grammar `<cat>[_<dialect>]_<subject>` | Presentation key; **never a `res://` path**. A module sheet (`mach_<name>`) names its frames by the state tokens `running`, `stopped`, `whole`, `cracked` and, for a lamp, the pair `lit` / `unlit` (In-World Instruments IW-17); the def lists the tokens its sheet draws in `sprite_states` |
| `sprite_states` | string[] | subset of `running`, `stopped`, `whole`, `cracked`, `lit`, `unlit`; default empty | Presentation only; read by `has_both_frames` (IDB-28); no sim code reads it |
| `cell` | enum | `M96` (96x96, 2x2 tiles), `P48`, `P48x96`, `P96x48`, `T80` (80x80 tool and overlay) | Sprite cell class [ART:1013-1055, 8.2] |
| `glyph_id` | string | tools and hand-rail consumables | The hand-rail glyph key: the item's sprite silhouette reduced to the 16 px grid [ART:815]; no bespoke icon |
| `mass_g` | int | 1..500000 (g) | True mass of one unit (IDB-21) |
| `carry_class` | enum | CARRIED, HAND, STOWED | IDB-2; asserted equal to the class default (a def may not disagree with its class) |
| `stack_max` | int | 1..99 | 1 for every CARRIED and HAND def, and for any def with `mass_g` >= `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` |
| `salvageable` | bool | default true for modules | Read by Interaction's `begin_cut` `validate`; the Graph does not enforce it [LSG:185] |
| `emits` | optional struct `{shape_id, base_pm, hue_class}` | any def that glows: a module (lamp, node lantern) or a consumable (glowstick); absent on every other def | Field *shape* defined in IDB-6; *semantics* are Emitter State's. A def with `emits` and no `lantern_on_states` uses the default of IDB-6 |
| `grade` | int | 0 in the MVP | Reserved for Depth Rings & Scarcity; **no behaviour**; never called `tier` (that word is the Graph's power priority); any future use needs a creative-director review (IDB-29 rule 5) |
| `renamed_from[]` | string[] | optional | Alias table, honoured only at restore (IDB-27) |
| `replaced_by` | string | only on a retired def | Restore remap (IDB-27) |

*Forbidden keys (lint).* A def has **no** field named `damage`, `accuracy`, `armour`, `range`, `durability`, `dps`, `xp`, `unlock`, `price`, `value`, `value_gold`, `sell`, `buy`, `cost`, `vendor`, `shop`, `recipe`, `ingredients`, `craft*`, `build_cost`, `editable`, `rarity_weight`, `drop_rate`, `set_bonus`, `win`, `escape`, `quest`, `story_flag`, `lore_text`, `appears_as`, `displayed_as`, `fake_mass`, `lure_sprite` or `hallucination_*`, and no key that makes one item produce another (the single exception is the `repair_part_id` to scrap-yield link of IDB-17). A forbidden key anywhere under `assets/data/items/` refuses to boot (`ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, field)`). The list is the machine form of guard rails AP-1 to AP-7. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `range` and `durability` added, because AC-22 forbade them while AC-08 did not list them; AC-08 now scans the same list, with the AC-08 extras `speed`, `noise`, `v_carry` and `carry_speed` folded in here as well.)* The extras are forbidden keys too: `speed`, `noise`, `v_carry`, `carry_speed`.

**IDB-6 — ModuleDef: the Graph-facing fields, and which are snapshotted.** One def is one Graph node: `install_module(slot_node_id, def_id, hp_start)` installs one `def_id` at one slot node [LSG:184, M5], and no composite def exists. A ModuleDef adds these fields to IDB-5.

| Field | Type | Range | Source / status | Graph consumer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `node_kind` | int | an LS-3 node-kind id | read-only mirror of LS-3 [LSG:68] | M5, M6 |
| `subtype_id` | int | 0 none; LOAD only: `1 HEATER`, `2 LAMP`, `3 DOOR_MOTOR` | the Graph names `HEATER` but gives it no number [LSG:68]; the Item DB assigns the numbers, append-only; the Graph adopted `1 HEATER`, `2 LAMP`, `3 DOOR_MOTOR` in its Item DB Interactions row *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: confirmed, no longer provisional)* | LOAD `subtype` |
| `ports[]` | list of `(port_type_id, cap, local_index)` | IDB-7 | **[DECL]** declared here, validated by the Graph at data load; ids from LS-4 [LSG:70] | node ports |
| `power_tier` | int | 0..4, or -1 for a node that is not a power consumer | the Graph's `tier` [LSG:68, D.6]: 0 life-critical (scrubber), 1 pumps, 2 doors, 3 lights and the HEATER, 4 other. Renamed `power_tier` in the def so that the word `tier` never means item value; the loader maps `power_tier` to the Graph's `tier` | LOAD `tier` |
| `req_w` | int W | 0..5000 | per-def table, IDB-8; D.6 `R_k` range 0-5000 W [LSG:425] | LOAD `req_w` |
| `req_max` | int W | `req_w`..5000 | M14 clamps a request to 0..`req_max` [LSG:193]; "the heater's `req_max` is a module-def cap, not a Graph clamp" [LSG:837]. **Read live through `def_id`, never snapshotted** (LS-3 persists LOAD `req_w`, `tier`, `subtype` and `energised_at_step` and no `req_max`); the table of values is IDB-8 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the review proposed snapshotting `req_max` with `req_w`; declined, because LS-3 has no `req_max` field and LSG:837 calls it a module-def cap)* | M14 |
| `waste_permille` | int | 0..1000 | per-def table, IDB-8 | D.6, D.7, D.8 heat |
| `hp_start` | int bp | 1..10000 (`HP_FULL`); the def default is `HP_FULL` (the Tuning Knobs range 7000-10000 is the advisory guidance for the *default*, IDB-9 is the boot rule) | contract IDB-9 | M5 argument |
| `rated_out` | int | per kind | SCRUBBER `R_SCR` (mGU/step); REACTOR `out_w_rated` (W) | D.5; D.6, D.9 |
| `stock_cap` | int | per kind | BATTERY `BATT_CELL` (dJ); O2_TANK (mGU); RESERVOIR `cap_ml` (mL); FUEL_TANK (mL) | store caps. Read live: a retune that lowers a `stock_cap` below the stock a saved node holds is a restore case (see "Restore against a retuned catalogue" below) |
| `duty_wear` | bool | false in the MVP | RADIATOR only [LSG:505, D.9]; SCRUBBER, PUMP, REACTOR and HEATER wear unconditionally [LSG:226] | D.9 |
| `repair_part_id`, `parts_per_full`, `scrap_count` | string, int, int | IDB-19; `scrap_count` is **pinned to 1** for every module def (range 1..1; 0 would make a DESTROYED salvage yield an illegal count-0 instance, so it is refused `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | roster table | `repair_part_for`, `salvage_yield` |
| `socket_tag` | string | snake_case | an authoring convenience Layout and Authoring may match on (provisional vocabulary); port types are authoritative | none |
| `emits` | `{shape_id, base_pm, hue_class}`, **optional on any ModuleDef** | `shape_id` a key of Emitter State's shape table (EM-8 [EM:167-179]): `FIXTURE` for a lamp (row 7, 3 tiles / 144 px); `LANTERN` for a node lantern (row 9, 2 tiles / 96 px, `LANTERN_REACH_PX` [EM:179]); `POOL_GLOWSTICK` for the glowstick (row 6); `base_pm` 0..1000; `hue_class` a colour token | the field's *shape* is defined here at the request of Emitter State (EM:354: "`emits` on any def that glows (module lantern or beacon, or consumable)"); its *semantics* are Emitter State's. The MVP defs that carry it are listed in IDB-31 (binding: Emitter State is designed, EM:5; its refresh request is answered here and closed on its side [EM:925: "No refresh owed to the Item DB"]) | Emitter State |
| `lantern_on_states` | set of LS-10 state ids | default `{0 OK, 1 DEGRADED}` [LSG:125]; meaningful only with `emits` | which Graph states count as running for a node lantern: `FAILED` is "no function" in LS-10, so a dead module is dark (Pillar 1: the world never lies); Emitter State asks for the set at EM:192 and EM:331 (binding: Emitter State is designed, and EM-13 leaves the set to the Item DB) | Emitter State |

The Item DB does **not** carry Graph tuning: `R_VENT`, `CH_CAP`, `RAMP`, `INRUSH_PM`, `INRUSH_STEPS`, `K_CAP`, `K_RAD`, `K_MACH`, `C_COOL`, `L_CAV_*` and `OVL_K` stay in the Graph's `GraphTuning` data file.

*Which fields are snapshotted at install and which are read live.* LS-3 persists on the node itself: `kind`, `def_id`, `hp`, `hp_cap`, `patched_at_step`, LOAD `req_w` and `tier`, and each port's `port_type_id`, `local_index` and `cap` [LSG:68]. It does not persist `waste_permille`, `R_SCR`, `BATT_CELL`, `cap_ml` or `out_w_rated`. So the contract is below. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: settled. The Graph's Item DB Interactions row states "The Graph reads `waste_permille`, `R_SCR`, `BATT_CELL`, `cap_ml` and `out_w_rated` live through `def_id` — never copied at M5, never persisted"; Open Question 8 is closed on that row.)*

| Class of field | Fields | A catalogue edit after a save exists |
|---|---|---|
| **Snapshotted at install** (copied into `graph.state` by M5) | `node_kind`, `ports[]` (type, index, cap), `power_tier`, `req_w`, the initial `hp` | Affects **new** installs only; a saved node keeps its old value until it is salvaged and reinstalled. Not a `catalogue_version` bump |
| **Read live by the Graph through `def_id`** (settled) | `waste_permille`, `rated_out` (`R_SCR`, `out_w_rated`), `stock_cap` (`BATT_CELL`, `cap_ml`, tank caps), `duty_wear`, `req_max` | Applies to existing saves at the next restore. A `stock_cap` edit that lowers a cap is **save-affecting** (the restore case below) and CI flags it |
| **Read live by other systems** | `mass_g`, `repair_part_id`, `parts_per_full`, `scrap_count`, `salvageable`, `emits`, sprite and glyph keys | Applies immediately; an edit to `repair_part_id` changes the part an existing saved node asks for at once, so the structural-diff tool prints it as a **save-affecting edit** (no `catalogue_version` bump, F-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

The split exists because "I retuned the scrubber and my save still has the old one" is a defect report waiting to happen. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the former ADR candidate "whether the Graph copies or reads live" is closed by the Graph's ruling; the classification is complete for every ModuleDef field in the IDB-6 table: `node_kind`, `ports[]`, `power_tier`, `req_w`, `hp_start` are snapshotted; `subtype_id` is persisted by the Graph as LOAD `subtype`; `socket_tag` is read by no Graph code; every other field is read live.)*

**Restore against a retuned catalogue** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the systems-designer found that the live-read decision creates two restores this GDD had not defined).* (a) **Stock above a live `stock_cap`.** Because `stock_cap` is read live, a catalogue retune that lowers `BATT_CELL`, `cap_ml` or a tank cap leaves saved stores holding more than the new cap. Stock is a Graph node field, not an `ItemInstance` field, so the Graph owns the behaviour; this GDD states the **binding requirement** on it: on restore, the excess above the live cap moves to the section's `discarded_*` counter (conserved, so the conservation sum closes) and is **logged** (a diagnostic naming the node, the old stock and the cap); it is never a refusal and never silent. Rationale: the Graph's `GRAPH_RESTORE_OUT_OF_RANGE` treats a value above its documented max as a hard failure, which would brick every saved game on a downward retune; the world must lose the excess the way it loses stock at a cut, by a conserved, visible rule. This is routed to the Graph owner (Open Question 31) and tested by AC-105, BLOCKED-ON the Graph. (b) **`cap_bp` against a retuned `PATCH_CAP_BP`.** The patched-module invariant is `cap_bp < HP_FULL` with no tie to the current `PATCH_CAP_BP` (IDB-15), so a saved `cap_bp` 6000 restores and reinstalls at 6000 even after `PATCH_CAP_BP` is retuned to 5500; the next PATCH takes `min(hp_cap, PATCH_CAP_BP)` (LS-23), and a PATCH on `hp` at or above the new cap is refused `ABOVE_PATCH_CAP` with `hp` never clamped down (M7). The Item DB never rewrites a saved cap. AC-106 tests it.

**IDB-7 — Declared ports: the Item DB declares, the Graph validates.** The Graph says modules *declare* `ports[] = (port_type_id, cap, local_index)` and "the Item DB does not define them" [LSG:68] — it never lists ports per node kind. The per-def table below is therefore **the Item DB's own declaration, validated by `connectable` at data load** [LSG:70, LSG:1038]; it is not a Graph fact and not a restatement of LS-4. Every row carries the tag **[DECL] declared here; Graph validates at load**. Port-type ids are the Graph's LS-4 numbers, cited by number and never redefined. The block below is a **read-only mirror** of LS-4 (`ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR(table)` refuses a boot where it differs from the Graph's boot table): encoding `loop x 100 + role`, roles 1 SOURCE, 2 SINK, 3 STORE, 4 THRU; `101 O2_SRC`, `103 O2_STORE`, `104 O2_THRU`; `201 PWR_SRC`, `202 PWR_SNK`, `203 PWR_STORE`, `204 PWR_THRU`; `301 CLT_HEAT_IN`, `302 CLT_HEAT_OUT`, `303 CLT_STORE`, `304 CLT_THRU`; `401 FUEL_SRC`, `402 FUEL_SNK`, `403 FUEL_STORE`, `404 FUEL_THRU`; `501 STR_ANCHOR`, `504 STR_THRU`; `901 ATMO_VENT`, `902 THERMAL_COUPLE`, `903 HULL_FACE`, `904 OPENING_FACE`. There is **no `102`**: oxygen consumers draw through the compartment, not a port. `connectable(a, b)` is the Graph's pure function [LSG:70]: same loop; THRU joins anything of its loop; SRC-SNK, SRC-STORE and STORE-SNK allowed; SRC-SRC, SNK-SNK and STORE-STORE rejected; `301` and `302` attach only to `304`; `904` only to `904`; `901`-`903` never carry edges. The node-kind mirror is LS-3's: `1 COMPARTMENT`, `2 EXTERIOR`, `10 REACTOR`, `11 BATTERY`, `12 CABLE_JUNCTION`, `13 LOAD`, `20 SCRUBBER`, `21 O2_TANK`, `22 VENT`, `23 DUCT_JUNCTION`, `30 PUMP`, `31 RADIATOR`, `32 RESERVOIR`, `33 PIPE_JUNCTION`, `34 HEAT_EXCHANGER` (vertical slice), `40 FUEL_TANK`, `41 FUEL_JUNCTION`, `42 FUEL_PORT` (vertical slice), `50 HULL_SEGMENT`, `51 FRAME_NODE` (vertical slice) [LSG:68].

The Graph's ruling is that a door motor heats its room only through its def's `waste_permille` (D.7), with no separate D.15 HEALTHY term; 0 stays the Item DB's choice, and a non-zero value would move the locked +21 degC baseline, so it needs the Graph owner's baseline re-run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the Graph answered, the "port is added with the heat term" wording is withdrawn)* |

*Rules for caps.* (a) A port `cap` is in the loop's unit per step [LSG:68]: mGU/step for loop 1, W for loop 2, **mL/step** for loops 3 and 4 [LSG:64, 72]. (b) **A fluid-port cap (loops 1, 3, 4) is binding**: it is one of the two inputs to the D.14 seam capacity at a weld [LSG:640, 644, 654] and a SEVERED stub leaks at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` of it per step until `cap_port` [LSG:772, 784, 960]. Fluid caps are therefore declared as the def's **rated flow where the Graph (or demand) supplies one, and otherwise as a [PH] throughput or loudness choice** (F-12 lists which is which), and the severed-leak column above is the audible price of cutting that port out of a line. **POWER-port caps (201-204) are declared at or above the Graph bound** (inrush-safe on `req_max`) because power has no inventory and a power stub leaks nothing [LSG:172]; the Graph reads a power-port cap only in D.14 at a seam, never in the island balance *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: answered by the Graph's Item DB Interactions row; was an Open Question)*. (c) The non-loop couplings `901`-`903` carry `cap` **0** (routed): LS-4 says they never carry an edge [LSG:70], so no seam capacity or stub leak is computed from them, and the Graph confirmed that it reads cap 0 on them as not applicable, not blocked *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `301 CLT_HEAT_IN` and `302 CLT_HEAT_OUT` are **loop-3 edge endpoints** — LS-4 says they "attach only to `304`" [LSG:70] — so they are fluid ports under rule (b): their cap is binding for D.14 at a weld and for the severed-stub leak, and they are declared at 40 mL/step, the pump's 304 cap, as a [PH] throughput choice (the Graph publishes no coolant mL/step rated flow: its D.8 `circ` is a permille, so a coolant circulation figure in mL/step is requested of the Graph owner, Open Question 33). (d) `local_index` is the declaration order, and it is what a hardpoint matches (F-6). (e) A catalogue lint runs `connectable` over every declared port against every Layout hardpoint declaration and reports a hardpoint with no compatible def [LSG:774], printing the distinct reason (`STORE-STORE`, `SRC-SRC`, `SNK-SNK`) so the author knows whether to insert a junction or choose another def. (f) F-12 is a **blocking** lint over every fluid port: it prints the severed leak and the `seam_cap_floor / rated_flow` ratio, evaluates the seam capacity by calling the Graph's `cap_seam` (D.14; the Graph publishes it as a pure function callable at data load, so the lint never carries a copy of the formula) and fails a cap below a rated flow that has an upstream source or a severed leak that floors to zero. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a per-port `rated_flow` column typed by the same author as the cap would make the lint circular, so the lint reads rated flow only where an upstream value exists.)* (g) **`POWER_CAP_LINT` (BLOCKING, boot or content lint)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the power caps were hand-copied, unlinted and could be invalidated by a legal knob edit)*: for every consumer power port (202), `cap >= req_max x INRUSH_PM_MAX / 1000` (the Graph's upper bound 3000 [LSG:933]); for `mod_reactor_cell` 201, `cap >= out_w_rated`; for 203 and 204, `cap` at or above the largest cable cap in any authored section (a lint against Authoring's data). The shipped caps (1800, 1200, 3000, 120, 360, 2500) are the equalities; raising `req_max` or `out_w_rated` therefore fails the lint until the cap is raised with it. Paired knobs share a **joint range** in Tuning Knobs (`req_w` <= `req_max`; `R_SCR` <= the 101 cap), so no single-knob safe range admits a combination the boot refuses. *Decided by:* LS-3, LS-4, [LSG:774]; Pillar 1 — a slot either fits or it does not, and a cut port is heard.

It omits the beacon (+20 W), the heater's own install inrush (+250 W), the battery that D.6 adds to the reactor, and the trunk cable cap of 2161 W, which binds before the reactor. Authored derelict nodes override `hp` per placement (the derelict-A scrubber at 2000, the broken breaker at 2000 [LSG:228]); those numbers are Authoring's, not the def's. `PATCH_CAP_BP`, `HP_OK`, `HP_FAIL`, `V_CARRY`, `NOISE_PER_TICK`, `TILE_PX`, `TICK_RATE` and `PATCH_DECAY_S` are used by name only and are never given a second value here.

**Power budget** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The binding constraints on the home-ship bus are two: the reactor rating `out_w_rated` 2500 W plus, for a short deficit, the battery (the Graph's D.6 serves `S + D`, `D` being the cell's discharge up to its `BATT_DIS_CAP`, a Graph default of 2000 W that this GDD's battery def does not yet carry: Open Question 50), and the **trunk cable cap** `cab_trunk` 2161 W (Derelict Authoring DA-20, window 2159 to 2163), which is lower than the reactor and so binds first for anything that rides the trunk. Standing load is `HOME_STANDING_LOAD_W` 1880 W (DA-20, both door motors and one scrubber included), the beacon lamp adds `BEACON_W` 20 W when lit (DA-24), and an inrush surplus is `floor(req_w x (INRUSH_PM - 1000) / 1000)` for `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 steps (0.5 s) at the defaults 1500 and 5 [LSG:933]. Loads in watts, beacon lit unless marked:

| Moment | Load (W) | Margin to trunk cap 2161 | Margin to `out_w_rated` 2500 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standing, beacon dark | 1880 | +281 | +620 |
| Standing + beacon | 1900 | +261 | +600 |
| + both door motors re-energise (surplus 2 x 60 = 120) | 2020 | +141 | +480 |
| + `htr_a` steady (500) | 2400 | **-239 (overload, authored: DA-47 PWR-OVL)** | +100 |
| + `htr_a` install inrush (surplus 250, 5 steps) | 2650 (2630 beacon dark) | -489 | **-150** (-130 beacon dark; the battery covers it) |
| `htr_a` steady + both doors re-energise | 2520 | -359 | **-20** (the battery covers it) |
| + one more `mod_scrubber` (600), no heater | 2500 | -339 (the harsher mistake of DA-20: destroyed in about 2.1 min) | 0 |
| + one more scrubber install inrush (surplus 300) | 2800 | -639 | -300 |

Reading it: the reactor shortfalls (-150, -20 W) last 5 steps and are covered by the battery while it holds charge, so the tier-3 shed that Open Question 42 feared occurs only on an empty cell, a SCRAM or a starved reactor; the trunk, not the reactor, is the real ceiling, and it is exceeded by the heater beat **by design** (DA-47 authors the trunk overload as a player-made fault, the anchor beat's "price shows"). Raising `out_w_rated` to 3000 W would change none of the trunk rows. **Maximum sustained crew:** the number of scrubbers `S` that fit is `1 + floor((cap - 1900) / 600)`, which is 1 on the trunk (2161) and 2 on the reactor (2500), so the trunk binds and `S` = 1; one scrubber (`out` 300 patched, 400 at `HP_FULL`) holds `N` = 1 at margin 0 (patched) or +100 (full). The MVP air economy therefore supports **one crew member in steady state**, which is what G-0 already says; `N` = 2 to 4 are timed on the O2 tank (F-14) until a second scrubber is fed by a stronger trunk or reactor, which is Vertical Slice content (Open Question 48). **Lint (BLOCKING, `POWER_BUDGET`, a proposed id for Derelict Authoring's `content_lint_ids`, which owns lint ids; it runs at content lint, not at boot, because the standing load is Authoring's number)**: the lint refuses a reactor `out_w_rated` below `HOME_STANDING_LOAD_W` + `BEACON_W` + the door-motor inrush surplus (2020 W at the defaults), which is why the Tuning Knobs range now starts at 2100 W.

**IDB-9 — The `hp_start` contract.** The def's `hp_start` (10000) is the `hp` of a **fresh** module: one an author places in a template or a catalogue spawns. A salvaged module is reinstalled with `hp_start := SalvageRecord.hp`, because M5 takes `hp_start` as an argument [LSG:184] and M6 returns `hp` [LSG:185]; its cap and decay clock ride with it through the extra M5 arguments `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step` (IDB-18, settled by the Graph's LS-18 M5/M6 amendment) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional" removed)*. The Item DB never edits `hp`. Wear is the Graph's and appears on the module itself (Pillar 1): its output falls through D.4, its WORN descriptor is module-body channel [LSG:44, LS-14], and its weight is a Suit body cue. A def with `hp_start` 0 or above `HP_FULL` refuses to boot (`ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`), because a node installed at 0 is a DESTROYED node leaking at its maximum [LSG:772].

**IDB-10 — The index's named modules map onto defs.**

| Index word | Resolution | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| scrubber | `mod_scrubber`, node kind 20 | unambiguous |
| reactor cell | `mod_reactor_cell`, node kind 10; and, **separately**, `mod_battery_cell`, node kind 11 | A reactor cell is not a battery cell: derelict A holds a partially charged BATTERY [LSG:228] and derelict B a live REACTOR (derelict B also places one `mod_battery_cell` for its residual island, Derelict Authoring OQ-DA-27; no value changes); `BATT_CELL` is the Graph's battery term [LSG:932] |
| coolant unit | a **roster family name** over three defs: `mod_coolant_pump` (30), `mod_radiator` (31), `mod_coolant_reservoir` (32) | One def is one node; no composite def exists |
| hull plate | class `part`: `part_hull_plate` (IDB-13) — **not** a module def | A HULL_SEGMENT has an empty `def_id` [LSG:68] and is excluded from `salvage_node` [LSG:185]; a DESTROYED segment "is replaced through its hardpoint" [LSG:771] while M5 accepts only ABSENT or SALVAGED slots [LSG:184]. **Conflict with the index wording** (the index calls the hull plate a salvageable module [IDX:20]); the replacement path is undefined in the Graph and is routed (Open Questions). The Graph is not changed here |
| data core | `curio_data_core`, `node_kind` 0 | LS-3 has no data-core kind; no node kind is added (the Graph's append-only rule is its owner's to exercise, and the MVP buys nothing from it) |

**IDB-11 — ToolDef: identity, hand slot, verbs, mass, glyph. No durability, no damage.** A ToolDef adds to IDB-5: `verbs[]` (**string[]**: the `verb_id` tokens the tool enables, spelled exactly as Interaction's IA-2 declaration table spells them, a V-id such as `"V8"` or an Interaction id; the Item DB defines **no verb** and allocates no id) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the field was `int[]` of V-numbers, but the seam-cut verb has no V-number in the Graph's list, so it could not be stored; IA-2's `verb_id` is "a V-id or an Interaction id", so the field is string-keyed and the allocation of any new id stays Interaction's, Open Question 52)*, and `charges_max` (int: 0 for a tool with no charge); `glyph_id` is the IDB-5 field, valued for every tool. A tool is never consumed, has no condition and no durability; no MVP tool has any per-use state: every shipped tool has `charges_max` 0 and `charge` 0, and the field survives only so the dormant `tool_suppressant` (below) needs no schema change if it returns *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Tools are drawn once as an anchor sprite layer and never styled per dialect; **the tool layer carries no light** [ART:512]. The player's **chest lamp is not a tool def** (Suit equipment; the beam origin is the per-facing chest point [PCI:155]), and it is not `mod_lamp`, which is a ship fixture — a 20 W LOAD node on the Graph's power loop. A def has no accuracy, damage, armour or range field, so "tools may repel or distract" [CON:290] is expressed entirely by the acting systems.

| `def_id` | `type_id` | `mass_g` [PH] | `charges_max` | `verbs[]` (Interaction and Assembly designed) | Acting system for the effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tool_cutter` | 2001 | 3500 | 0 | {"V8"} salvage, plus the seam-cut verb stored as the string `"begin_seam_cut"` until Interaction names its `verb_id` (no V-number in the Graph's list; Open Question 52) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Interaction (`begin_cut`), Assembly (`begin_seam_cut`) |
| `tool_welder` | 2002 | 4000 | 0 | {"V1", "V9"} patch, weld *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: string keys)* | Interaction (`begin_patch`), Assembly (`begin_weld`) |
| `tool_scanner` | 2003 | 1500 | 0 | {} | Sanity (falsification); the Graph's V-list names no scanner verb |
| ~~`tool_suppressant`~~ **DORMANT, not in the MVP roster** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 2004 (reserved) | 3000 | 3 [PH] | {} | Attention and Horror (repel or distract), a dormant `[CUT-FIRST]` interface (HE-39); finite charges, see below |

The verb assignment is provisional: the Graph's MVP verb set is V1 patch, V3 breaker, V4 bulkhead, V5 valve, V6 emergency vent, V8 salvage, V9 weld and V12 hand crank [LSG:228], and which of them need a tool in hand (V3-V6 and V12 are bare-hand verbs in this reading) is Interaction's to confirm. A tool is unique per player: `stack_max` 1, and Suit holds at most one of each (SE-12, designed); a loose duplicate of a held tool has no value and is an authoring warning. **`tool_suppressant` is DORMANT and outside the MVP roster** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): Option A (X6, D19, D61; Open Question 29). The tool leaves the MVP start kit (Suit SE-11 slot 4, SE-14, SE-15), this GDD's MVP roster and the HUD hand rail; Horror Entities' HE-39 repel stays a dormant `[CUT-FIRST]` interface; AC-HE-124 is BLOCKING; AC-109 passes. The registry constant `SUPPRESSANT_START_CHARGES` is retired (status deprecated). The definition row above is kept as a reserved, unshipped record (`def_id` `tool_suppressant`, `type_id` 2004, 3000 g, `charges_max` 3 [PH]) so that the interface can return after the MVP without reassigning an id: it is not in `assets/data/items/*.json`, it is not counted in `roster_golden.json`, it never enters a save, and the id ledger holds it as a `dormant` entry (IDB-4, F-8). Because nothing in the MVP can spend a charge, the "no refill source" question is moot until the interface returns (a post-MVP def would decide it). A tool is unique per player; the other stated rules of this paragraph are unchanged. *Alternatives considered:* a charge or fuel cell per tool use (adds a faucet and a crafting-like sink); tool durability repaired with parts (upkeep that scarcity already supplies through O2, power, Attention and parts); the suppressant as a consumable (would drop its hand-rail glyph, which the art bible lists with the hand tools [ART:512, 815]) — all rejected as scope creep for a first MVP and, for the first two, as weapon-tree drift.

**IDB-12 — ConsumableDef and the glowstick.** `cons_glowstick` (`type_id` 3001) is the only MVP consumable. Parameters here are the Item DB's by the user's decision and the art bible's explicit hand-off ("radius number provisional — game-designer" [ART:1084]); the output curve and the emitter record are Emitter State's.

| Field | Value | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `mass_g` | 200 | [PH] |
| `stack_max` = `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` | 4 (range 2-8) | [PH] registry candidate. Suit owns the per-player count and the **start count**; the start count is not an Item DB field |
| `use_verb` | `throw_glowstick` | Interaction's command [PCI:79, 611-633]; the Item DB lists it and defines none |
| `emits.shape_id` | `POOL_GLOWSTICK` | row 6 of Emitter State's shape table, adopted by that key (EM:354, shape row 6; art bible 8.3: omnidirectional) |
| `emits.base_pm` | 1000 | [PH]: the uniform `emits` shape needs a base brightness; this field **is** Emitter State's `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM` [EM:518] (Open Questions to Emitter State) |
| `emits.hue_class` | `CHEMLIGHT` | the colour token `#86B896` (hue about 139 degrees, S 27 percent, L* about 70) [ART:384]; **light colour only, never a pipe, fill, fixture or UI colour**; the def holds the class key and the hex stays in the art bible |
| `life_s` = `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` | 150 s (range 60-300) | [PH] registry candidate |
| `radius_tiles` = `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` | 2 tiles = 96 base px via `TILE_PX` 48 (range 1.5-3, strictly below `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3 [PCI:779]) | [PH] registry candidate; the number is confirmed by the lighting spike (art bible 8.12 step 2) |
| `wane_from_pm` = `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` | 400: output is steady until 40 percent of life remains | [PH] registry candidate |
| `wane_floor_pm` = `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` | 250: the radius fraction at the last tick, after which the emitter is removed | [PH] registry candidate |

Rules. (a) The **life is counted from the throw tick**, not from landing: the throw arc is `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 [PCI:611] and a stick that never lands still burns out. (b) A stowed glowstick is inert and does not age; the instance is a count only. (c) A landed glowstick is **spent**: it is not a world pickup, and a stick that lands where it cannot emit (out of bounds, a shaft) is spent without refund. (d) It counts as exposure like any other pool [ART:286] and toward the sixth-slot and lighting-budget rules (shadow-slot assignment is Lighting's, EM-26; Emitter State keeps the authoring lint and the `priority` field that make Lighting's cut reproducible, EM:354); it casts no shadow by default, never flickers and is never a cone [ART:396, 1084]; and because exposure scales with area, a pool at the floor radius has about one sixteenth of the full pool's area, which Emitter State honours by carrying `reach_px` and the shape in its read view rather than a radius alone (EM:354). (e) `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_TICKS` = `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` x `TICK_RATE` = 9000 ticks is a **derivation (F-7), not a registry constant**, and every duration in this GDD is labelled tick (60 Hz) or step (the Graph's 10 Hz, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6). (f) Designer floats (a 1.5-tile radius) are converted once at boot by `int(round(x * scale))` into pixels. **Alignment with Emitter State.** EM-15 [EM:196] adopts these five numbers by name (`GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S`, `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES`, `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM`) and owns the curve semantics: curve id `WANE_LINEAR_25` is steady while the remaining life is at or above `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` / 1000 of the total, then linear to `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` / 1000 of full reach at the last tick. It takes `life_total_ticks` (9000, F-7) from the def at spawn, and life is counted from the throw tick because Interaction passes the life remaining at landing, `9000 - THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 [PCI:611] (binding: Emitter State is designed and adopts the five numbers by name [EM:196], `POOL_GLOWSTICK` [EM:176] and `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM` = `emits.base_pm` [EM:518]). *Deployable lights of the flare family:* the art bible keeps a separate flickering Tungsten deployable family (flare, torch, decoy; radius 2.5 tiles / 120 px [ART:395, 1083]) that is distinct from the steady Chemlight glowstick, and the MVP roster has no def for it; any such def is a future `cons_` def, deferred past the MVP, and is recorded as an Open Question rather than added here.

**IDB-13 — PartDef: five repair-part families keyed by subject.** A part is the finite thing that makes a full repair possible. The repair part is chosen by what is being mended, not by a per-def part, which gives an edge subject (a pipe, a duct, a cable, none of which carries a `def_id` [LSG:72]) a rule and gives each part a read in the world through its loop hue.

| `def_id` | `type_id` | Mends | `mass_g` [PH] | `stack_max` [PH] | `carry_class` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `part_duct_coupling` | 4001 | O2 loop: DUCT edges, VALVEs on O2, DUCT_JUNCTION | 800 | 4 | STOWED |
| `part_cable_splice` | 4002 | POWER loop: CABLE edges, CABLE_JUNCTION, lamps, door motors | 600 | 4 | STOWED |
| `part_pipe_fitting` | 4003 | COOLANT and FUEL: PIPE edges and VALVEs on those loops (the junction node kinds have no MVP def) | 1200 | 4 | STOWED |
| `part_hull_plate` | 4004 | INTEGRITY: HULL_SEGMENT (and, in the vertical slice, STRUT) | 30000 | 1 | STOWED |
| `part_machine_kit` | 4005 | every machine node kind (IDB-19) | 2000 | 2 | STOWED |

A part is consumed on the first productive channel step of a full repair (IDB-20); a patch never needs one [REG:6033 "full repair needs an Item DB part"; LSG:222]. Parts have no condition and merge by `def_id` (IDB-15). **`part_hull_plate` is STOWED with `stack_max` 1**, not CARRIED, because PC-8 rejects every `interact` mode except `drop` with `BUSY` while `carry_ref` is set [PCI:131, 168], and a full repair is a channelled verb: a player holding the plate as their one carried module could never begin the repair, and a plate on the ground is a loose-world item outside the stowed stock that Suit's reservation (SE-41) and F-4 read; the plate leaves the stowed stock for the ground only by the explicit shed of States row 14, which is also how it moves between crewmates *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier sentence about a "dropped plate" named an action that did not exist; `drop` releases only the carried instance, IA-28)*. Whether a 30 kg stowed part should be *seen and heard* (a stowed 30000 g part is invisible, and a carry is twice as loud per tile as a walk [PCI:556-562]) is a question for Interaction and Suit, not a rule here (Open Questions). **The plate is not a free second carry slot: while any HEAVY item is stowed (any **unreserved** STOWED instance of `weight_class` HEAVY; the single implementation is Suit's `stowed_heavy`, SE-41, which excludes units RESERVED by a FULL job; F-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `run` is disabled** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): option (a) of D18, Open Question 32; the design review had found that the stowed 30 kg plate costs nothing in movement, so it undercut the single-`carry_ref` haul bottleneck, and a flat noise term is too weak because carrying is already quieter per tile than running, 48 against 64)*. The rule: while the holder has any STOWED instance whose def `weight_class` is HEAVY (F-1; the MVP case is `part_hull_plate`), the Controller refuses the run gait, so the holder moves at `V_WALK` 2 with empty hands or `V_CARRY` 1 with a module carried. There is no grams-based term: `V_CARRY` and `NOISE_PER_TICK` stay locked, F-1's class is the only input (the same class that draws the back-frame plate, IDB-21), and the rule keys on `weight_class`, never on `stowed_mass_g`, so a heap of light parts cannot trigger it (the roster-semantic lint of IDB-21, clause 2, already guarantees the plate is the only HEAVY STOWED def and that the heaviest ordinary stack stays below `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`). It fits "slow, hands full". What it costs and what it does not cost is derived in F-13 (the plate costs the lost run on every empty-handed leg and nothing extra while a module is carried; this is the price the ruling accepts). The ways to get run back are the explicit shed of States row 14 and the FULL repair that consumes the plate, because both remove the HEAVY stowed instance. The Controller's run rule changes accordingly (Player Controller PC-8 gait text; the Controller's own editor applies that side). The single-`carry_ref` bottleneck claim in the Fantasy, MDA and F-13 text now holds for a carried module and, through the lost run, for a stowed plate. (orchestrator ruling: the economy and systems proposals made the plate CARRIED by a derived rule; the binding ruling keeps parts STOWED, so the plate keeps `stack_max` 1 as the one mass-linked guard and the heavy-stack ban of IDB-5 covers any future heavy part.)

**IDB-14 — CurioDef and the junk reservation.** `curio_data_core` (`type_id` 5001): `node_kind` 0 (no Graph presence), `mass_g` 6000 [PH], `carry_class` CARRIED, `stack_max` 1, `salvageable` not applicable (it is a placed item, not a node). It has no use in the MVP: the Knowledge Codex consumes it in the vertical slice [IDX:102]. It is a thing with mass, not a quest item [CON:285-298]. Because it has no consumer, an authoring placement of a data core in the MVP is a lint warning (`PLACEMENT_NO_CONSUMER`, WARN; a Derelict Authoring content-lint id, registry: `content_lint_ids`): a carry trip with no payoff would break Pillar 1's honesty. The `junk_` class is reserved: junk defs are real items with true data and an off-band true mass (IDB-22); none ship in the MVP.

**IDB-15 — `ItemInstance`: the one value schema every holder embeds.** The Item DB defines the shape; it does not store, allocate or move instances, and **an instance id is not part of the schema** — the owning block allocates any id it needs from a counter inside itself [KRN:102, CR-8].

| Field | Type | Range | Canonical value when not applicable | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `def_id` | string | catalogue | n/a | The only identity key; never `type_id` |
| `count` | int | 1..`stack_max` | 1 | Stack size; modules, tools and curios are always 1 |
| `condition_bp` | int | 1..`cap_bp` for a module; 0 otherwise | 0 | A module's `hp`, from `SalvageRecord.hp` |
| `cap_bp` | int | for a module, `HP_FULL` 10000 (un-patched) or `1..HP_FULL-1` (patched: the `hp_cap` the Graph recorded, with **no tie to the current `PATCH_CAP_BP`**, so a retune of that constant invalidates no saved module); 0 otherwise | 0 | The module's `hp_cap`, carried through salvage and reinstall by M6's `SalvageRecord.hp_cap` and M5's `hp_cap` argument (IDB-18, settled by LS-18 M5/M6): a patched module stays capped until a FULL repair spends a part *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional on the Graph owner's ruling" removed; invariant changed from "at most `PATCH_CAP_BP`" to "below `HP_FULL`")* |
| `patched_at_step` | int | for a module whose `cap_bp` is below `HP_FULL`, the Graph's absolute `step_no` of its last PATCH, at least 1 and at most the restoring holder's current `step_no`; 0 otherwise | 0 | The decay clock's origin (`step_no − patched_at_step` against `PATCH_DECAY_STEPS` [LSG:769]); copied unchanged from the cut to the reinstall, so time in transit counts toward the hold (IDB-18, settled); at the cut itself F-5 writes 0 whenever `cap_bp` is `HP_FULL`, because the Graph's M7 FULL does not clear the field *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional with `cap_bp`" removed; the upper bound closes the clock-field hole in AC-82)* |
| `charge` | int | 0..`charges_max` | 0 | Tool charges; 0 for every MVP def (the only charge-bearing def, the suppressant, is dormant, X6); the field stays so a post-MVP charge tool needs no schema change *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

Invariants (lint at serialise, assert at restore): `1 <= condition_bp <= cap_bp` for a module, where `cap_bp` is `HP_FULL` (10000, un-patched) or any value in `1..HP_FULL-1` (patched; **no tie to the current `PATCH_CAP_BP`**); `patched_at_step` is 0 whenever `cap_bp` is `HP_FULL`, and at least 1 and at most the holder's current `step_no` whenever `cap_bp` is below `HP_FULL` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the old "at most `PATCH_CAP_BP`" form failed every saved patched module when that constant was retuned)*; and all four (`condition_bp`, `cap_bp`, `patched_at_step`, `charge`) are 0 for a non-module; a module instance at `condition_bp` 0 is refused at restore (a module at `hp` 0, whether DESTROYED or FAILED, yields a part, never a module, F-5 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*); `count <= stack_max` (a lint at serialise only: after a catalogue edit lowers a `stack_max`, a restore keeps the held count and the holder refuses further pickups, see Edge Cases); `stack_max` is 1 for every CARRIED def; no float, no `res://` path, no perceived field. Canonical zeros are mandatory because holder blocks feed `state_hash`: two encodings of one state would desync a replica. **Canonical positional encoding** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the review measured, on the installed 4.7.2, that `var_to_bytes` of an instance Dictionary serialises keys in insertion order, so two code paths building the same instance — restore, pickup, `salvage_yield` — can produce different raw `var_to_bytes` bytes for one state. *Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: the accurate reason.* The kernel's `canonical_bytes` sorts dictionary keys (D.12), so `state_hash` is order-independent and a Dictionary instance would not desync a co-op replica by itself; the positional array is adopted instead because it gives one byte form per instance, a fixed schema and the measured size budget below. The adoption ruling is unchanged. The Item DB therefore publishes one form, as the Graph does with `canonical_bytes()`)*.
