The only persisted, hashed or replicated form of an `ItemInstance` is the fixed-order array returned by `to_array()`: `[def_id: String, count: int, condition_bp: int, cap_bp: int, patched_at_step: int, charge: int]`, always six elements, every number an `int` (never a float, never a `StringName`), the encoder being `var_to_bytes(inst.to_array(), false)`. `from_array(a, graph_step_no)` is the only decoder (the step is the Graph's absolute 10 Hz `step_no`, the restoring holder's current step, never a 60 Hz tick; the `patched_at_step <= graph_step_no` invariant cannot be checked without it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: it rejects a wrong length, a wrong element type and any value that fails `validate_instance`, and returns a reason code (it never clamps). Holders (Suit, Interaction, and any future holder) call `to_array()` in their snapshots and `from_array()` in their restores and never hand-build an instance Dictionary; the array order, not a key order, defines the bytes. **Measured on Godot 4.7.2** (2026-10-27): the Dictionary form is 176 bytes per instance and 2,120 bytes for twelve; the same Dictionary with its keys inserted in another order compares equal (`==`) yet encodes to different bytes; the array form is 68 bytes for `mod_scrubber`, 72 for `part_machine_kit`, 80 for `mod_coolant_reservoir` (the longest roster id, 21 characters) and 96 at `ID_MAX_LEN` 40 (cost: 8-byte array header + 4 + 4 + the id padded to a multiple of 4 + five 8-byte integers); a holder block of an 8-integer header plus twelve instances of mixed roster ids is 956 bytes, twelve of the longest roster id 1,048, and twelve at `ID_MAX_LEN` 40 is 1,240 bytes. **Budget (re-derived from the measurement):** one instance at most 96 bytes (the `ID_MAX_LEN` bound); a mixed-roster holder block of twelve instances plus an 8-field header at most 1,024 bytes (956 measured, 68 bytes of headroom), which is the 25 percent share of `MAX_PAYLOAD` 4096; the two worst cases (1,048 and 1,240 bytes) exceed that share, so the share is **requested larger by Save/Load, 1,280 bytes (31 percent)**, and until Save/Load answers AC-28 asserts the 1,024 figure on the mixed-roster fixture and records the worst cases as ADVISORY (Open Question 43). Persisted instances hold `def_id` plus these fields and **never `mass_g`, `weight_class`, `carry_class` or any other def number**, so a balance edit to a mass or class re-derives on load with no migration. Two instances merge iff `def_id`, `condition_bp` and `charge` are equal (and, for a module, `cap_bp` and `patched_at_step`; moot, because modules never stack). `life_ticks` is **not** an instance field in the MVP (a stowed glowstick is always at full life; life exists only on the Emitter's record after the throw), so the field named `charges_or_life_ticks` in the working draft is one field with one meaning here, `charge`, and the second meaning is reserved. (orchestrator ruling: canonical zeros adopted from the systems proposal so one state has one byte form; the game-designer proposal's 10000 for non-modules is dropped.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier claim "an instance serialises to roughly 40-60 bytes, so a holder carrying twelve is far below `MAX_PAYLOAD` 4 KiB" is withdrawn; the measured figures are above. The encoder half of Open Question 20 is closed by this section; the hash function for `catalogue_hash` remains an ADR candidate.)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync: ADR-0007 § 3.4 fixes the hash input; the encoding and `H64` are ADR-0005's.)*

**IDB-16 — Who holds an instance (ownership clause).** The Item DB defines what a thing is; the holder owns who has it and how many.

| Holder | Owns | Instance field use | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Graph node | an installed module | `def_id` and `hp`; the instance dissolves into the node on install [LSG:184] | designed |
| Suit & Equipment (a PER_PLAYER block) | slots, counts, the hand-rail tool, the carried instance | the full `ItemInstance`, always via `to_array()` / `from_array()` (IDB-15) | designed; conformed: SE-12/SE-13/SE-38 persist `to_array()` arrays and decode in `post_restore`, failure reason `SUIT_RESTORE_INSTANCE_FORM`, AC-SE-96/97 (all six fields kept, AC-SE-79a) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| Interaction (`interaction.state` `loose[]`, IA-28) | loose items, and the instance-id counter that `carry_ref` names | the full six-field `ItemInstance` via `to_array()` / `from_array()` | designed; conformed: IA-28 `loose[]` = `{item_id, section_id, pos_px, inst}`, `place_loose(inst, section_id, pos_px)`, IA-36 `INTERACTION_RESTORE_LOOSE_INSTANCE`, AC-IA-152/153 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| Player Controller | `carry_ref`, an id and not the instance [PCI:131] | none | designed |
| Save/Load (the container, not a holder) | the bytes of every holder block | none of its own; SL-2 names the positional `to_array()` form as the canonical encoding of an `ItemInstance` inside any block (registry `item_instance_to_array`) | conformed: SL-2 names the `to_array()` form canonical, AC-SL-149/150 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |

**Binding clauses on the holders: record of conformance** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6: the schema owner's form stands and the holders conform. This replaces the 2026-10-27 asks, which are now met)*. (1) **Interaction** conformed: `loose[]` entries are `{item_id, section_id, pos_px, inst}` where `inst` is the `to_array()` form and `place_loose(inst, section_id, pos_px)` takes the full six-field instance (IA-28), a restore that cannot decode `inst` fails with `INTERACTION_RESTORE_LOOSE_INSTANCE` (IA-36), so a drop and pickup returns exactly the instance that was dropped; tests AC-IA-152 and AC-IA-153. This is the launder fix (IDB-18): a free, repeatable drop and pickup can no longer strip a patch cap and its decay clock without spending a part. (2) **Suit** conformed: SE-12, SE-13 and SE-38 persist `to_array()` arrays and decode them in `post_restore`, failing with `SUIT_RESTORE_INSTANCE_FORM`; tests AC-SE-96 and AC-SE-97. (3) **Save/Load** conformed: SL-2 names the `to_array()` form canonical; tests AC-SL-149 and AC-SL-150. The array order, not a key order, defines the bytes. Suit already kept all six fields (AC-SE-79a) and had only the byte-form to conform; Interaction had lost fields and now keeps them. AC-67 and AC-99 now run against the real holders (`suit.state`, `interaction.state`).

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional" and "owner open" removed.)* Interaction owns the loose-world list (IA-28) and the counter that `carry_ref` names; Suit mints no ids (SE-13, Open Question 20 answered). The Item DB takes no position beyond "the owning block allocates" (CR-8).

**IDB-17 — Salvage yield is deterministic in the MVP.** `salvage_yield(rec)` (F-5) maps the five-field `SalvageRecord{def_id, hp, state, hp_cap, patched_at_step}` that an applied M6 returns (LS-18 M6; IDB-18) to the instance that appears *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the record was three fields before the Graph's amendment)*. A module node in OK, DEGRADED or FAILED **with `hp` at least 1** yields exactly **one instance of its own def** with `condition_bp = rec.hp`, `cap_bp = rec.hp_cap` and `patched_at_step` = `rec.patched_at_step`, normalised to 0 when `rec.hp_cap` is `HP_FULL` (F-5 `patched_out`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*; a DESTROYED node, **or a FAILED node at `hp` 0** (legal while the Graph's warning gate is closed), yields `scrap_count` (pinned to 1) of its `repair_part_id` and **no module** (a lamp or a door motor therefore breaks down to the scarce `part_cable_splice`; whether scrap should be value-aware is Open Question 39 and the destruction path is counted in F-11 supply); SALVAGED and ABSENT are rejected by the Graph before this runs. There is no randomness. The Graph's phrase "the loot roll is the Item DB's" [LSG:185, 850] is satisfied by this function. The yield is computed from the return value of the *applied* M6, never from a pre-read of the node, so two players cutting one node in the same tick cannot duplicate it: the second M6 sees SALVAGED and is rejected under validate-then-apply [KRN:CR-9]. **Salvage-once**: the node becomes SALVAGED [LSG:244], so the faucet is bounded by the authored node count (plus at most one part per DESTROYED node). A keyed draw is *reserved by name only* for Procedural Derelict Generation (vertical slice): `rng_derive(world_seed, "item.yield", node_id)`, never a stored counter — the Item DB has no block, so a registry-style counter "stored in the owning block" [REG:2742] cannot exist and the Graph's pattern of an owner-defined key [LSG:123] applies. Node ids are never reused [LSG:68] and a node is salvaged once [LSG:244], so node identity alone is a sufficient key and the draw is a pure function of the node: there is no step number in the key and so no re-roll by choosing when to cut. It is not a loot table and has no MVP effect. *Alternatives considered:* a per-def seeded bonus roll (reserved, not used) and full container loot tables (rejected: both MVP derelicts are handmade with authored salvage slots, and the faucet must be legible — Pillar 5).

**IDB-18 — What survives salvage then reinstall (settled rule).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: reconciled with the Life-Support Graph's 2026-10-27 amendment. The Graph's M6 now returns `SalvageRecord{def_id, hp, state, hp_cap, patched_at_step}` and M5 now takes `hp_start, hp_cap = HP_FULL, patched_at_step = 0`, keeping the absolute decay clock, with a store installing empty (LS-18 M5, M6; LS-23). The rule below, formerly "provisional" and routed as Open Question 10, is the Item DB's settled statement of what those two mutators now guarantee, and no AC waits on a ruling.)* The Graph's signatures are locked and are not restated or edited here; the Item DB states: (i) **stored quantities** — BATTERY `stock`, O2_TANK `stock`, RESERVOIR `stock_ml` and FUEL_TANK `stock_ml` — are **discarded into the section's `discarded_*` counters on salvage**, conserved the way `discard_section` (M4 [LSG:183]) conserves [LSG:799], and a reinstalled store starts at stock 0 (the Graph's own conservation note lists M5 among the only mutators that create a Graph quantity [LSG:799]; stock 0 creates none, so the invariant stays trivial); (ii) **patch state persists through salvage.** A module cut out and put back is the same body with the same history: a reinstalled module gets `hp = SalvageRecord.hp`; a module whose `hp_cap` was `PATCH_CAP_BP` reinstalls **capped** at it and **carries its decay clock** (`patched_at_step` unchanged); an un-patched module reinstalls at `hp_cap = HP_FULL` with no clock (the fresh-node default [LSG:68]). Only a FULL repair, which spends a part (IDB-20 (6)), lifts a cap; no cut and reinstall does. **The launder is closed by rule, not by a tripwire, and only while every holder keeps all six instance fields** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: Interaction's IA-28 `loose[]` then stored a single `condition` number, so a drop lost `cap_bp` and `patched_at_step` and a pickup returned a clean module; the rule is closed on the Item DB and Graph side (M5/M6) and *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* closed at the Interaction holder by IA-28, and the regression proof is AC-117)*: a free, repeatable action must not stand in for the finite part (the Player Fantasy design test), and the bolted patch plate keeps its meaning after the module has been cut out and put back (Pillar 1, physical history; Pillar 5). **Mechanism (settled; was the routed CONFLICT).** The record carries `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step` (M6) and the reinstall restores them through M5's `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step` arguments, so Assembly's `install_module` passes the instance's `cap_bp` and `patched_at_step` straight through with `hp_start := condition_bp`; the Item DB extends no Graph mutator and edits no Graph value. The Graph's Open Question was answered by the amendment itself (LS-18 M5/M6), not by a separate ruling. **The clock is absolute and time in transit counts:** `patched_at_step` is the Graph's `step_no`, copied unchanged, and the Graph's wear terms apply only to subjects in the graph [LSG:505, 769], so transit never costs `hp`; but a module carried or shelved longer than `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 s reinstalls already past its hold and starts to decay at the next wear period. A shelf is not a pause button. `hp` is the damage and carries unchanged. The cut still costs real time, noise and air, so a salvage is never a free action: the SEVERED stubs leak at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` until `cap_port` [LSG:772], the island re-solves with the module gone [LSG:773], the carry runs at `V_CARRY` 1 and the module stays DEGRADED at `eff` 750 until a part is spent. The control is the rule itself plus the split persistence tests (AC-32a to AC-32f), and any build in which a reinstall resets a cap or a clock reopens this rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the F-15 break-even and its `T_CYCLE_MIN` tripwire are withdrawn as blocking items; they compared one salvage cycle against the whole patch chore over a horizon, which are not comparable, and the persistence tests are the real control)*. **Stock is in situ, not hauled:** O2 is regenerated by the scrubber, charge by the reactor at `CH_CAP`, and coolant circulates rather than being consumed; **fuel is the exception** — it is finite and non-renewable ("nothing the Graph does refills the fuel tank or the O2 tank" [LSG:793]) — so fuel reaches the home-ship by welding a derelict section whose tank joins the loop, not by hauling a tank, and the MVP roster has no fuel-canister def. The patch-state rule (ii) and the stored-quantity rule (i) are now guaranteed by the Graph's own M5 and M6 text (Open Question 10 closed). A reinstalled empty store must read as empty in the world (a dark store, a silent gauge on the sane channel), or it reads as the game cheating — a note for the presentation systems. **A saved `cap_bp` is never re-based:** a patched module whose `cap_bp` was recorded under an older `PATCH_CAP_BP` reinstalls at the recorded value even if that constant was retuned since (IDB-15, "Restore against a retuned catalogue"), and the next PATCH takes `min(hp_cap, PATCH_CAP_BP)`.

**Runtime fields on reinstall: reset or carry** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the review asked what reinstall does to the other node fields, and warned that resetting them is itself a free "fix" of the same shape as the launder).* M5's signature passes only `hp_start`, `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step`, so every other field of the new node takes the Graph's fresh-node default. The rule that decides whether that is acceptable: **physical history persists, environment and switch state does not.** A field persists if it is damage or wear (`hp`, `hp_cap`, `patched_at_step`); a field resets if it is derived from `hp` and so recomputed (it cannot be a free fix because the cause is carried), a switch or setpoint the player chose (not history), or a transient whose cause lives in the old room.

| Runtime field (LS-3) | On reinstall | Why this is not a free fix |
|---|---|---|
| `hp`, `hp_cap`, `patched_at_step` | **carried** (M5 arguments) | the damage and the patch history are the module's body |
| `state`, `forced_mode`, `forced_mode_next`, `fail_since_step`, `warn_since_step` | reset, then **re-derived from `hp`** by the next Graph run | a module below `HP_FAIL` is FAILED again at the next run; the cause (`hp`) is carried, so no failure is cleared that `hp` did not already clear. A forced mode caused by a hazard in the old room (overheat, short) re-fires only if the new room produces it |
| REACTOR `scram`, `starve_steps`, `out_w`, `fuel_rem` | reset (cold, unfuelled, ramping from 0) | a reactor cut out is cold; a SCRAM is a latched trip whose cause (overheat, no fuel) is the old room's and re-trips in a room that causes it. This is a **switch-state reset, not a repair**: it does not raise `hp` |
| CABLE_JUNCTION `breaker_closed` | the Graph's fresh default | a breaker position is a switch; the damage that tripped it is `hp`, which is carried |
| PUMP `throttle_pm`, VENT `setpoint_pp` | fresh default | player settings, not history |
| LOAD `energised_at_step` | reset | the inrush window restarts at install; the first re-energise carries the inrush (V3's cost) |
| RESERVOIR `t_cool`, `tc_rem` | reset to the loop's ambient | thermal state is equilibrated by the new room |
| Store stock (BATTERY, O2_TANK, RESERVOIR, FUEL_TANK) | **discarded at the cut, installs empty** (rule (i)) | see the refill table below |

The confirmation of these fresh defaults is the Graph owner's (they are M5's, not the Item DB's); the table is the Item DB's statement of what it relies on, and Open Question 37 lists the one place a carry would be more honest (a tripped breaker).

**Stores after a reinstall: the refill path and the time to fill** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: reinstalled stores are near-worthless unless something refills them; each store def states how, or says plainly that nothing does)*:

| Store def | Refill path in the MVP | Time to fill from empty | Visible cause of the lost stock at the cut |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mod_battery_cell` | the reactor charges it at `CH_CAP` 300 W when the island has surplus | `BATT_CELL` 3,600,000 dJ at 300 dJ/step = 12,000 steps = 20 min at best | **none diegetic yet** (a battery has no fluid stub); Open Question 36 asks for a closed-list cause (a BURN trace or scorch at the cut) or that the stock be kept |
| `mod_o2_tank` | **none** ("nothing the Graph does refills the fuel tank or the O2 tank" [LSG:793]) | never in the MVP | the severed 103 stub hisses at 600 mGU/step until capped (the cut itself; a visible, audible cause) |
| `mod_coolant_reservoir` | none by design (coolant circulates, it is not consumed; only leaks lose it) | never in the MVP | the severed 303 stub leaks 20 mL/step until capped |
| `mod_fuel_tank` | none; fuel arrives only by welding a derelict section whose tank joins the loop | never by hauling | the severed 403 stub leaks 2 mL/step until capped |

A reinstalled O2 tank, reservoir or fuel tank is therefore **a part of the structure, not a supply**: the MVP gives no way to fill it, which is stated here so no author places one expecting a refill (Open Question 37).

**IDB-19 — Repair parts: `repair_part_for` and `parts_per_full`.** The pure lookup `repair_part_for(subject_kind, kind_id, loop) -> (part_def_id, count)` (F-3) returns a part and a count, or `NONE`. It exists because the Graph says a full repair "requires the Item DB part named by the subject's `def_id`" [LSG:222] while edges carry no `def_id` [LSG:72], and two of the eight MVP faults — the coolant leak on a PIPE and the oxygen pinhole on a DUCT [LSG:228] — are edges. The lookup is **total over the five `subject_kind` values** of the Graph's M7 enum, NODE 1 / EDGE 2 / COMPARTMENT 3 / SEAM 4 / OPENING 5 [LSG:150], so it answers for whatever M7 is handed. Resolution order: (1) a NODE with a non-empty `def_id` uses that def's `repair_part_id` and `parts_per_full`; (2) a NODE with an empty `def_id` (HULL_SEGMENT) uses the node-kind row; (3) an EDGE uses `(edge_kind, loop)`, including the SEAM loop sub-edge (kind 6) mapped to that loop's line part; (4) COMPARTMENT (3), SEAM (4) and OPENING (5) return `NONE`, as do the node kinds 1, 2 and the vertical-slice kinds with no part. `NONE` is defined in IDB-20. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the formerly routed wording conflict is closed; LS-23 and M7 now read "named by `repair_part_for(subject)`".)*

| Subject | Key | Part | Count |
|---|---|---|---|
| NODE with a def | `def.repair_part_id` | per def (below) | `def.parts_per_full` |
| NODE 50 HULL_SEGMENT (no def) | node kind | `part_hull_plate` | 1 |
| EDGE 1 DUCT | edge kind | `part_duct_coupling` | 1 |
| EDGE 2 CABLE | edge kind | `part_cable_splice` | 1 |
| EDGE 3 PIPE on loop 3 or 4 | edge kind + loop | `part_pipe_fitting` | 1 |
| EDGE 5 VALVE | edge kind + loop | loop 1 `part_duct_coupling`; loop 3 or 4 `part_pipe_fitting` | 1 |
| EDGE 6 SEAM (a loop edge crossing sections) | edge kind + loop | the part of that loop's line (as DUCT, CABLE or PIPE above) | 1 |
| EDGE 4 STRUT (vertical slice) | edge kind | `part_hull_plate` | 1 |
| SEAM record (`SeamRecord`; `q`, `leak_bp`) | subject kind | `NONE` in the MVP | 0 |
| EDGE 7 OPENING; NODE 1, 2; vertical-slice node kinds 34, 42, 51 | — | `NONE` | 0 |

*Per-def parts.* Machine defs — `mod_scrubber`, `mod_reactor_cell`, `mod_battery_cell`, `mod_coolant_pump`, `mod_radiator`, `mod_coolant_reservoir`, `mod_o2_tank`, `mod_fuel_tank`, `mod_vent`, `mod_heater` — name `part_machine_kit`; `mod_breaker_junction`, `mod_lamp` and `mod_door_motor` name `part_cable_splice` (electrical fixtures, not machines; this refines the ruling's "every machine node kind"). `parts_per_full` is 1 except `mod_reactor_cell`, which is 2 [PH] (the heart of the ship costs twice). `scrap_count` is 1 on every module def (IDB-17). A DESTROYED subject takes no part because it is never repairable (`NOT_REPAIRABLE` [LSG:771]). `PATCH_CAP_BP` stays 6000 [REG:6021-6037]; its build rule is the Graph's, quoted: `PATCH_CAP_BP` "must be >= `LEAK_HP` + one wear period and < `HP_OK`" [LSG:903], where one wear period is `PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100` 100 bp [LSG:769] — never restated here as a number. (orchestrator ruling: on the SEAM rows the specialists split three ways — the game-designer maps a seam to the hull plate, the systems-designer resolves seam sub-edges per loop with the `SeamRecord` unmapped, the economy-designer leaves the record to Assembly's weld; this rule takes the systems form; the Graph has since ruled that a FULL on `subject_kind` SEAM 4 is not offered at MVP (LS-18 M7; `repair_part_for` NONE), so the held `NONE` is confirmed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. On tanks and reservoirs the economy-designer's plumbing-part mapping is dropped in favour of the machine kit, keeping the kit the scarce part and the family count at five.)

**IDB-20 — Part consumption economics (Interaction designed: IA-18 discharges these statements).** The Item DB owns `parts_per_full`, so it states the economic rule; Interaction owns the command, the channel timing and the state that implements it. (1) **One part-count per job.** A full repair job is one accepted `begin_patch(FULL)`; its cost is `parts_per_full` of `repair_part_for(subject)`, consumed at the **first productive channel step** — the first step in which `hp_cap` or `hp` rises (registry: `full_job_charge`, Interaction's: the part is RESERVED at the Applied FULL begin tick, so the actor's visible stock falls then, and is released, with no timer, if the channel ends early with no credited block or on an OUTCOME; `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` is the hold length only; it is PAID, i.e. spent for good, at the first productive step, and at any cancel, abort or restore once a block is credited) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*. A full repair on a subject with `hp_cap = HP_FULL` and `hp = hp_cap` consumes nothing and resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` (Interaction IA-18 statement 7, an Applied-and-did-nothing outcome). (2) **A full job issues only `kind = FULL` steps**: a PATCH step inside a paid job would re-cap `hp_cap` to `PATCH_CAP_BP` and waste the part [LSG:186]. (3) **No refund on interruption**: the part is spent, and the `hp` already applied stays (steps are real). (4) **Recommendation to Interaction (not an Item DB rule or knob)**: a resume allowance for a re-issued `begin_patch(FULL)` on the same subject shortly after an interruption (adopted: Interaction's `FULL_JOB_GRACE_S` 60 s [PLACEHOLDER], IA-18 statement 6, for the same seat on the same subject; other seats are charged), so a stagger or a pod hop does not double-charge a part. (5) **A PATCH never consumes a part** [REG:6033]; **no patch-kit def exists** in the roster, and a PATCH consumes no item at all [REG:6033; LSG:222] — the Graph has removed the former "patch kits are the Item DB's finite consumable" wording, so LS-23, M7 and the registry now agree; a PATCH on a subject whose `hp` is at or above `PATCH_CAP_BP` is refused by the Graph as `ABOVE_PATCH_CAP` and `hp` is **never clamped down** (LS-18 M7), so Interaction offers only FULL there *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Open Questions 9 and 14 (patch kits) closed on those rule ids)*. (6) **Wear is a full repair**: a duty-worn machine at 6900 `hp` with `hp_cap` 10000 can only be raised above `PATCH_CAP_BP` by a full repair, which is the engine of Pillar 5 — the part is what wear costs. (7) In co-op, each player's part is consumed only if that player's first step is applied; a second seat beginning on a subject already under a job is `BUSY` at `validate` and makes no reservation (Interaction IA-34). (8) A missing part produces no `BLOCKED` and no reject: FULL is simply not offered without `parts_per_full` of the part (Interaction IA-18 statement 1); the Item DB rejects nothing. (9) **`NONE` means Interaction offers no FULL job on that subject** (PATCH only); a FULL repair never costs zero parts, so a subject whose `repair_part_for` is `NONE` cannot have its `hp_cap` restored for free. The SEAM record (subject_kind 4) is held at `NONE`, which the Graph confirmed: a FULL on `subject_kind` SEAM 4 is not offered at MVP (LS-18 M7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; Open Question 12 closed)*. The weld that makes a seam is V9 (MVP); V11 SEAL_SEAM, which sets a seam's `leak_bp` to 0, is vertical slice A [LSG:228, 648].

**IDB-21 — Weight: true integer grams, a class, and a body cue — never speed or noise.** `mass_g` is an integer (the Graph's pattern is scaled integers: mGU, mC, dkPa, µmL [LSG:64]). `weight_class` (F-1) is LIGHT, MEDIUM or HEAVY by two thresholds `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` 5000 and `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` 25000 [PH]. The Item DB supplies the true number; **Suit turns carried (and stowed) mass into a consequence-channel body cue** — breath, strain; never a number on screen — and the class is the resolution that cue must at least have. Mass does **not** change `V_CARRY` (1 base px/tick, one constant [REG:5338-5356]) or the carry `NOISE_PER_TICK` (1 [REG:5442-5457]); the Controller already makes the carry visibly slow ("a module at chest height halves the walk" [PCI:98]), and the haul cost of a module is its distance and the single `carry_ref`, not its mass (F-13 is the worked comparison: a 90 kg reactor cell and a 6 kg data core cost the same per tile); a stowed HEAVY item adds one class-keyed rule, the run disable of IDB-13, never a grams term *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **There is no pack capacity and no weight readout in the MVP**: weight matters for the single carried module, the hull plate and the field check. **BINDING on Suit & Equipment (a requirement, not a courtesy): stowed mass counts, not only the carried module.** When `stowed_mass_g` (F-2: the sum of `instance_mass_g` over the holder's STOWED instances) is at or above `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`, the Suit must produce a closed-list consequence-channel body cue (breath, visor fog, rime or shiver, from Suit's SE-27 set, no vignette; the Suit chooses which, it may not choose none and may not choose a module-body cue). The MVP case is the 30 kg `part_hull_plate`, the heaviest object a player can hold: a design in which it has no world footprint, or costs the hauler nothing the body can report, makes the exposure cost of hauling it avoidable and weakens "the physical world is ground truth" (Pillars 1 and 3). **It is also seen, derived and not authored:** a STOWED def at `weight_class` HEAVY draws a back-frame sprite (a plate on the back rig) that never covers the chest point, the per-facing beam origin [ART:512]; there is no new def field, the rule is the class (and `stack_max` 1, above). The sprite keys on a stowed def's own `weight_class` and the body cue on the summed `stowed_mass_g`; they are two readings of one rule and in the MVP roster only the hull plate crosses `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` either way. The body cue is the truthful report and is the sub-class d field check. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier sentence "the sprite is module-body channel and may lie" is out of date. Suit's conflict C1 was closed on 2026-10-27: the HEAVY back-frame is on the Graph's closed consequence-channel list (SE-27; registry `truth_channel_union`), so for a stowed HEAVY item the plate on the back is a non-corruptible visual as well as a breath cue. The inspect card and any other module-body surface may still lie. The MEDIUM class has no such visual; see the accessibility clause below.)* Whether a stowed haul adds carry-rate noise is Interaction's open choice (OQ-4); any such term is flat per tile and keyed to the class, never a function of grams, so the locked `NOISE_PER_TICK` carry value and the rule above stay true. Channel split: the body cue is consequence-channel and never corruptible; the sprite and the inspect card are module-body and may lie [LSG:44]. Boundary hazard: a def sitting exactly on a threshold flips class on a 1 g edit, so the lint `WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM` (default 100 permille [PH]) keeps every def at least that far from both thresholds (the forbidden bands are 4500-5500 g and 22500-27500 g at the defaults) and is **blocking in CI**; a boundary value is a defect, not a class-population argument. Every roster mass clears both bands (`mod_battery_cell` is 22000 g, MEDIUM; the nearest others are `tool_welder` 4000 g and `mod_vent` 6000 g).

*Weight-system clauses added by the design review* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. (1) **Feasible windows (published, and computed by the lint).** Against the shipped masses and the blocking margin lint, the thresholds are not free across their old knob ranges. `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` must keep `part_hull_plate` (30000 g, and `mod_coolant_pump` 30000 g) HEAVY with margin, so `30000 - H >= H x margin / 1000`, and `mod_battery_cell` (22000 g) MEDIUM with margin, so `H - 22000 >= H x margin / 1000`: at the default margin 100 the window is **24,445 to 27,272 g** (at the old range top of 40000 the plate silently stops being HEAVY). `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` must keep `tool_welder` (4000 g) LIGHT and `mod_vent` and `curio_data_core` (6000 g) MEDIUM with margin: **4,445 to 5,454 g**. `WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM` is feasible only up to **150** (the window for `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` closes between 150 and 160, and is 25,883 to 26,086 g at 150); the old range top of 200 is infeasible with this roster. The lint computes these windows from the roster and prints them; a knob outside its window fails the build, and a roster mass edit shifts them. (2) **Roster-semantic lint (blocking).** The plate is HEAVY, and no other STOWED def is HEAVY; the heaviest full stack of ordinary stowed parts at the shipped `stack_max` values (4 duct couplings, 4 cable splices, 4 pipe fittings, 2 machine kits, 4 glowsticks = 15,200 g) stays below `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`, so no legal `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` value lets an ordinary stack reach HEAVY at those `stack_max` values; `stack_max` is itself a knob (1-8), so a `stack_max` edit re-runs the lint. (3) **Suit's masking and exclusion, acknowledged.** Suit's SE-28 reads `cue_load = max(carried_class, stowed_class)`, so a stowed plate masks a carried module's class and the Vertical Slice lift-and-weigh check is blind to a carried item while the plate is on the back (Suit OQ-8); SE-41 excludes parts reserved by a FULL job from `stowed_mass_g` (F-2). Both are Suit's rules and are cited, not restated. States row 14 is what restores the check (shed the plate first). (4) **Hysteresis (requested of Suit).** The stowed-sum class has no margin: a 200 g glowstick can flip `stowed_class` at the edge, so the cue needs enter/exit hysteresis (enter at `WEIGHT_*_G`, exit below it by `WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM`); the cue is Suit's, so this is a binding ask, Open Question 35. (5) **Calibration (requested of Boarding and Suit).** Three classes carry little information without a reference: the first sortie must give the player a calibrating carry of a MEDIUM and a HEAVY item before any lure can appear (Boarding's reference carry `htr_a` is MEDIUM; the plate is HEAVY), Open Question 35. (6) **Accessibility of the sole mass channel.** The weight check is audio-first (breath), which is a gap for a deaf or hard-of-hearing player (Boarding OQ-BS-21). For HEAVY the back-frame already supplies a paired visual on the closed consequence list; MEDIUM has none. This GDD requires that any further visual cadence for the load (a visor fog or rime variant) be placed on the closed list by Suit and the Perception Filter, because a visual that is not on the list can be corrupted and so becomes lie-able (Pillar 1); otherwise `design/accessibility-requirements.md` must record an explicit exception. The accessibility file does not exist yet; this GDD does not block another system's story on it (Open Question 40), but the Vertical Slice phantom-salvage story depends on the answer. *Alternatives considered:* weight shown only on the inspect card (corruptible, fails the field check); a second instrument such as a pod scale (reserved for the vertical slice); weight scaling noise (conflicts with the locked carry noise for heavy modules) — rejected or deferred.

**IDB-22 — Phantom-salvage support (hallucination sub-class d): a true mass on every def, and off-band junk.** The Item DB supplies exactly two things to sub-class d: a **true `mass_g` on every def**, and the existence of `junk_` defs. A junk def's `mass_g` differs from the mass of the lure it will be dressed as by at least `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` 300 permille [PH] (F-10), **and** the two fall in different `weight_class` values — because the Suit's cue resolves classes, not grams, a 30 percent gap inside one class gives the same cue and the check could not fail the lie (Suit's cue ladder is three steps, LIGHT, MEDIUM, HEAVY, SE-28, so the class clause stands; if a later Suit ladder is finer the class clause is replaced by "differs by at least one cue step"). **SE-28 precondition** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the class check can fail a lie only while the carried item's class is what the cue reads; because Suit's `cue_load = max(carried_class, stowed_class)`, a seat whose stowed class is HEAVY (the plate on the back) is masked, and a junk-versus-lure comparison is valid only for a seat with no HEAVY stowed item (Sanity SN-21 already gives such a seat no lure); the player's way to remove the mask is the shed of States row 14. The lure sprite is the Perception Filter's to draw; the lure-band data and the lie belong to Sanity (SN-21); a def never carries a believed, displayed or fake value. The junk-tell lint (F-10) reads the Sanity-owned band file (registry: `lure_bands.json`; path `assets/data/sanity/lure_bands.json`, SN-21; `{band_id, mid_g, class}`, lint-only, never loaded at runtime or persisted). **No lure data lives under `assets/data/items/`**: the table of what a junk def is dressed as is the lie's data, so neither a junk def nor any file in the Item DB folder says what a thing will look like. The MVP uses the field for `weight_class` and the body cue only; junk defs ship in the vertical slice (reserved ids 9000-9999), and a junk def that fails F-10 is not releasable.

**IDB-23 — Dialect is a skin, not a stat.** Defs carry `family` and `dialect`. A variant row may override stats, but **no MVP def does** (lint: every MVP def has `dialect: foundry` and no override), so no dominant dialect can emerge. `lattice` and `civil` are reserved enum values. The MVP derelicts are Foundry [IDX:31] and the home-ship's origin is Foundry [ART:298-330, 3.3]. Port *shape* (round, octagonal, rectangular) is sprite grammar and never a port-type id. A grafted section keeps its donor dialect, so a foreign module reads foreign and plays identically — the "foreign" read is a sprite concern. Two defs sharing a `family` with different stats are allowed only after the MVP. **Dialect and `grade` stay behaviourless:** a dialect never raises a stat, and `grade` is a reserved integer that is 0 everywhere in the MVP; any future behaviour for `grade`, or any stat that differs by dialect or ring, needs a creative-director review before it enters a def (IDB-29 rule 5).

**IDB-24 — "Dead" modules are not variant defs.** No def field encodes "powered-off", and no `_dead`, `_cold` or `_dormant` def exists. A dried-trail derelict fault is the **same `def_id`** authored at `hp` at or below `HP_FAIL` on an unpowered branch — "a `def_id` whose module is powered-off" [LSG:226] — emitting descriptors at `intensity_pm` 0 with a large `since_steps`. Failure & Trail reads the descriptor, not the def (its own Interactions row, FT:305), so the convention is Derelict Authoring's to follow when it authors a dead fault. Pillar 1: the dead module is the live module without power; the world, not the catalogue, says so.

**IDB-25 — Things that have `hp` but no def.** HULL_SEGMENT (empty `def_id` [LSG:68]), edges (no `def_id` [LSG:72]), VALVE (an edge), OPENING and SEAM have no def; a part mends them (IDB-19). This is why a thing with `hp` can lack a `def_id`: the Graph gives a `def_id` to every node that is a *module*, and these are structure. The junction node kinds (DUCT_JUNCTION 23, PIPE_JUNCTION 33, FUEL_JUNCTION 41) are not in the MVP slice list [LSG:228] and get no def until a template uses one. **Door motors (resolved).** LS-3 gives a `def_id` to every node except COMPARTMENT, EXTERIOR and HULL_SEGMENT [LSG:68], and the home-ship has door-motor LOAD nodes among its ~8 LOADs [LSG:228] (tier 2 [LSG:425]), so a door motor has a def: `mod_door_motor` (`type_id` 1013; `node_kind` 13, LOAD subtype `3 DOOR_MOTOR`; 120 W, `power_tier` 2, `waste_permille` 0, all [PH] except the tier; IDB-6, IDB-8, IDB-31). Whether power is a precondition of opening a door stays Interaction's [LSG:192]. The one-sentence reason a thing with `hp` can lack a `def_id` stands: structure (hull segments, edges, openings, seams) is not a module, and the Graph gives a `def_id` only to modules.

**IDB-26 — Data lives in JSON under `assets/data/items/`.** One file per class: `modules.json`, `tools.json`, `consumables.json`, `parts.json`, `curios.json` (`junk.json` in the vertical slice), plus `meta.json` (`catalogue_version`, `retired_ids`) and `ids.lock.json` (IDB-4). There is no lure data in this folder (IDB-22). Integers only; any designer float (a 1.5-tile glowstick radius) is converted once at boot by `int(round(x * scale))` into the immutable table, as `GraphTuning` does [LSG:64, LS-1 (b)]; no `res://` path on any field that can reach a save; sprite and glyph keys follow the art bible's 8.5 naming grammar `<cat>[_<dialect>]_<subject>[_<variant tokens>]_<map>.png` [ART:1117-1148]. This follows the controller's `assets/data/*` convention [PCI:238]. **ADR candidates closed by ADR-0007** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync)*: the export include filter is `assets/data/*.json` in the release preset, verified by spike CONTENT-EXP-1 and the exported-build smoke check (ADR-0007 § 1.6); the `catalogue_hash` input is fixed in IDB-27, and its byte encoding and `H64` belong to ADR-0005. The class files are listed in `assets/data/items/manifest.json`, and the schema asserts that the manifest names exactly the file set above (ADR-0007 § 1.2). **Runtime representation: requirements the ADR must meet** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: AC-69 (no allocation) and AC-85 (immutability) depend on the representation, and the review found it unstated; the ADR may choose the details but may not loosen these)*. (i) A def is a typed `RefCounted` class `ItemDef` whose fields are written once by the loader and guarded afterwards; it is not a `Resource` (SL-2 forbids Resource paths, and a `Resource` is editor-mutable shared state) and not a bare Dictionary (`make_read_only` is shallow, so a nested `ports[]` or `verbs[]` would stay mutable; array fields are exposed as read-only copies built once). (ii) An instance is a value type `ItemInstance` (six fields) built only by its constructor or `from_array` and serialised only by `to_array` (IDB-15); there is no Dictionary form. **Immutable** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: GDScript `RefCounted` has reference, not value, semantics, so two holders sharing one object would alias it)*: an `ItemInstance` has no setter after construction; `with_count(n)` and `merged(other)` return **new** objects, and a holder stores the `to_array()` form (never the object) in its persisted state and in `loose[]`, rebuilding an instance with `from_array` when it needs one. `ItemDef` fields are get-only properties written once by the loader. (iii) `ItemCatalog.new(data, graph_tables)` takes both inputs by injection and holds **no static, singleton or autoload state**, so a catalogue test can build many catalogues in one process. (iv) The loader returns an `Array[BootError]` (a plain data class `{code, table, key, field, line, detail}` whose `code` is a domain-2 fault code from `ContentFaults`; `table` holds the class or table, `key` the id or `def_id`, `line` the JSON line when known) and never calls `assert` (stripped in release builds), `push_error` (which trips gdUnit4) or `quit`. Content loads before the kernel exists, so the composition root acts on a non-empty list: it never starts the kernel, shows the errors, and exits non-zero in headless runs *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync: was `{code, class_or_table, id, def_id, field, detail}` and "the kernel's BOOTING acts"; ADR-0007 § 2.3)*. (v) Each file is opened by explicit name from the manifest (no directory scan), parsed with `JSON.new().parse(text)` so a failure carries `get_error_line()` and `get_error_message()` (`JSON.parse_string` gives no location), and every integer field is range- and integrality-checked at load (`ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`: `40000.5` and `1e9` are refused; an integral float such as `40000.0` is accepted and converted once); **any integer of magnitude 2^53 or more is refused** because a JSON number arrives as `float` and loses precision there (the bound is exclusive: `9007199254740993` silently parses to 2^53; ADR-0007 § 1.4). Array fields are read-only typed arrays stored once, and a packed array is never returned (ADR-0007 § 1.5). These are MEDIUM-confidence engine claims (no engine-reference module), spike-bound by AC-11. The engine reference has no module for this domain (`modules/` has animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering, ui; there is no `scripting.md` or `core.md`), so these claims are MEDIUM confidence and spike-bound; the 4.7 typed-return rule (an override of a typed-return method needs an explicit `return`) applies to any loader or validator base class.

**IDB-27 — Versioning, renames, retirement.** The Item DB offers Save/Load one integer, **`catalogue_version`**, bumped on any def removal, rename or field-shape change (F-9), plus `catalogue_hash` (diagnostic only, never gates a load; H64 over the canonical bytes of the post-cast typed rows, sorted ascending by `def_id`; ADR-0007 § 3.4 fixes this input and ADR-0005 the encoding). Pre-release saves are disposable [LSG:832]. After the first build given to anyone outside the project, a rename needs a `renamed_from[]` entry on the new def, honoured **only at restore**. The fields are reserved in the format now, and their restore behaviour (`resolve_alias`, `ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS`) is built in a story that must land before the ledger's `SHIPPED` flip, not in the MVP epic *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync, § 3.3)*; a removed def must name `replaced_by` or restore fails loudly with the holder's named failure — `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF` for Graph nodes [LSG:832], the holder's own code for instances; never silently. A value tuned within its type (`mass_g`, `req_w`, a glowstick number) is **not** structural and does not bump. The Item DB does not embed itself in saves. The integer is named `catalogue_version`, the name Save/Load's header table uses [SL:159] and its own Item DB row adopts [SL:476] (name history: Save/Load OQ-13 [SL:1301], closed). This is checkpoint ruling A2 applied to Save/Load's final text: the header is Save/Load's namespace (kernel row 3), so the Item DB carries the owner's written name. `catalogue_hash` is kept as diagnostic. `catalogue_hash` sorts the canonical bytes by `def_id` (ascending byte order), not by `type_id`, so a legal renumber never changes the diagnostic hash.

**IDB-28 — The query API (all pure, read-only).** `def(def_id)`, `has(def_id)`, `def_by_type_id(type_id)`, `defs_of_class(class)` (ascending `type_id`, the only order sim code may iterate), `compatible_defs(hardpoint)` (F-6), `repair_part_for(subject_kind, kind_id, loop)` (F-3), `salvage_yield(rec)` (F-5), `weight_class(def)` (F-1), `instance_mass_g(instance)` (F-2), `resolve_alias(def_id)` (restore only), `catalogue_version()`, `catalogue_hash()` and `has_both_frames(def_id, pair) -> bool` (true iff both tokens of `pair` are in the def's `sprite_states`; requested by In-World Instruments IW-17, it implements Sanity AC-SN-152's SPRITE_SWAP eligibility; presentation-only). No query returns a Dictionary iteration order; none mutates; none allocates an instance id. **Pure helpers (orchestrator ruling, QA B2):** `part_cost(subject_kind, kind_id, loop)` (F-4: the count rule only; the channel is Interaction's), `held_mass_g(instances)` (the F-2 holder sum), `validate_instance(instance, graph_step_no)` (IDB-15; returns a reason code, never clamps; `graph_step_no` is the Graph's absolute **10 Hz step** counter, never a 60 Hz tick: the `patched_at_step <= step_no` invariant compares steps with steps, and a harness that passes the kernel tick `T` fails the unit check `graph_step_no = T / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `fresh_instance(def_id, count, condition_bp) -> ItemInstance or the validate_instance reason code` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, GAP: the owner adds the authoring/seed constructor Derelict Authoring DA-26 needs; it is the single such constructor)*: for a module it forces `count` 1 and requires `condition_bp` in `1..HP_FULL`, with `cap_bp` `HP_FULL`, `patched_at_step` 0 and `charge` 0; for a non-module it requires `condition_bp` 0 and `count` in `1..stack_max`, with `cap_bp`, `patched_at_step` and `charge` 0; it allocates no instance id (CR-8) and never clamps, returning the `validate_instance` reason code for any input that fails, and `glowstick_reach(rem)` (F-7: equals Emitter State's D.9 [EM:506], which reproduces F-7 tick for tick; a **non-authoritative** reference oracle, Emitter State remains authoritative). The analysis formulas (F-1 margin, F-10, F-11, F-12, F-14, F-15) are CI tools in `tools/ci/item_db_lints`, not runtime queries. **Totality (orchestrator ruling, QA B4/B5):** `def` and `def_by_type_id` of an unknown key return the shared immutable sentinel `NO_DEF` (empty `def_id`, `type_id` 0, mass 0; callers test `has` first, and a sentinel reaching a holder is the holder's named failure); `repair_part_for` of an unknown `kind_id` returns (`NONE`, 0); `salvage_yield` of an ABSENT or SALVAGED record returns an empty list; `resolve_alias` outside the restore path returns `NO_DEF`.

**IDB-29 — The faucet, the sinks, and what the Item DB refuses to be.** Salvage is the only faucet: an item enters the world by an authored placement, by `salvage_yield`, or by the Suit's one-time starting kit, and by nothing else. There is no shop, no currency, no recipe, no crafting, no vendor and no refill station. Anti-pillars applied [CON:285-298]: not a freeform ship-building sandbox (a def fixes its ports and cannot be edited), not a combat game (no weapon stats on any def), no authored story, no win state. Scarcity comes from O2, power, Attention and finite parts — not from tool upkeep.

| Faucet | Mechanism | Bound | Owner of the placement number |
|---|---|---|---|
| Module node | `salvage_node` yields the node's own def at its `hp` | one per authored module node, once | Derelict Authoring |
| Scrap | `salvage_node` on a DESTROYED node (or a FAILED node at `hp` 0, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*) yields `scrap_count` (pinned to 1) of its part | at most one per destroyed node; counted in F-11 supply, including a node the player destroys on purpose *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | wear and damage (the Graph) |
| Loose placement | an authored part, glowstick or spare | one per placement, picked up once | Derelict Authoring, Layout |
| Starting kit | tools (cutter, welder, scanner), starting glowsticks | one-time; **the kit contains no def without a consumer** (AC-109) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): Option A (X6, D19, D61), `tool_suppressant` is removed from the kit; it would have shipped with 3 charges and no effect at MVP (AT-19, HE-39), so a tool the player cannot verify read as a lie or a bug. Suit's side (SE-11 slot 4, SE-14, SE-15) is amended in its own GDD; the registry constant `SUPPRESSANT_START_CHARGES` is retired)* | Suit & Equipment (designed) |

| Sink | Mechanism | Capacity |
|---|---|---|
| Part | a full repair consumes `parts_per_full` (IDB-20) | finite: equals the parts supply |
| Glowstick | `throw_glowstick`; a landed stick is spent (IDB-12) | finite: equals the stick supply |
| ~~Suppressant charge~~ *(dormant, outside the MVP; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | use would spend one charge (IDB-11) | none at MVP: no def in the MVP roster carries a charge |
| Installed module | converted into ship capability | bounded: the hardpoint count is Layout's, and the load a hardpoint may add is bounded by the Power budget (IDB-8): the 2161 W trunk cap binds before the 2500 W reactor, leaving 261 W of headroom with the beacon lit, so one heater (+500 W) overloads it by design (DA-47) and one more scrubber (+600 W) is a destroyed trunk in about 2.1 minutes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "open")* |
| Discarded section | `discard_section` (M4) discards everything installed [LSG:183] | Layout's and Assembly's |

*Basic economy metrics* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the review asked for demand rows against a "45-55 minute sortie clock"; that figure is not a sortie length — Derelict Authoring DA-25 gives "about 45 to 55 minutes in" as the time of the second sortie, and Boarding gives a five-minute loop with an 8-minute safe dwell, `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 ticks — so the rows below use only sourced figures and mark the rest as measured in playtest)*:

| Metric | Supply | Demand | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Repair parts (all families) | 4 loose placements (A 2, B 2) plus at most one per DESTROYED node | 4 parts for the 5 authored, non-seam, non-spare-covered FULL subjects (F-11 demand scope: authored faults only; the installable `scr_a` and the home machines are not counted) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | DA-25; F-11 | sourced |
| Glowsticks | 12 loose across 9 loose placements (AC-DA-35) plus Suit's start count | one pool at a time over an 8-minute safe dwell = ceil(480 s / `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` 150 s) = 4 sticks per sortie if a pool is lit throughout | AC-DA-35; Boarding `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS`; IDB-12 | derived |
| Patch actions | no item cost | about 20 patch actions before the first full repair on the leaky home faults | DA-25 (X-DA-9) | sourced; playtest target |
| FULL repairs | bounded by parts supplied | 4 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | F-11 | sourced |
| ~~Suppressant charges~~ *(row retired; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | 0 (`SUPPRESSANT_START_CHARGES` retired, X6) | 0 | AT-19; HE-39 | Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions), Open Question 29 |

Five economy rules follow. (1) **A patch is free of items; a part is an upgrade priced in scarcity.** A patch is free of items by locked design [REG:6033], so the part is never the *only* way to survive an MVP fault; it is the only way to stop *paying time* for it. That honest reading of Pillar 5 means **the MVP is always survivable with zero parts for a crew of N = 1: the patch treadmill is a playable, deliberately costly terminal state** (guarantee G-0, an authoring acceptance, **qualified per crew size N** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "always survivable with zero parts" held only for N = 1 in a 1-to-4-player game; for N >= 2 one scrubber runs a deficit even fully repaired (F-14), so G-0 is "survivable for N = 1; for N >= 2 survivable only until the O2 tank empties unless a second scrubber is installed", and AC-42 is parameterised by N)*; it **holds by LS-10 and M7**: `FAILED` is repairable [LSG:125] and M7 rejects only DESTROYED [LSG:186], so a patch works on the broken breaker at `hp` 2000; the LS-26 phrase "dark until replaced" [LSG:228] is prose, routed to the Graph owner as a wording question, and one spare `mod_breaker_junction` placement is a recommendation to Derelict Authoring, not an Item DB guarantee): the terminal state when the parts are spent is the patch treadmill, and that state is designed as playable. It is a treadmill, not a farm: a patch decays (`PATCH_DECAY_S`), a patched scrubber holds one crew at margin zero, and with N >= 2 crew the treadmill loses ground (F-14: one scrubber, even fully repaired, runs a 200 mGU/step deficit at N = 2; holding N crew takes N patched scrubbers at margin zero and N times the re-patch chores), so zero-part survival is pressure and not a safe place to farm (the Pillar 5 design test). What makes the part worth hunting is quantified in F-14 (a patched scrubber at `PATCH_CAP_BP` makes exactly `R_BREATH` for one crew — margin zero — against a margin of +100 mGU/step for a fully repaired one, and the margin vanishes with a second crew). (2) **Part conservation: no verb converts parts.** `scrap_count` is exactly 1 on every module def in the MVP (boot-asserted), no def consumes a part to yield an item (no recycling, no upgrade), and the number of part instances in existence never exceeds the authored loose parts plus the DESTROYED module nodes. (3) **A supply ratio is an authoring target, not a runtime field.** The ratio of parts supplied to full-repair parts a diligent player could demand, `part_cover_pm` (F-11, now measured per part family and per reachable sortie set, in parts), is held in an advisory band of 500-800 permille [PH]; below it permanent fixes feel out of reach, above it every chronic fault is solved and nothing pushes inward. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: whether a per-family cover of 100 percent is intended is Open Question 38, resolved 2026-10-28 by delegated ruling G2.)* (orchestrator ruling: the three specialist proposals gave 750-1000, 500-750 and 500-800; the reconciled band is 500-800.) (4) **No placement without a consumer**: a module names an install target kind, a part a repair family, a consumable a `use_verb`, a tool its `verbs[]` — a def with no consumer in a placement table is the lint `PLACEMENT_NO_CONSUMER` (WARN; the data core, in the MVP). A lint fails any def or file under `assets/data/items/` that carries a forbidden key (IDB-5). (5) **Depth richness is expressed by placement, never by def stats (BINDING on Derelict Authoring and Depth Rings & Scarcity).** "Deeper Is Richer" is delivered through placement density, part supply (`part_cover_pm` by ring: F-11's band is the MVP default and Depth Rings may set a per-ring target) and authored `hp` (better-condition modules deeper). It is never delivered through def stats: no def carries a worth, dialect is a skin (IDB-23), and `grade` stays 0 and behaviourless; any future behaviour for `grade`, or any stat that differs by ring or dialect, requires a creative-director review before it enters a def.

**IDB-30 — Boot order and the closed failure list.** The catalogue is loaded at start-up, before the kernel's BOOTING (ADR-0007 § 1.7, stage 2), **after** the Graph's type tables and `GraphTuning` are loaded (it needs LS-4 for port-id validation and the pure `connectable` for lint) and **before any block restore** (the Graph's `def_id` validation at restore reads it). Lifecycle: UNLOADED, PARSED, VALIDATED, FROZEN, REFUSED (see States). Failure codes — a closed list, all fatal at boot, all fault domain 2 (content data) in `ContentFaults` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync, § 2.3)*: `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(class, id)` (IDB-4); `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, field)` (missing, out of range, a forbidden key, a float where an integer is required, a heavy def with `stack_max` above 1, `hp_start` outside 1..`HP_FULL`, an integer above 2^53, a module `scrap_count` other than 1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the last two added)*; `ITEM_BOOT_PORT(def_id, local_index)` (a port-type id not in LS-4, or illegal for the node kind); `ITEM_BOOT_REPAIR(def_id)` (`repair_part_id` not a part def, `parts_per_full` below 1, or `repair_part_for` not total over the five `subject_kind` values [LSG:150]); `ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR(table)` (the LS-3 or LS-4 mirror differs from the Graph's boot table); `ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS(def_id)` (IDB-4). `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD` also carries the three structural invariants of the tuning knobs (orchestrator ruling, QA B7): `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` below `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`, `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` below `THROW_RANGE_TILES` (reported as `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(cons_glowstick, radius_tiles)`) and `WANE_TICKS` at least 1; the published ranges in Tuning Knobs are guidance checked by an advisory lint, not boot assertions. An authored `def_id` unknown to the catalogue (`PLACEMENT_DEF_UNKNOWN`) is checked at boot as well as in CI, because a runtime `fresh_instance` on it would be unsafe (Derelict DA-41 BOOT set; ADR-0007 § 2.4) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync: was CI only)*. **Content lints (CI, not boot):** a hardpoint with no compatible def [LSG:774]; `WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM` (IDB-21; blocking); the junk tell (IDB-22); `PLACEMENT_NO_CONSUMER` (IDB-29; WARN); the part-cover band (F-11; `PART_COVER_BAND`) and the part-supply cap (`PART_SUPPLY_EXCEEDS`); the content-lint ids are Derelict Authoring's (DA-40; registry: `content_lint_ids`), this GDD supplies the rules they check; the binding port-cap lint (F-12: severed leak, seam capacity and the `seam_cap_floor / rated_flow` ratio per fluid port); the weight-window and roster-semantic lints (IDB-21); `PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED` (F-11); and the start-kit consumer lint (IDB-29, AC-109) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The Item DB raises no runtime error: at runtime every query is total.

**IDB-31 — The MVP roster: twenty-three defs, Foundry only** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "twenty-four"; X6 removed `tool_suppressant`)*. `type_id`s are the append-only ids of IDB-4; masses are all [PH] and registry candidates; `weight_class` uses `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` 5000 and `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` 25000.

Masses for the scrubber, reactor cell, pump, radiator, reservoir, O2 tank, fuel tank, data core, hull plate and glowstick follow the ruled values; the battery cell is 22000 g (retuned off the `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` boundary, IDB-21); the rest are proposals inside the ruled bands (tools 1500-4000 g, parts 500-2000 g bar the plate). Every module is `salvageable` true by default because M6 excludes only COMPARTMENT, EXTERIOR and HULL_SEGMENT [LSG:185]; a def may set it false (none does in the MVP) but the Graph does not enforce it — Interaction's `begin_cut` `validate` reads it. "Which modules are carryable" is the class default: all modules and the curio are CARRIED (the single `carry_ref`), tools are on the hand rail, consumables and parts are stowed. The next free module `type_id` is 1014. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the counts "24 defs" and "next id 1014" are literals in prose and tests that break when a def is added; the single source of truth for every count and for the next free id is the golden fixture `tests/fixtures/item_db/roster_golden.json`, and the ACs read it instead of repeating the literals.)*

*Which MVP defs carry an `emits`* (all values [PH]; the semantics are Emitter State's, EM-14 and EM-15, and the contract is binding because Emitter State is designed): `mod_lamp` `{FIXTURE, base_pm 1000, hue_class a fixture token}`; `mod_reactor_cell`, `mod_coolant_pump` and `mod_scrubber` as node lanterns `{LANTERN, base_pm 300, 150 and 200 [PH], hue_class LANTERN_POWER, LANTERN_COOLANT and LANTERN_O2}` (EM-14 as restated 2026-10-28: a node lantern is carried by every node whose def carries `emits` with shape `LANTERN` [EM:194]; shape row 9 [EM:179]), all three with the default `lantern_on_states` `{0 OK, 1 DEGRADED}`; `cons_glowstick` `{POOL_GLOWSTICK, base_pm 1000, CHEMLIGHT}`. Five defs carry an `emits`; no other def does. A lantern def's `hue_class` is the per-loop token of its node's loop (`LANTERN_O2`, `LANTERN_POWER`, `LANTERN_COOLANT`, `LANTERN_FUEL`, `LANTERN_INTEGRITY`; 2D Lighting LO-8, Ask E-A; registry: `hue_class_token_map`; the three MVP lantern defs use `LANTERN_POWER`, `LANTERN_COOLANT` and `LANTERN_O2`), never a bare `LANTERN` hue token; `mod_lamp` keys `FIXTURE`. The token-to-hex mapping and the sixth-slot assignment are Lighting's (LO-8, LO-9). *Derivation of the scrubber's 200:* it sits between the pump (150) and the reactor (300) and is at or above `LIT_ON_PM` 150, so the lantern lights at least its own anchor. At the Emitter defaults (R = `LANTERN_REACH_PX` 96, `LIT_ON_PM` 150, `LIT_OFF_PM` 120, F(d) = floor(1000(96-d)/96), c = floor(200 x F / 1000)): r_lit = 24 (d 24: F 750, c 150; d 25: F 739, c 147) and r_off = 38 (d 38: F 604, c 120; d 39: F 593, c 118). Method check against Lighting AC-LO-64: `base_pm` 300 gives r_lit 48 / r_off 57; `base_pm` 150 gives r_lit 0 / r_off 19 — the same chain reproduces both. **Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6 (user delegated all remaining calls; creative-director ruling): the scrubber carries a node lantern.** Reason: Pillar 1, an air scrubber is the machine the crew most needs to read at a glance, and the scr_a dark-lantern beat is the MVP's clearest falsifier witness; the alternative (conform Derelict Authoring and Boarding to an unlit scrubber) is rejected. The registry entry `mvp_fixture_counts.lanterns` (home 3 r_main/pump_main/scr_main, A 2 pump_a/scr_a, B 3 r_b/pump_b/scr_b, world 8 of 20) stands and this GDD conforms. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

**IDB-32 — The formula register.** The Formulas section (Section D) defines these by the ids below, and every other section cites them by id. F-1 `weight_class`; F-2 `instance_mass_g`; F-3 `repair_part_for`; F-4 `part_cost`; F-5 `salvage_yield`; F-6 `ports_match` and `compatible_defs`; F-7 the glowstick derivation (`GLOWSTICK_LIFE_TICKS`, radius in pixels, the wane curve contract); F-8 the id-ledger and bijection assertion; F-9 the `catalogue_version` bump rule; F-10 the junk off-band rule; F-11 `part_cover` (authoring lint); F-12 the binding port-cap lint (severed leak and D.14 seam capacity per fluid port); F-13 haul cost (mass-independent by lock); F-14 the scrubber margin ladder (why a part is worth a sortie); F-15 the salvage-reinstall break-even (withdrawn as a blocking item, design review 2026-10-27; id kept). Formulas the Graph owns — D.4 `eff`, D.5 `out_scr`, D.6 and D.8 — are cited by name and line and never restated.

### States and Transitions

The Item DB has three state machines, none of them per-tick: the **catalogue lifecycle** (boot only), the **def lifecycle** (across builds) and the **instance location** (the states an `ItemInstance` can be in, defined here and *performed* by the named owner). Nothing in this section runs in a kernel step.

**Catalogue lifecycle (boot only; one pass).**

| State | Entry | Exit | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNLOADED | start-up content load, stage 2 (before the kernel's BOOTING; ADR-0007 § 1.7) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync)* | the data files are read | -> PARSED | the composition root's `ContentLoader.load_all()` |
| PARSED | every `assets/data/items/*.json` read; every designer float converted once to an integer | tables built, nothing yet checked | -> VALIDATED; -> REFUSED on a parse or type error (`ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`) | the loader |
| VALIDATED | the IDB-4 ledger checks, IDB-7 port checks, IDB-19 repair resolution (total over the five `subject_kind` values), IDB-30 mirror equality and alias checks all pass | end of BOOTING | -> FROZEN; -> REFUSED on any closed-list failure code | the loader |
| FROZEN | end of BOOTING | none in a release build (re-entered only by a new process; no hot reload) | none; all IDB-28 queries answer | nobody — the catalogue is immutable |
| REFUSED | any boot code of IDB-30 | terminal | none; the kernel reports a BOOTING failure and the build does not start | the loader |

**Def lifecycle (content, across builds).**

| State | Meaning | Entry | Exit and transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ACTIVE | in the catalogue and offered to authors | a def is added with a fresh `type_id` | a rename keeps it ACTIVE under the new id with `renamed_from`; a removal moves it to RETIRED | a content author, with a `catalogue_version` bump (F-9) |
| RETIRED | removed from play; its `type_id` is in `retired_ids`; `replaced_by` names a successor or is empty | a def is removed | never revived; its `type_id` and its `def_id` are never reused; a save naming it restores through `replaced_by` or fails loudly | a content author; the Save/Load restore consumes it |

Every ACTIVE-to-ACTIVE rename and every move to RETIRED bumps `catalogue_version`.

**Instance location.** The Item DB defines these states and the legal moves between them; the **named owner** performs every move through its own command or mutator, and the Item DB performs none. (Suit, Interaction, Assembly, Boarding and Layout are designed as of the 2026-10-27 consistency pass; each repeats its moves in its own GDD.)

| State | Meaning | Held by |
|---|---|---|
| INSTALLED | a module that is a Graph node (the instance has dissolved into the node's `def_id` and `hp`) | the Graph |
| LOOSE_WORLD | an item on the ground: a placement, a dropped module, a downed carrier's module, a shed stowed stack | Interaction's `loose[]` (IA-28) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: owner no longer open)* |
| CARRIED | the one module or curio on `carry_ref` | the Controller (id) and Suit (instance) |
| STOWED | a counted entry in a player's stowed stock | Suit |
| IN_HAND | the tool in the hand-rail slot | Suit |
| SPENT | consumed; terminal | nobody |

| # | From | To | Trigger | Performed by | What happens to the `ItemInstance` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | none | INSTALLED | an authored node in a template (M1 at build) | Authoring and Layout via the Graph | no instance exists; the node holds `def_id` and `hp` (the placement override or `hp_start`) |
| 2 | none | LOOSE_WORLD | an authored salvage-slot placement | Authoring | the instance is created by the placement |
| 3 | INSTALLED | LOOSE_WORLD | `salvage_node` (M6), then `salvage_yield` (F-5) | Interaction (the cut verb places the yield in `loose[]`, IA-28); a module reaches CARRIED only by row 4, a separate `pickup` | one instance with `condition_bp = hp`, `cap_bp = hp_cap` and `patched_at_step` as recorded in M6's five-field record (IDB-18, LS-18 M6), with `patched_at_step` written 0 when `cap_bp` is `HP_FULL` (F-5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional" removed; Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: "or CARRIED" removed, no system performed it, and the cut-to-hands path is one `loose[]` entry then one pickup, so the full instance must survive IA-28 (IDB-16, conformed 2026-10-28))*; or `scrap_count` parts if DESTROYED or at `hp` 0 |
| 4 | LOOSE_WORLD | CARRIED | `pickup` (sets `carry_ref`) | Interaction | CARRIED-class only; gait `V_CARRY` |
| 5 | CARRIED | LOOSE_WORLD | `drop` (Interaction), or downed (Controller, PC-8) | Interaction (`drop`); Controller (downed) | unchanged |
| 6 | LOOSE_WORLD | STOWED | `pickup` of a STOWED-class item | Interaction and Suit | merges per IDB-15 |
| 7 | STOWED | IN_HAND | equip | Suit | a tool instance with its `charge` |
| 8 | CARRIED | INSTALLED | `install_module` (calls M5) | Assembly | the instance dissolves; `hp_start := condition_bp`; `hp_cap := cap_bp` and `patched_at_step` passed to M5 and kept (IDB-18, LS-18 M5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional" removed)*; stock 0 |
| 9 | STOWED | SPENT | the first productive step of a full repair (IDB-20); the part is RESERVED from the Applied begin tick (registry: `full_job_charge`) | Interaction | the visible `count` falls by `parts_per_full` at the begin tick; the part is PAID at the first productive step, or released if the channel ends early with no credited block or on an OUTCOME (no timer) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* |
| 10 | STOWED | SPENT | `throw_glowstick` is accepted | Interaction; Emitter State spawns the pool | `count` decrements by 1; the glow is the Emitter's |
| 11 | IN_HAND | IN_HAND | use of a charge-bearing tool (no MVP def has one; the dormant suppressant would) | Suit | `charge` decrements; inert at MVP *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| 12 | CARRIED | CARRIED | a pod hop with the module [PCI:173, 694] | Boarding | unchanged |
| 13 | any | the previous autosave | crew death (restart from the last autosave) | Save/Load | nothing: the restore replays the holder blocks; **no item-loss rule exists** |
| 14 | STOWED | LOOSE_WORLD | **shed or hand-over**: the holder sheds `n` (1..the stack count) of one stowed stack onto the ground at their position; a crewmate takes it with row 6. The same move is how the plate leaves the back (the MVP's only HEAVY stowed item, so "drop the plate first" becomes performable) and how parts and the 1-of-1 plate pass between crewmates *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: the table had no STOWED -> LOOSE_WORLD move, recorded by Boarding as ASK-BS-S1 / AC-BS-96)* | Interaction (a shed verb that writes `loose[]`, IA-28) together with a Suit holder mutator that removes `n` from the stack (Suit names it; the Item DB invents no mutator name, and Suit's published surface is closed, SE-40) | one `ItemInstance` of the stack's def with `count` `n` (canonical zeros, IDB-15) appears in `loose[]`; the stack's count falls by `n`; units RESERVED by a FULL job (SE-41) cannot be shed; Suit's `cue_load` is re-derived on the next `suit.vitals` run (AC-107) |
| 15 | STOWED | STOWED (unchanged) | the holder is DOWNED (cap NONE) | Suit and the Controller | **stays on the body until revive**: nothing spills, no `loose[]` entry is made, the stack is unchanged (Suit already persists `reserved` counts, SE-41, and a spill would duplicate entries). A carried module still drops (row 5). In the MVP the death of every seat restarts from the autosave (row 13); a seat that is DEAD while others live is the Failure Contract's and Co-op's (Vertical Slice B): the recommended rule there is that the body's stowed stock then spills as LOOSE_WORLD at the body's position so surviving crew can recover the parts *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: the fate of a downed holder's stowed stock was undefined)*; the row is a statement, not an action |

SPENT is terminal. There is no consumed-by-death transition (user decision: death handling is Save/Load's restart). A module that leaves INSTALLED by M6 and re-enters by M5 is a different `ItemInstance` carrying the same `def_id`, the record's `hp`, its cap and its decay clock (IDB-18, settled);

**Binding ask for rows 14 and 15** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: these are requirements on **Suit & Equipment** (a holder mutator that removes `n` from a stowed stack and returns the instance, and the row-15 statement that a DOWNED seat's stock is unchanged) and on **Interaction** (a shed verb that builds the `loose[]` entry from that return, with a noise class). They are not open questions; they are routed as asks, Open Question 34, and the review criteria AC-107 and AC-108 close them when Suit and Interaction repeat them. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the shed path depends on a Suit mutator and an Interaction verb that **do not exist yet**; every D18 story that needs the plate to leave the back by shedding or hand-over (AC-46 (iii) and the row-14 stories) is therefore **BLOCKED-ON Open Question 34**, and the only unblocked ways the plate leaves the back until then are the FULL job that consumes it and the autosave restore.)* Until they exist the MVP has no way to shed the plate, which Boarding's ladder already accounts for (BS-14).

Beyond the fields IDB-15 lists, the Item DB tracks no individual module history.

### Interactions with Other Systems

The Item DB registers no hook, block, command or mutator, so every row is a **data** contract: what the neighbour reads from the catalogue, what the catalogue reads from the neighbour at boot or lint time, and who owns the live state. Names fixed by the kernel, the Graph or the controller are in code format; everything else about a Vertical Slice (still undesigned) neighbour is **(provisional)** and must be repeated by that neighbour when its GDD is written (the Graph's own criterion for this, [LSG:1140]); every MVP neighbour is designed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Dependency status (refreshed 2026-10-27, *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "checked by Glob on 2026-10-26 ... no other dependent exists" paragraph was stale*): every MVP sibling GDD carries Status: Designed — the kernel, render pipeline, controller, Graph, Save/Load (`save-load-and-versioned-format.md`, SL:5), Emitter State (`emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query.md`, EM:5), Failure & Trail (`failure-and-trail-system.md`, FT:5), Suit, Layout, Derelict Authoring, Assembly, Boarding, Interaction, Attention, Sanity, Perception Filter, In-World Instruments, HUD & Suit Layer, 2D Lighting, Horror Entities, Adaptive Audio and Tells, Ship Body and Web Map. The Save/Load and Emitter State rows below quote each sibling's own Item DB row back as a binding contract (Save/Load Core Rules SL:133-304, Emitter State Core Rules EM:99-253). Failure & Trail's Interactions row 6 [FT:305] labels this system designed, reads no def and no `def_id`, and states that no Item DB Interactions row is needed because no data crosses (FT:321); this GDD agrees and adds none. Each row's contract is the one that sibling repeats in its own Item DB row; whether it does is a document checklist (the former AC-63 / AC-96 / AC-102, now in "Document checklist" under Acceptance Criteria), not a coverage-counted criterion.

| System | Status | Data in (to the Item DB) | Data out (from the Item DB) | Interface and exact names | Ownership clause |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Life-Support Graph** | designed | LS-3 node-kind ids, LS-4 port-type ids, the pure `connectable(a, b)`, the loop ids (LS-2), `GraphTuning`'s bounds, `HP_FULL`, `HP_OK`, `HP_FAIL`, `PATCH_CAP_BP`, `PATCH_DECAY_S` (by name); `SalvageRecord{def_id, hp, state, hp_cap, patched_at_step}` returned by M6 (LS-18; was a three-field record before the Graph's 2026-10-27 amendment), M5's `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step` arguments, `cap_seam` (D.14) as a pure function callable at data load | module defs (`node_kind`, `ports[]`, `power_tier`, `req_w`, `req_max`, `hp_start`, `waste_permille`, `rated_out`, `stock_cap`, `duty_wear`), `def_id` strings for M5, `repair_part_for` for M7 FULL, `salvage_yield` for the M6 return, `def_id` validation at restore | M5 `install_module`, M6 `salvage_node`, M7 `repair_step`; `check_install_module`, `check_repair_step`; failures `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `NOT_REPAIRABLE`, `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`, `GRAPH_BOOT_TYPE_ID(table, id)`; no hook, no dirty key; data load in BOOTING, M5/M6 in P2 | The Graph owns the ids and the physics; the Item DB owns the vocabulary and the numbers the Graph marks "data (module def)" |
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (kernel) | designed (context) | the BOOTING sequence (CR-6), H10 scene-tree-free | nothing: no block, hook or command | KRN Interactions table (KRN:190-206) has no Item DB row | The Item DB is invisible to the kernel; instance ids are allocated by the owning block (CR-8) |
| **Player Controller & Input** | designed | `V_CARRY` 1, `NOISE_PER_TICK` carry 1 and walk 1 and run 4, the single `carry_ref`, PC-8 `BUSY` rule, `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3 and `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 | `carry_class`, `mass_g` and `weight_class` (to Suit, which the controller reads from) | `controller.state.carry_ref` [PCI:131]; `throw_glowstick {aim_angle}` [PCI:79] | Mass never changes speed or noise; the Controller owns the carry |
| **Pixel-Perfect Pipeline / Art bible** | designed | `TILE_PX` 48, sprite cells (8.2), naming grammar (8.5), Chemlight, the tool-silhouette rule (5.1, 7.2) | `sprite_family`, `cell`, `glyph_id` keys | ART 8.2, 8.5, 5.1, 7.2 | The Item DB holds string keys; art owns the pixels |
| **Suit & Equipment** | designed (`suit-and-equipment.md`) | slots, start loadout, per-player glowstick and charge counts, the carry-weight body cue (**binding for stowed mass at or above `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` too**, IDB-21) | `ToolDef`, `ConsumableDef`, `ItemInstance`, `mass_g`, `weight_class`, `charges_max`, the glowstick numbers | Suit's PER_PLAYER block (kernel row 4: "equip/loadout/consume commands") | Item DB defines what a thing is; Suit owns who holds it and how many |
| **Ship Section & Layout Data Model** | designed (`ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md`) | hardpoint declarations (accepted `node_kind` plus an ordered port-type list), the salvage and module slots | `compatible_defs(hardpoint)` (F-6), footprint `cell`, `dialect` | the Layout lint; LSG:774 "a hardpoint with no compatible module" | Layout owns slot geometry and hardpoint declarations; the Item DB owns what fits |
| **Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)** | designed (`derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`) | placements by `def_id`; per-placement `hp` | stable `def_id` strings; the dead-module convention (IDB-24); `hp_start` for a fresh node | the content lint | Authoring owns placement; the Item DB owns the vocabulary; an unknown `def_id` fails the lint (`PLACEMENT_DEF_UNKNOWN`), not the runtime. Lane nodes (OQ-DA-26, OQ-DA-33): `j_main` and `j_b_main` are `mod_breaker_junction` (three 204 PWR_THRU ports, `cap` 3000 W each, IDB-7), the CABLE lane endpoints; `dj_air` and `dj_b_dock` are bare DUCT_JUNCTION (23) nodes with no Item DB def (IDB-25: junction kinds get no def until a template uses one), so their DUCT `cap` is not published here (Open Question 30) |
| **Modular Ship Assembly** | designed (`modular-ship-assembly.md`) | the `install_module` command (`remove_module` is reserved and unregistered, MSA C9) | `ModuleDef`, the `SalvageRecord`-to-`ItemInstance` mapping (`salvage_yield`) | calls M5 only; Assembly does not call M6, V8 salvage is Interaction's (MSA C9) (kernel row 5: "install, uninstall as single commands whose `apply` calls published mutators") | The Item DB defines no command |
