Offers Save/Load one integer content epoch, bumped on a def removal, rename or field-shape change, plus a diagnostic `catalogue_hash` (never gates a load), stable `def_id` strings, `renamed_from[]` honoured only at restore and `replaced_by`; an unknown `def_id` at restore is the holder's named failure. **Name:** the integer is the Item DB's `catalogue_version` (IDB-26 `meta.json` key, IDB-27, its Save/Load row and registry candidate), which this GDD uses as the header field (history: OQ-13). The Item DB's item-lifecycle transition for crew death ("the previous autosave"; "no item-loss rule exists") agrees with SL-7." Consistent: its **Name:** clause adopts `catalogue_version`, which is this GDD's name (IDB-27); no collision note remains (Save/Load OQ-13 closed [SL:1301]); an unknown `def_id` at restore is the holder's named failure (AC-SL-96e [SL:1220]) |

Its Item DB paragraph [EM:354] records the `emits` field shape, the `LAMP` subtype, the glowstick parameters, `POOL_GLOWSTICK`, `emits` on any glowing def and the `lantern_on_states` default as adopted, and leaves two asks: (a) lantern defs `emits.shape_id` `FIXTURE` to `LANTERN` (shape row 9) and (b) `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM` as the def's `emits.base_pm`; this GDD answers both (IDB-6, IDB-12). | **Procedural Derelict Gen., Depth Rings & Scarcity, Resource Depletion & Wear, Knowledge Codex** | vertical slice, undesigned | `grade` values; wear-rate overrides keyed by `def_id` [LSG:274]; data-core consumption | reserved `grade` (0), `curio_data_core`, the `junk_` class, the reserved `"item.yield"` stream name | none in the MVP | Reserve fields, define no vertical-slice behaviour. **Binding on Depth Rings:** depth richness is placement density, part supply (`part_cover_pm` by ring) and authored `hp`, never def stats; `grade` stays behaviourless unless the creative-director reviews it (IDB-29 rule 5) |

**Bidirectional consistency with the Graph.** The Graph's own Interactions row for this system reads (quoted, [LSG:261]): "Item & Module Database | module defs: `node_kind`, `ports[]` by port-type id, `cap`, `tier`, `req_w`, `hp_start`, `waste_permille`; repair part ids | `SalvageRecord` on M6; `def_id` validation at restore | Item DB consumes the LS-4 ids; the Graph owns them (ADR-LSG-08) | data load, M5/M6 in P2 | provisional (Item DB unwritten; the id contract is fixed here)". *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: that is the pre-amendment text. The Graph's current row, found by the name "Item & Module Database" in its Interactions table (cited by name, not by line number) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, reads: data out "`SalvageRecord{def_id, hp, state, hp_cap, patched_at_step}` on M6 (read by `salvage_yield`); `def_id` validation at restore; `cap_seam` (D.14) callable as a pure function at data load for the Item DB F-12 lint"; ownership "The Graph reads `waste_permille`, `R_SCR`, `BATT_CELL`, `cap_ml` and `out_w_rated` live through `def_id` — never copied at M5, never persisted"; and status "designed". The 301/302 direction, the cap-0 meaning on 901-903, power-port caps read only by D.14, a VENT drawing no power, `out_w_rated` 2500 W and the LOAD subtypes 1/2/3 are all stated there.)* Its Dependencies row reads (current text): "Module definitions reference this GDD's **port-type IDs** (stable numeric, append-only); `install_module` / `salvage_node` exchange `def_id`, `hp`, `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step`; the yield is the Item DB's `salvage_yield`, deterministic at MVP. Full repair consumes an Item DB part (`repair_part_for`); patch does not." This GDD answers each clause. Module defs: supplied field for field, with one **rename the loader maps** — the def field `power_tier` is the Graph's `tier` (the Graph's persisted LOAD `tier` and the module-def `tier` of its row), renamed so the word `tier` never means item value; `cap` is the per-port `cap` of IDB-7. Repair part ids: `repair_part_id` per def and `repair_part_for` for edges (IDB-19). `SalvageRecord`: consumed by `salvage_yield` (IDB-17); the Graph's M6 now returns `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step`, and a store's stock leaves into `discarded_*` at the apply, so the former routed conflict of IDB-18 is closed (LS-18 M5/M6; OQ-10 closed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `def_id` validation at restore: IDB-27. LS-4 ids: consumed by number and mirrored read-only (IDB-7). The loot roll: the deterministic `salvage_yield`. Full repair consumes a part, patch does not: IDB-19 and IDB-20. **Wording this GDD asked the Graph owner to confirm or fix** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: items (1) and (2) are now applied in the Graph's M5, M6, M7 and LS-23 text — the callers read "Assembly (`install_module`); the Item DB calls no mutator" and "Interaction (`begin_cut`); the yield is the Item DB's `salvage_yield`", and LS-23 reads "named by `repair_part_for(subject)`"; the edge direction (3) stands as explained)* (routed, none changes a Graph value): (1) the Graph's M5 and M6 "Caller" cells read "Item DB / Assembly (`install`)" and "Interaction / Item DB (loot roll is the Item DB's)" — the Item DB calls no mutator and defines no command, so the caller of M5 is Assembly's command and of M6 Interaction's `begin_cut`, with the Item DB supplying the pure `salvage_yield` after M6 returns; (2) LS-23 says a full repair "requires the Item DB part named by the subject's `def_id`", which is undefined for an edge — read "named by `repair_part_for(subject)`"; (3) the edge direction between the two systems in the index, explained next. The systems index lists this system as depending on the Graph, and the Graph lists the Item DB as *its* hard upstream (now "Hard", designed); both are true at different levels, and neither is a cycle: the Item DB consumes the Graph's **ids** at boot (hence the BOOTING order of IDB-30: Graph type tables first), and the Graph consumes the Item DB's **defs** at M5 and at restore (hence the catalogue is frozen before any block restore).

**Bidirectional consistency with the kernel and the controller.** The kernel's fourteen Interactions rows contain no Item DB row, which is consistent with a static catalogue (IDB-1); the Suit row (4) gives Suit the `PER_PLAYER` block and "equip/loadout/consume commands", and the Assembly row (5) gives Assembly "install, uninstall as single commands whose `apply` calls published mutators on Graph, Layout and Body" — both match IDB-16. The controller's downstream table lists, for Suit & Equipment, "what the controller takes from it: ... carry weight" [PCI:726]; this GDD supplies `mass_g` to Suit and states that the controller must take no speed or noise change from it (`V_CARRY` and the carry `NOISE_PER_TICK` are single constants), so the only legal reading of "carry weight" is the Suit's body cue. The controller's text on the glowstick keeps "throw verb and arc are Interaction's, charges are Suit's, colour is the art-director's (inside the palette)" [PCI:155]; this GDD adds only that the *def properties* (life, radius, stack, wane) are the Item DB's. **Index edges to add** (routed, not edited here: `systems-index.md` is not this GDD's file) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier "Phase 5d" wording and the word "provisional" are historical; every neighbour below is designed)*: the index lists no edge from the Item DB to Interaction, Emitter State, Attention, Perception Filter or HUD; Interaction's own Dependencies row already records "Soft — index edge missing", and each row above is the proposal for that edge.

## Formulas

All formulas are integer-only: the catalogue is data, and anything an instance carries into a holder block may be hashed or replicated (the kernel's `state_hash` and the Graph's LS-1 (b) rule), so no float is stored, hashed or compared. Truncation is floor, applied to non-negative operands only [LSG:64, LS-1 (d)]. Every duration is labelled **tick** (60 Hz, `TICK_RATE` 60) or **step** (the Graph's 10 Hz, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6) or **s**. Formulas the Graph owns (D.4 `eff`, D.5 `out_scr`, D.14 `seam_at_weld`) are cited by name and line and never restated; where an example needs their value it says "evaluated by calling D.n". The ids F-1 to F-15 are those of IDB-32. Formulas F-1 to F-10 and F-12 are **runtime or boot** formulas; F-11, F-14 and F-15 are **design-time checks** (authoring and balance lints), and F-13 is a derivation that proves a rule by lock.

### F-1 `weight_class`

The weight_class formula is defined as:

`weight_class(m) = LIGHT if m < WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G ; MEDIUM if WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G <= m < WEIGHT_HEAVY_G ; HEAVY if m >= WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` — and the blocking margin lint `margin_ok(m) = for T in {WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G, WEIGHT_HEAVY_G}: abs(m - T) * 1000 >= WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM * T`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `m` | int g | 1..500000 | def `mass_g` (IDB-5) | True mass of one unit |
| `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` | int g | **feasible 4445-5454**, default 5000 (the old range 3000-8000 is infeasible against the roster under the blocking margin) | data [PH] ★ registry candidate | LIGHT/MEDIUM boundary *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` | int g | **feasible 24445-27272** at margin 100, default 25000 (the old range 15000-40000 let the plate silently stop being HEAVY at 40000 and let a full stack of ordinary parts, 15,200 g, reach HEAVY at 15000) | data [PH] ★ registry candidate | MEDIUM/HEAVY boundary *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM` | int permille | **50-150**, default 100 (200 is infeasible: the `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` window closes between 150 and 160) | data [PH] ★ registry candidate | Minimum relative distance of any def's mass from either threshold *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** one of three classes; `margin_ok` is a boolean. Boot assertion `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G < WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` (`ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`). The margin lint is a blocking CI check (IDB-21), not a boot code. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the lint also computes and prints the feasible window of each threshold from the roster, and a roster-semantic lint asserts the plate is HEAVY and no other STOWED def is; the windows are derived in IDB-21 and are inputs to the registry ranges.)*
**Example:** `tool_cutter` 3500 g -> LIGHT (distance to 5000 is 1500 >= 5000 x 100 / 1000 = 500, pass). `curio_data_core` 6000 g -> MEDIUM (1000 >= 500, pass). `mod_battery_cell` 22000 g -> MEDIUM (distance to 25000 is 3000 >= 2500, pass; at the old 25000 g it sat exactly on the threshold, where the `>=` rule made it HEAVY and the lint failed). `mod_coolant_pump` 30000 g -> HEAVY (5000 >= 2500, pass). A def at 24000 g would fail the lint (1000 < 2500) and so cannot ship.

### F-2 `instance_mass_g`

The instance_mass_g formula is defined as:

`instance_mass_g(inst) = mass_g(inst.def_id) * inst.count` ; `held_mass_g(holder) = sum of instance_mass_g over the holder's CARRIED, STOWED and IN_HAND instances` ; `stowed_mass_g(holder) = sum of instance_mass_g over the holder's STOWED instances` (the quantity the binding body cue of IDB-21 reads: `weight_class(stowed_mass_g)` HEAVY means the cue is owed; Suit counts **only the unreserved** count of a stack, because units RESERVED by a FULL job are out of the pack from the begin tick, SE-41, and Suit's `cue_load` also takes the max with the carried class and excludes in-hand tools, SE-28) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mass_g(def_id)` | int g | 1..500000 | catalogue | Per-unit true mass |
| `inst.count` | int | 1..`stack_max` | `ItemInstance` (IDB-15) | Stack size; 1 for modules, tools and curios |

**Output Range:** 0..about 10^7 g (far below 2^53). Mass is **never persisted** on an instance (IDB-15); it is derived from the def on read, so a mass edit needs no migration.
**Example:** a player stows 4 `cons_glowstick` (200 g each = 800), 2 `part_pipe_fitting` (1200 g each = 2400) and 1 `part_machine_kit` (2000 g): `held_mass_g` = 800 + 2400 + 2000 = 5200 g; while also carrying a `mod_scrubber` (40000 g, count 1) it is 45200 g. The Suit turns the sum into a body cue (IDB-21); the Item DB supplies only the number. A player who stows one `part_hull_plate` (30000 g) has `stowed_mass_g` 30000, at or above `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` 25000, so the body cue is owed even though no module is carried.

### F-3 `repair_part_for`

The repair_part_for formula is defined as:

`repair_part_for(subject_kind, kind_id, loop) = (d.repair_part_id, d.parts_per_full) for NODE with def d ; (node_kind_part[kind_id], 1) for NODE with empty def_id ; (edge_part[(kind_id, loop)], 1) for EDGE ; (NONE, 0) for COMPARTMENT, SEAM, OPENING`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `subject_kind` | enum | NODE 1, EDGE 2, COMPARTMENT 3, SEAM 4, OPENING 5 | Graph M7 [LSG:150, 186] | The function is total over all five values |
| `kind_id` | int | an LS-3 node-kind id or an LS-5 edge-kind id | Graph [LSG:68, 72] | Selects the row of IDB-19 |
| `loop` | int | 0..5 | Graph [LSG:66] | Needed for PIPE, VALVE and SEAM sub-edges |
| `d.repair_part_id` | string | a `part_` def | module def (IDB-19) | The part a module names |
| `d.parts_per_full` | int | 1..3, default 1 (reactor 2) | module def [PH] | Parts one full job costs |

**Output Range:** `part_def_id` in the part class, or `NONE`; `count` 0..3 (0 only with `NONE`). A FULL repair never costs zero parts; `NONE` means no FULL job is offered (IDB-20 (9)). Totality over the five subject kinds is boot-checked (`ITEM_BOOT_REPAIR`).
**Example:** `mod_scrubber` node -> (`part_machine_kit`, 1); `mod_reactor_cell` node -> (`part_machine_kit`, 2); `mod_door_motor` node -> (`part_cable_splice`, 1); the oxygen pinhole DUCT (edge kind 1, loop 1) -> (`part_duct_coupling`, 1); the coolant-leak PIPE (kind 3, loop 3) -> (`part_pipe_fitting`, 1); the fuel-drip PIPE (kind 3, loop 4) -> (`part_pipe_fitting`, 1); a SEAM sub-edge of loop 3 (edge kind 6) -> (`part_pipe_fitting`, 1); HULL_SEGMENT (node kind 50) -> (`part_hull_plate`, 1); a COMPARTMENT, a `SeamRecord` (subject_kind 4) or an OPENING (subject_kind 5) -> (`NONE`, 0).

### F-4 `part_cost`

The part_cost formula is defined as:

`parts_consumed(job) = parts_per_full if the first productive channel step of an accepted FULL job is applied, else 0` ; `stock_after = stock_before - parts_consumed` ; permitted only if `stock_before >= parts_per_full` (Interaction's `validate`) ; `stock_visible = stock_before - parts_per_full` from the Applied FULL begin tick while the record is RESERVED or PAID (released, with no timer, if the channel ends early with no credited block or on an OUTCOME; registry: `full_job_charge`, Interaction's) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* ; `parts_consumed` above is the PAID amount

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `parts_per_full` | int | 1..3 | F-3 | Cost of one full job |
| `stock_before` | int | 0..`stack_max` x stacks | holder (Suit's block) | Count of the required part def held |
| first productive step | event | — | Interaction | The first step in which `hp_cap` or `hp` rises (IDB-20) |

**Output Range:** `parts_consumed` is 0 for every PATCH [REG:6033], for a job rejected before its first applied step, and for a FULL on a subject already at `hp_cap` = `HP_FULL` with `hp` = `hp_cap` (`PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)`); otherwise `parts_per_full`. A RESERVED part that is never PAID returns to the stock at `until`, so `parts_consumed` 0 can still show a visible dip for one channel. `stock_after >= 0` always.
**Example:** a FULL repair of the reactor (`parts_per_full` 2) with 3 `part_machine_kit` held (`stack_max` 2, so stacks of 2 and 1): from the Applied begin tick the visible stock reads 1 (2 kits RESERVED, `full_job_charge`); the first productive step pays them, leaving 1; with no credited block (an early end or an OUTCOME) they are released, with no timer. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* A PATCH on the same reactor spends 0. If the channel is interrupted after the first step the part stays spent and the raised `hp` stays (IDB-20 (3)).

### F-5 `salvage_yield`

The salvage_yield formula is defined as:

`salvage_yield(rec) = [] if rec.state in {ABSENT, SALVAGED} ; [ItemInstance(d.repair_part_id, d.scrap_count, 0, 0, 0, 0)] if rec.state = DESTROYED or rec.hp = 0 ; [ItemInstance(rec.def_id, 1, rec.hp, rec.hp_cap, patched_out, 0)] otherwise (rec.state in {OK, DEGRADED, FAILED} and rec.hp >= 1)` , where `d = def(rec.def_id)` and `patched_out := 0 if rec.hp_cap = HP_FULL else rec.patched_at_step` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: the branch is keyed on `hp`, not on `state` alone, because the Graph lets a node sit FAILED at `hp` 0 while the warning gate is closed (Graph state-transition table, FAILED to DESTROYED row), and the old state-keyed form would have yielded a module at `condition_bp` 0, which IDB-15 and AC-82 refuse at restore. Cutting an `hp`-0 FAILED node therefore scraps it. The `patched_out` normalisation is also new: the Graph's M7 FULL restores `hp_cap` to `HP_FULL` but does not clear `patched_at_step`, so the old pass-through produced `{cap_bp 10000, patched_at_step > 0}`, which IDB-15 also refuses; F-5 now writes the canonical zero itself, so the instance never depends on the Graph clearing the field. Both are normal-play paths, not edge cases.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `rec.def_id` | string | catalogue | `SalvageRecord` returned by the **applied** M6 [LSG:185] | The salvaged module's def |
| `rec.hp` | int bp | 0..`HP_FULL` | `SalvageRecord` | Condition at the cut |
| `rec.hp_cap` | int bp | `HP_FULL`, or `1..HP_FULL-1` for a patched module (no tie to the current `PATCH_CAP_BP`) | `SalvageRecord` field returned by M6 (LS-18 M6; IDB-18, settled) | The module's cap at the cut *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional extension" removed)* |
| `rec.patched_at_step` | int | 0, or the Graph's absolute `step_no` (at least 1) | `SalvageRecord` field returned by M6 (LS-18 M6; IDB-18, settled) | The decay clock's origin. **Not guaranteed 0 at `HP_FULL` by the Graph:** M7 FULL restores `hp_cap` but does not clear this field, so a record can read `{hp_cap HP_FULL, patched_at_step > 0}`; F-5 maps it to 0 (`patched_out`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional extension" removed; Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: the earlier note "0 when `rec.hp_cap` is `HP_FULL`" assumed a Graph rule that does not exist; the request that M7 FULL clear the field at the source is Open Question 46)* |
| `rec.state` | enum | OK, DEGRADED, FAILED, DESTROYED | `SalvageRecord` | ABSENT and SALVAGED are rejected by M6 before this runs; called directly as a pure query on either, the function returns an empty list (orchestrator ruling, QA B5) |
| `d.scrap_count` | int | exactly 1 (boot-asserted: a count of 0 would yield an illegal count-0 instance) | module def [PH] | Parts a DESTROYED module breaks down to *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pinned to 1)* |
| `HP_FULL` | int bp | 10000 | registry [REG:5863-5917] | `cap_bp` of an un-patched salvaged module (IDB-18); the exclusive upper bound of a patched module's `cap_bp` |

**Output Range:** exactly one `ItemInstance` for a record in OK, DEGRADED, FAILED or DESTROYED (an empty list for ABSENT or SALVAGED); `condition_bp` in 1..`cap_bp` for a module instance, with canonical zeros for a part instance (IDB-15); a module instance has `patched_at_step` 0 exactly when `cap_bp` is `HP_FULL`, whatever the record held, and a record with `hp` 0 yields the part, never a module *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*. No randomness in the MVP (the reserved `"item.yield"` draw has no MVP effect).
**Example:** derelict A's scrubber, FAILED at `hp` 2000 [LSG:228] -> one `mod_scrubber` instance with `condition_bp` 2000. A healthy `mod_battery_cell` at `hp` 10000 -> one `mod_battery_cell` at `condition_bp` 10000. A DESTROYED `mod_breaker_junction` -> one `part_cable_splice`, no module. The same scrubber at `hp` 0 -> one `part_machine_kit`, **whether its state is DESTROYED or FAILED** (a FAILED node at `hp` 0, legal while the Graph's warning gate is closed, is scrapped by the cut). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)* A scrubber that was patched, then FULL-repaired (`hp_cap` `HP_FULL`, `hp` 10000, and the Graph left `patched_at_step` S) -> one `mod_scrubber` at `condition_bp` 10000, `cap_bp` 10000 and `patched_at_step` **0** (`patched_out`); its bytes equal a never-patched scrubber at the same `hp`. A patched scrubber `{hp 5800, hp_cap 6000, patched_at_step S, DEGRADED}` -> one `mod_scrubber` at `condition_bp` 5800, `cap_bp` 6000 and `patched_at_step` S (the patch survives the cut).

### F-6 `ports_match` and `compatible_defs`

The ports_match formula is defined as:

`ports_match(d, h) = len(d.ports) == len(h.ports) and for every i: d.ports[i].port_type_id == h.ports[i]` , both ordered by `local_index` ; `compatible_defs(h) = [d for d in defs_of_class(MOD) if d.node_kind in h.accepted_kinds and (h.accepted_subtypes is empty or d.subtype_id in h.accepted_subtypes) and ports_match(d, h)]`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `d.ports` | list of `(port_type_id, cap, local_index)` | 1..`MAX_PORTS_PER_DEF` 8 | def (IDB-7) | Declared ports |
| `h.ports` | list of port_type_id | 1..8 | Layout hardpoint declaration (Layout designed: `ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md`) | What the slot exposes |
| `h.accepted_kinds` | set of int | LS-3 node-kind ids | Layout (designed, LY-20) | Node kinds the slot takes |
| `h.accepted_subtypes` | set of int | LOAD subtype ids, or empty | Layout (designed, DA-12 carries it on LOAD hardpoints) | Needed because `mod_heater`, `mod_lamp` have the same port signature |

**Output Range:** boolean / a list ascending by `type_id`. Caps are not compared (the def owns the cap). The Graph does not define the match function [LSG:184 says only "ports match the slot's hardpoint"]; this is a proposal for the Graph owner and Layout to confirm. An empty result is a content-lint finding [LSG:774], printed with the distinct reason (`STORE-STORE`, `SRC-SRC`, `SNK-SNK`).
**Example:** a scrubber hardpoint `{202, 101, 902}` with kinds {20}: `mod_scrubber` -> true. A reservoir hardpoint `{303}`: `mod_scrubber` -> false (the Graph would answer `PORT_INCOMPAT`). A LOAD hardpoint `{202, 902}` with kinds {13} and no subtype filter -> both `mod_heater` and `mod_lamp` match, so a heater could be offered to a lamp socket; with `accepted_subtypes` {2} only `mod_lamp` does. A door-motor hardpoint `{202}` kinds {13}, subtypes {3} -> `mod_door_motor` only.

### F-7 Glowstick parameters and the curve contract

The glowstick_life formula is defined as:

`LIFE_TICKS = GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S * TICK_RATE` ; `RADIUS_PX = int(round(GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES * TILE_PX))` ; `WANE_TICKS = floor(LIFE_TICKS * GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM / 1000)` ; `f(rem) = 1000 if rem >= WANE_TICKS else GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM + floor((1000 - GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM) * rem / WANE_TICKS)` ; `reach_px(rem) = floor(RADIUS_PX * f(rem) / 1000)` ; `rem_at_landing = LIFE_TICKS - THROW_ARC_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` | int s | 60-300, default 150 | data [PH] ★ | Seconds of light, counted from the throw tick |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 | registry (locked) | Ticks per second |
| `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` | number | 1.5-3, default 2; strictly below `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3 [PCI:779] | data [PH] ★ | Radius at full life; converted once at boot |
| `TILE_PX` | int | 48 | registry (locked) | Base pixels per tile |
| `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` | int permille | 200-1000, default 400 | data [PH] ★ | Share of total life remaining at which waning starts (1000 = linear from the throw) |
| `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` | int permille | 100-500, default 250 | data [PH] ★ | Reach fraction at the last tick |
| `rem` | int ticks | 0..`LIFE_TICKS` | Emitter State's record (`life_remaining_ticks`) | Life remaining |
| `THROW_ARC_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 | Controller [PCI:611] | Flight time; the stick has burned that long before it lands |

**Output Range:** `LIFE_TICKS` 3600..18000; `f` in `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM`..1000, monotone non-increasing as `rem` falls (never rises, never pulses); `reach_px` from `RADIUS_PX` down to the floor reach, then the emitter is removed at `rem` 0. Emitter State owns the evaluation (curve id `WANE_LINEAR_25`, EM-15 [EM:196]); this formula is the parameter contract, and Emitter State's D.9 [EM:506] reproduces it tick for tick (its example: `r` 1800 gives `f` 625 and reach 60; `r` 1 gives reach 24 [EM:520]). Build assertions: `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES < THROW_RANGE_TILES`; `WANE_TICKS >= 1`. `LIFE_TICKS` is a derivation, not a registry constant.
**Example:** `LIFE_TICKS` = 150 x 60 = 9000; `RADIUS_PX` = 2 x 48 = 96; `WANE_TICKS` = floor(9000 x 400 / 1000) = 3600, so the pool is steady for the first 5400 ticks (90 s) after the throw. At `rem` 1800: `f` = 250 + floor(750 x 1800 / 3600) = 625 and `reach_px` = floor(96 x 625 / 1000) = 60 px. At `rem` 3600 the formula is continuous (250 + 750 = 1000). At landing `rem_at_landing` = 9000 - 30 = 8970 (Interaction passes it, EM-15). Floor reach = floor(96 x 250 / 1000) = 24 px, one sixteenth of the full pool's area, which is why Emitter State carries `reach_px` and the shape rather than a radius alone. With `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` 1000 the curve is linear from the throw.

### F-8 Id-ledger and bijection assertion

The id_ledger formula is defined as:

`ledger_ok = (for all d1 != d2: type_id(d1) != type_id(d2) and def_id(d1) != def_id(d2)) and (for all d: floor(type_id(d) / 1000) * 1000 == base(class_of_prefix(d.def_id)) and type_id(d) != base(class_of_prefix(d.def_id))) and (for all d: type_id(d) not in retired_ids) and (for all (id, t) in ids.lock: id in catalogue and catalogue[id].type_id == t, or t in retired_ids, or (id, t) is dormant) and (no def in the catalogue uses the `def_id` or `type_id` of a dormant pair under a different pair) and (count of defs per class <= 999)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `type_id` | int | 1001..9999 (the six class bases are reserved) | def (IDB-2) | In-memory key |
| `base(class)` | int | 1000, 2000, 3000, 4000, 5000, 9000 | IDB-2 | Class range base |
| `retired_ids` | set of int | — | `meta.json` | Ids never to be reused |
| `ids.lock` | map def_id -> type_id | — | `ids.lock.json` (IDB-4) | Every pair ever shipped, plus the `dormant` reserved pairs *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

**Output Range:** boolean; false raises `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(class, id)` naming the offending id (a bad alias raises `ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS(def_id)`).
**Example:** `mod_scrubber` has prefix `mod`, so its `type_id` must lie in 1000..1999: 1001 passes. Adding a def `mod_gasket` (illustrative, not in the catalogue) with `type_id` 1001 collides with `mod_scrubber` -> `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(mod, 1001)`. Renumbering `mod_scrubber` to 1020 disagrees with the ledger -> refused. A module given `type_id` 4001 fails the range clause. The next free module id is 1014. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: the ledger holds `tool_suppressant` 2004 as `dormant`; a catalogue that omits it passes, and a catalogue that gives `type_id` 2004 to a different def fails `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(tool, 2004)`.)*

### F-9 `catalogue_version` bump rule

The catalogue_version_bump formula is defined as:

`catalogue_version' = catalogue_version + 1 if the structural diff between the last released catalogue and this one is non-empty, else catalogue_version` , where structural = {a def removed ; a `def_id` renamed ; a field added to, removed from or retyped in the schema ; a `type_id` range change}

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `catalogue_version` | int | 1..2^31 | `meta.json`; offered to Save/Load's header [SL:159] | Starts at 1; monotone |
| structural diff | set | — | CI tooling against the last released catalogue | Computed from git |

**Output Range:** a monotone non-decreasing integer. **Not structural** (no bump): a value tuned within its type (`mass_g`, `req_w`, a glowstick number, a port `cap`), an edit to a snapshotted-at-install field such as `node_kind` or `ports[]` (a saved node keeps its old value until it is salvaged and reinstalled, IDB-6; a reinstall that no longer fits is caught by the hardpoint lint and `PORT_INCOMPAT`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `repair_part_id` is removed from this list; IDB-6 classes it as **read live** because `repair_part_for` reads the def, so an edit to it changes the repair cost of a node in an existing save at once; it is still not structural (no bump), but the structural-diff tool prints it as a **save-affecting edit**, exactly like a lowered `stock_cap`, so the author knows saved games change the part they ask for)*, and the addition of a new def (an old save cannot name it). `catalogue_hash` is diagnostic and never decides a load. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a CI note, not a bump: lowering any `stock_cap` is a **save-affecting edit** — the restore rule of IDB-6 moves the excess stock to `discarded_*` and logs it — and the structural diff tool prints it so the author knows a saved game will lose stock.)*
**Example:** tuning `mod_scrubber.mass_g` 40000 -> 38000 keeps version 1. Adding `mod_door_motor` keeps the version (an addition). Renaming `part_pipe_fitting` to `part_pipe_clamp` (illustrative, not in the catalogue) bumps to 2 and needs `renamed_from: ["part_pipe_fitting"]`. Removing `tool_scanner` bumps to 2 and needs `replaced_by` or restore fails loudly.

### F-10 Junk off-band rule

The junk_tell formula is defined as:

`tell_pm(junk, lure) = floor(abs(m_junk - m_lure) * 1000 / m_lure)` ; the lint requires `tell_pm >= WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` **and** `weight_class(m_junk) != weight_class(m_lure)` **and** `margin_ok(m_junk)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `m_junk` | int g | 1..500000 | the junk def's `mass_g` | The decoy's true mass |
| `m_lure` | int g | 1..500000 | the lure band's `mid_g`, from the Sanity-owned `lure_bands.json` (registry; SN-21; IDB-22) | The mass the lie dresses it as |
| `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` | int permille | 200-600, default 300 | data [PH] ★ | Minimum relative gap |

**Output Range:** `tell_pm` 0..unbounded; pass iff all three clauses hold. The class clause exists because the Suit's cue resolves classes, not grams (IDB-22). Vertical slice only (no junk def ships in the MVP); a junk def that fails is not releasable.
**Example:** lure `mod_scrubber` 40000 g (HEAVY). A junk at 21000 g: `tell_pm` = floor(19000 x 1000 / 40000) = 475, class MEDIUM != HEAVY, margin ok (distance to 25000 is 4000 >= 2500) -> pass. A junk at 30000 g: `tell_pm` 250 < 300 and the same class -> fail. A junk at 26000 g: `tell_pm` 350 passes the gap but is HEAVY like the lure -> fail on the class clause (a 14 kg difference gives the same body cue).

### F-11 `part_cover` (authoring lint)

The part_cover formula is defined as:

`demand(f, R) = sum of n over the authored-fault subjects s in the reachable set R that are FULL-repairable with repair_part_for(s) = (f, n)` (**scope** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: an authored fault is a subject Derelict Authoring places already damaged, the eight MVP faults [LSG:228]; an installable module that a player later patches, such as the installable `scr_a`, and the home machines are NOT counted, because their FULL repair is a player-made demand, not an authored one; so `part_machine_kit` has demand 0 by scope and its FULL-repair cost is reported separately in the scope note below), excluding any subject whose part is `NONE` and any subject whose fix is a placed spare module ; `supply(f, R) = loose placements of family f in R + one per authored DESTROYED module node of family f in R` ; `part_cover_pm(f, R) = floor(1000 * supply(f, R) / demand(f, R))`, or `PART_COVER_UNDEFINED` (-1) when `demand(f, R)` is 0 ; the aggregate `part_cover_pm(R) = floor(1000 * sum_f min(supply(f, R), demand(f, R)) / sum_f demand(f, R))` (**each family is capped at its own demand**, so a surplus in one family cannot hide a shortage in another; `PART_COVER_UNDEFINED` when `sum_f demand` is 0) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the uncapped sum let a spare hull plate make duct and pipe shortages read as 500 "in band")* ; advisory band `PART_COVER_MIN_PM <= part_cover_pm <= PART_COVER_MAX_PM`, evaluated per family with `demand(f, R) >= 2` and for the aggregate ; **the per-family band check applies only to a family with `demand(f, R) >= 2`; a family with demand 1 can only read 0 (`PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED`) or >= 1000 and is printed, not band-checked; the aggregate is always band-checked** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix), GAP: the owner adds the exemption, because without it every authored family with a single fault would warn `PART_COVER_BAND` forever and the shipped ledger could never be clean)* ; warning `PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED(f, R)` when `demand(f, R) > 0` and `supply(f, R) = 0` (registry: `part_cover_pm`, requested 2026-10-27, expression revised by the design review; this GDD is its source, Derelict Authoring reads it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier type-blind form divided parts by repairs, so a 714 permille "in band" hid a machine-kit cover of 0, the reactor's 2-part cost, and subjects that cannot take a part at all)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `f` | family | `part_duct_coupling`, `part_cable_splice`, `part_pipe_fitting`, `part_hull_plate`, `part_machine_kit` | IDB-13 | A part family |
| `R` | set of sections | the cumulative loaded set after sortie k (Derelict Authoring's access order, D.4; Boarding's loaded sets S0 to S4) | Authoring | The reachable sortie set |
| `supply(f, R)` | int parts | 0.. | Derelict Authoring's placements plus one per DESTROYED module node (`scrap_count` pinned to 1); `supply_max(f, R)` is also printed: modules in R of family f that a player could destroy on purpose (not added to `supply`) | Finite parts of family f in R |
| `demand(f, R)` | int parts | 0.. | counted from the FULL-repairable subjects in R, **in parts** (the reactor counts 2), excluding subjects with `repair_part_for` `NONE` (the O2 seam record) and subjects whose fix is a placed spare module (the broken junction, covered in kind by the spare breaker) | Parts a diligent player could spend on family f |
| `PART_COVER_MIN_PM` | int permille | 400-600, default 500 | data [PH] | Below it permanent fixes feel out of reach |
| `PART_COVER_MAX_PM` | int permille | 700-900, default 800 | data [PH] | Above it every chronic fault is solved and nothing pushes inward |

**Output Range:** permille, 0..unbounded, or `PART_COVER_UNDEFINED` (reported, never divided); the per-family band check applies only to a family with `demand(f, R) >= 2` (a demand-1 family reads 0 or >= 1000 and is printed, not band-checked) and the aggregate is always band-checked *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the band is advisory (a warning, not a CI failure), as is `PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED` (its id is proposed to Derelict Authoring's `content_lint_ids`, which owns lint ids). A design-time check, never a runtime field. An early reachable set (after sortie 1) is expected to warn: Derelict Authoring deliberately places the pipe and duct parts on the second sortie (DA-25), so the lint reports that as a logged "uncovered by choice" entry, not a defect.
**Example (MVP numbers, Derelict Authoring's ledger DA-25; Authoring owns the real counts):** of the eight MVP faults [LSG:228], the pump stall is STARVE and takes no part, the reactor SCRAM is cleared by a reset, the O2 seam is a `SeamRecord` (`repair_part_for` `NONE`) and the broken junction is covered in kind by the spare `mod_breaker_junction`; that leaves five FULL-repairable, part-taking subjects at one part each: the pinhole DUCT (`part_duct_coupling`), the overloaded CABLE (`part_cable_splice`), the coolant PIPE and the fuel PIPE (`part_pipe_fitting` x 2) and the fatigued HULL_SEGMENT (`part_hull_plate`): demand 5 parts. Supply (all sections reachable; 4 loose parts, Derelict Authoring's tightened ledger DA-25, delegated ruling G2) is duct 1, cable 1, pipe 1, hull 1, kit 0 = 4 against demand 5 (duct 1, cable 1, pipe 2, hull 1). Per family: duct 1000, cable 1000 and hull 1000 (demand 1, printed, not band-checked), pipe floor(1000 x 1 / 2) = 500 (in band, at its lower edge); `part_machine_kit` demand 0 and supply 0 -> `PART_COVER_UNDEFINED`, no warning; aggregate floor(1000 x (1 + 1 + 1 + 1) / 5) = **800, inside the band at its top edge** (the cap changes nothing here: no family has surplus). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* **Scope note:** the demand above is the authored faults only. The installable `scr_a` (DA-47 supplies its home slot) and the home machines are not counted; a FULL repair of a patched scrubber needs `part_machine_kit`, which the MVP never places, so the permanent fix of a scrubber is unreachable in the MVP by content and `part_machine_kit` reads `PART_COVER_UNDEFINED` (0 of 0) by scope rather than `PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED`; whether that is intended scarcity or a content gap is Open Question 48, and F-14 is computed at the patch cap for that reason. The real MVP cover is complete in every family except pipe, where one fitting serves two pipe faults; the old type-blind figure of 714 (demand 7 counting the seam and the junction, parts divided by repairs) hid; whether 100 percent family cover is intended (Open Question 38) is resolved 2026-10-28 by delegated ruling G2: Derelict Authoring removed one `part_pipe_fitting` and the ledger now reads 800. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **`supply_max` by family, printed beside `supply`:** it counts the modules of each family a player could destroy on purpose. `part_machine_kit`: the ten machine defs placed in R (scrubbers, the reactor, batteries, pumps, radiators, reservoirs, tanks, vents, heaters); `part_cable_splice`: every `mod_lamp`, `mod_door_motor` and `mod_breaker_junction` placed in R, which is the large family (Derelict Authoring counts 26 LOAD nodes world-wide, lamps, the beacon, door motors and heaters, and the heaters scrap to machine kits, so the cable-splice `supply_max` is on the order of twenty before breaker junctions are added; the lint prints the exact figure) against an authored cable demand of 1 and an authored cable supply of 1. So the single placed splice is not the only splice in the world: a player who destroys a lamp on purpose holds a second. Whether lamps should scrap to a part at all is Open Question 39 (creative direction, not decided here); until it is, the anchor beat's "earned" reading holds for the *placed* splice only. After sortie 1 only (home plus derelict A): demand is duct 1, cable 1, pipe 2 (the home faults) = 4; supply is cable 1 and hull 1 (the plate has no demand until derelict B is welded); `PART_TYPE_UNSUPPLIED` fires for duct and pipe, the intended scarcity of DA-25 ("the first fault cannot be fully repaired until the second sortie"). **Capped aggregate: floor(1000 x (min(1,1) + min(1,0)) / 4) = 250, below the band**, where the old uncapped sum read floor(1000 x 2 / 4) = 500 "in band" because the unusable plate stood in for the missing duct and pipe parts *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Per-set capped aggregates of the MVP ledger *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: S0 0, S1 250 (as above), S2 and S3 400, S4 800. Generic capped aggregates (no surplus in any family; demand 5 in one family): supply 3 of 5 -> 600, in band; 4 of 5 -> 800, in band (the edge); 2 of 5 -> 400, below 500. (orchestrator ruling: IDB-29 reconciled the specialists' bands of 750-1000, 500-750 and 500-800 to 500-800.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*

### F-12 Port-cap lint (binding): severed leak and seam capacity

The port_cap_lint formula is defined as:

`severed_leak(p) = floor(p.cap * OPEN_END_RATE_PM / 1000)` per step ; `seam_cap_floor(p) = the Graph's cap_seam (D.14) called with cap_a = cap_b = p.cap at q = Q_MIN` (the lowest weld quality Assembly can store; the Graph clamps `q` to at least 400, so the earlier `q = 0` was unreachable and only made the floor 50 percent instead of 70) ; `rated_flow(p)` is read from the table in Variables and is **undefined** where no upstream value exists ; a fluid port passes iff `severed_leak(p) >= 1` and `seam_cap_floor(p) >= 1` and, **where `rated_flow(p)` is defined**, `p.cap >= rated_flow(p)`, and, for loop 1, `severed_leak(p) * 1000 <= SEVER_O2_LOUD_MAX_PM * DESTROYED_DUCT_LEAK` ; the lint prints `ratio_pm(p) = floor(seam_cap_floor(p) * 1000 / rated_flow(p))` (or `n/a` where `rated_flow` is undefined) rather than a bare boolean *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a per-port rated-flow column typed by the same author as the cap would make the lint circular, because IDB-7 (b) already defined the cap as the rated flow; the lint now reads rated flow only from an upstream value, and prints a ratio)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `p.cap` | int | loop unit per step (mGU for loop 1, mL for loops 3 and 4) | def port `(port_type_id, cap, local_index)` (IDB-7) | A fluid port's declared rated flow |
| `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` | int permille | 500 (Graph range 100-1000); **the shipped roster passes F-12 only inside 500-625** | Graph tuning [LSG:960] | Share of a port's cap a SEVERED stub leaks per step until `cap_port`; read, never re-valued. The window: at most 625 because the tank's loudness bound `floor(1200 x r / 1000) <= 750` fails above it, and at least 500 because the reactor's 402 stub `floor(2 x r / 1000) >= 1` floors to 0 below it. **As a formula, so it survives a knob edit** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the window held only at the default knob values)*: `ceil(1000 / min over fluid ports of p.cap) <= r <= floor(SEVER_O2_LOUD_MAX_PM x DESTROYED_DUCT_LEAK / cap_103)`, that is `ceil(1000 / 2) = 500` and `floor(1500 x 500 / 1200) = 625` at the defaults; the lint computes both ends and prints them, and a Graph retune of `r` or a knob edit that empties the window fails the lint. A Graph retune outside 500-625 fails the lint and must signal the Item DB (Open Question 41; the registry `referenced_by` of `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` already lists this GDD, and the window is requested as an annotation) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| D.14 `cap_seam` | int | 70-99.5 percent of the lesser cap (70 at `Q_MIN` 400) | Graph [LSG:654, 666, 674] | The seam capacity; the lint **calls** it (the Graph publishes it as callable at data load) and never restates it |
| `rated_flow(p)` | int, or undefined | per port, from the table below | **101 = `R_SCR`** (400 mGU/step, Graph D.5 [LSG:395]); **104 = `R_VENT`** (default 600, Graph [LSG:928]); **402 = the reactor burn converted to integer units**: 0.3 mL/step at 2500 W [LSG:958], rounded **up** to 1 mL/step; **103 = `MAX_CREW` x `R_BREATH`** = 4 x 300 = 1200 mGU/step, the four-crew breathing draw (there is no `MAX_CREW` registry entry yet; 4 is the concept's crew ceiling, [PH]; `MAX_CREW` now has a Tuning Knobs row and a registry candidate, Open Question 51, because it drives this BLOCKING clause *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the Graph publishes no rated flow for a tank, so the Item DB derives one from demand; **joint range with `R_BREATH`**: the legal port-103 cap range is `MAX_CREW x R_BREATH` up to the loudness ceiling 1500, so at `MAX_CREW` 4 an `R_BREATH` above 375 admits no legal cap and fails the lint; the Suit-owned `R_BREATH` range 100-600 is therefore usable here only as 100-375, a note routed to Suit and the registry); **301, 302, 303, 304 and 403 have no rated flow** — their caps (40 mL/step and 5 mL/step) are [PH] throughput or loudness choices, not Graph facts, and a coolant circulation figure in mL/step (the Graph's D.8 `circ` is a permille) is requested of the Graph owner (Open Question 33); 901-903 and the power ports are exempt | What the port must at least carry, where something upstream says so |
| `DESTROYED_DUCT_LEAK` | int mGU/step | 500 | Graph [LSG:772] | The reference for how loud a cut O2 port may be; read, never re-valued |
| `SEVER_O2_LOUD_MAX_PM` | int permille | **1200-2000**, default 1500 (the old floor of 1000 failed the shipped tank's own 600 mGU/step stub: 600 x 1000 > 1000 x 500) | data [PH] | Loudest allowed O2 stub, as a share of a DESTROYED duct *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; registry range change requested)* |

**Output Range:** a pass or fail per fluid port plus three printed numbers (the severed leak, the seam capacity floor and `ratio_pm`, or `n/a`). Blocking in CI (IDB-7 (f), IDB-30). Power ports 201-204 are exempt (power has no inventory [LSG:172]); the non-loop couplings 901-903 carry cap 0 and are exempt; 301 and 302 are loop-3 fluid ports and are checked.
**Example:** `mod_scrubber` 101 cap 400: leak floor(400 x 500 / 1000) = 200 mGU/step, D.14 at `Q_MIN` returns floor(400 x 700 / 1000) = 280, cap 400 >= `R_SCR` 400, pass, `ratio_pm` 700. `mod_o2_tank` 103 at cap 1200: leak 600, bound 750 (1500 x 500 / 1000), cap 1200 >= `MAX_CREW` 4 x `R_BREATH` 300 = 1200, seam floor 840, `ratio_pm` 700, pass; at the old cap 3000 the leak is 1500 > 750, **fail** (three times a DESTROYED duct); at 1000 the rated-flow clause fails (1000 < 1200). `mod_reactor_cell` 402 at cap 1: leak floor(1 x 500 / 1000) = 0 and the seam floor is 0, **fail** (a silent fuel-line cut and a seam that carries nothing); at cap 2: leak 1 mL/step, seam floor floor(2 x 700 / 1000) = 1, rated flow 1 (0.3 mL/step rounded up), `ratio_pm` 1000, pass. `mod_vent` 104 cap 600: leak 300, seam 420, `ratio_pm` 700, pass; with `R_VENT` retuned to 800 the cap 600 < 800 fails until the cap is raised. `mod_reactor_cell` 301, `mod_radiator` 302, `mod_coolant_pump` 304, `mod_coolant_reservoir` 303 (cap 40) and `mod_fuel_tank` 403 (cap 5): no rated flow, `ratio_pm` `n/a`; leak 20 mL/step (301, 302, 303, 304) and 2 (403), seam floors 28 and 3, pass; at cap 0 each would fail (leak 0, seam floor 0: a silent cut and a zero-capacity seam). **A retune of `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` to 700** makes the 103 stub 840 > 750, **fail**; to 400 makes the 402 stub floor(2 x 400 / 1000) = 0, **fail**.

### F-13 Haul cost (mass-independent by lock)

The haul_cost formula is defined as:

`ticks_carry = D_tiles * TILE_PX / V_CARRY` ; `noise_carry = ticks_carry * NOISE_PER_TICK_carry` ; `ticks_empty = D_tiles * TILE_PX / V_RUN` ; `noise_empty = ticks_empty * NOISE_PER_TICK_run` ; and, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, with `heavy_stowed` true when the holder has any STOWED instance of `weight_class` HEAVY (IDB-13, D18): `ticks_empty_hs = D_tiles * TILE_PX / V_WALK` ; `noise_empty_hs = ticks_empty_hs * NOISE_PER_TICK_walk` ; `ticks_carry` and `noise_carry` are unchanged by `heavy_stowed` (the carry gait is already `V_CARRY`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D_tiles` | int | 1..about 60 | map | One-way distance in tiles |
| `TILE_PX` | int | 48 | registry (locked) | Base pixels per tile |
| `V_CARRY` | int px/tick | 1 | registry [REG:5338-5356] (locked) | Flat carry speed; **not** scaled by mass |
| `V_RUN` | int px/tick | 3 | registry [REG:5317-5337] (owner: Player Controller & Input, [PCI:238]); locked | Empty run speed |
| `V_WALK` | int px/tick | 2 | registry (owner: Player Controller & Input); locked | Walk speed, the empty-handed gait while a HEAVY item is stowed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `NOISE_PER_TICK` | int | walk 1, carry 1, run 4 | registry [REG:5442-5457] (locked) | Attention units per tick *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

**Output Range:** ticks 48..2880 for D 1..60 carrying; mass appears nowhere in the expression, which is the point: the Item DB cannot make a heavy thing slower or louder without a registry conflict. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: this expression covers the single carried module; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: a stowed HEAVY item (the 30 kg plate) no longer costs nothing. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): option (a) of D18, Open Question 32: while any HEAVY item is stowed, `run` is disabled, with no grams-based term and `V_CARRY` and `NOISE_PER_TICK` locked. The plate is therefore a priced second load, not a free second slot. AC-46 asserts the values derived below.)*

**Derivation of the plate's price (constraint and arithmetic).** *Constraint:* the stowed plate must cost something a regression test can detect, without a mass term and without touching `V_CARRY` or `NOISE_PER_TICK`. The only free variable the ruling leaves is the empty-handed gait. With the plate stowed the empty gait drops from `V_RUN` 3 to `V_WALK` 2 px/tick, so the added time per tile is 48/2 - 48/3 = 24 - 16 = 8 ticks, a factor of 1.5. *Noise:* walking is quieter than running (1 against 4 per tick, so 24 against 64 per tile), so the plate does not add noise; it lengthens the exposure on the clock (the cost the player feels is time and, through Attention's time-based terms, the exposure that time carries), and Attention's X3 carry intake (keyed on HEAVY) is the route by which the burden becomes a legible stimulus, not this GDD. *While a module is carried* the gait is already `V_CARRY` 1, so the plate adds nothing: the price falls only on the empty-handed legs, which is the leg on which the free slot was free.

**Example (plate stowed, 30 tiles, empty hands):** `ticks_empty_hs` = 30 x 48 / 2 = 720 ticks (12 s) and `noise_empty_hs` = 720 x 1 = 720; the same 30 tiles without the plate: `ticks_empty` = 480 ticks (8 s) and `noise_empty` = 1920. The plate costs +240 ticks (+50 percent) on a 30-tile empty leg and 1200 fewer noise units. With a carried `mod_reactor_cell` and the plate stowed: 1440 ticks and 1440 noise, identical to the carry without the plate. Passes the constraint: a regression that re-enables run changes 720 to 480. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Exact values with the run ramp:** the Controller's `RUN_RAMP_TICKS` 6 starts every run at `V_WALK` for 6 ticks (12 px), so the unplated 30-tile run is 6 + (1440 - 12) / 3 = **482 ticks** and 6 x 1 + 476 x 4 = **1910 noise units**; the figures 480 and 1920 above are the idealised no-ramp values, and the plate's exact cost on that leg is 720 - 482 = +238 ticks.

**Pursuit exposure: what the plate actually costs** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Walking is always available, so the noise column understates the plate. The price is the **escape gait**: with a plate stowed the holder cannot reach `V_RUN`, so a pursuer whose speed `v_p` (px/tick, Horror Entities' number, not valued here) satisfies `V_WALK` <= `v_p` < `V_RUN` can be outrun by an unplated holder and cannot be outrun by a plated one; over a leg of `D` tiles a plated holder is exposed for `8 x D` extra ticks (48/2 - 48/3 per tile, ramp aside) and a pursuer covers `v_p x 8 x D` px more ground in that time. Over the 30-tile reference leg that is 240 extra ticks of exposure. The plate is therefore **slow to flee**, not loud: the MDA claim is reworded accordingly. **Attention C33 answered (this GDD's half)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R5)*: Resolved 2026-10-27 — delegated ruling R5 (re-review; user delegated to best judgment): a stowed HEAVY item adds **no** `BURDEN` push. The burden is keyed on `carry_ref` (X3) and on salvage carry only (delegated ruling R5), a stowed plate is not a `carry_ref` carry, and charging it twice (burden for the gait it already lost) would double-price one cost; Attention keeps C33 closed on that reading and its refresh is owed no further change by the Item DB. **Single implementation:** the HEAVY-stowed test is Suit's `stowed_heavy(player_id)` (SE-41, which excludes units RESERVED by a FULL job and is derived on read), not a per-def check: the rule reads "any **unreserved** STOWED instance of `weight_class` HEAVY". A reserved plate (a FULL job holds it) therefore produces no run toggle, and no visible switch occurs when the reservation begins or is released early *(CD agreed with the gameplay-programmer: the earlier alternative rules would duplicate Suit)*. The Item DB's contribution is the class (`weight_class` HEAVY, F-1), Suit's is the sum and the reservation. **Reconciliation** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: D18's user wording is "any HEAVY item is stowed", Suit's `stowed_heavy` is sum-based (its stowed weight class is HEAVY), and IDB-13 was per-def. Under G1 the publisher's reading is canonical, so Suit's `stowed_heavy` is the implementation. In the MVP the readings coincide: the plate is the only HEAVY def, and the largest ordinary stack without it is 15,200 g, below `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` (AC-46 (iv)). The divergence case (a post-MVP stowed sum at or above `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` with no plate) is folded into Open Question 35 (Suit's class margin and hysteresis).
**Example:** a 30-tile trip carrying `mod_reactor_cell` (90000 g): `ticks_carry` = 30 x 48 / 1 = 1440 ticks (24 s) and `noise_carry` 1440; the same trip carrying `curio_data_core` (6000 g) is identical. The empty run out is 1440 / 3 = 480 ticks (8 s) and 1920 noise units; the round trip is 1920 ticks (32 s) and 3360 noise. Walking the same 30 tiles takes 720 ticks, so carrying costs twice the time and, per tile, twice the noise (48 vs 24).

### F-14 Scrubber margin ladder (why a part is worth a sortie)

The scrubber_margin formula is defined as:

`margin(hp, N) = out_scr(hp) - N * R_BREATH` where `out_scr(hp)` is the Graph's D.5 evaluated at `sat` 1000 with `eff` from D.4 (cited, not restated) ; `scrubbers_to_hold(N, hp) = ceil(N * R_BREATH / out_scr(hp))` (the least count with margin >= 0) ; `scrubbers_with_margin(N, hp) = floor(N * R_BREATH / out_scr(hp)) + 1` (the least count with margin > 0, **strict-margin semantics, the design's standard**, because a margin of exactly zero "loses the room" at any wear: the Graph's own tuning note [LSG:927]) ; both return the sentinel `SCRUBBERS_UNDEFINED` = -1 when `out_scr(hp)` is 0, i.e. at or below `HP_FAIL` (no finite count exists) ; `T_hold = PATCH_DECAY_S` (240 s) is the maintenance interval of a life-critical scrubber at the patch ceiling: past it the patch decays and the margin goes negative, and duty wear (LSG:226) can shorten it ; `chore_per_hour_max = floor(3600 / T_hold)` re-patches, an **upper bound** (the earlier name `chore_per_hour` is retired), because it ignores the O2 tank buffer and the re-patch delay: the real interval is `T_hold` plus the time the tank takes to absorb the post-hold deficit, plus the player's reaction delay *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* ; `tank_steps(N, S, hp) = floor(stock / (N * R_BREATH - S * out_scr(hp)))` when the deficit `N * R_BREATH - S * out_scr(hp)` is positive, else `TANK_NEVER` (-1), with `stock` the O2 tank's stock (3,000,000 mGU at the shipped value) and 10 steps to the second (`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 at `TICK_RATE` 60), which is the tank-drawdown model that replaces the 15-per-hour figure as the planning number *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `scrubbers_needed` was off by one at exact divisibility — N = 4 at `hp` 10000 needs 3 scrubbers to reach margin 0 but 4 for a positive margin — and its UNDEFINED sentinel was not defined; the earlier `T_decay_cycle` 540 s as the maintenance interval was the time to FAILED, not the time to a negative margin, and understated the chore load by about 2.25x)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hp` | int bp | 0..`HP_FULL` | scrubber condition (the Graph) | Scrubber `hp` |
| `N` | int | 1..4 | the concept's 1-4 players | Crew breathing in the pool |
| `R_SCR` | int mGU/step | 400 (100-1000) | Graph data held in `mod_scrubber.rated_out` [LSG:927] | Scrubber nominal output |
| `R_BREATH` | int mGU/step | 300 (100-600) | Graph placeholder, owned by Suit & Equipment [LSG:929] | Draw per crew; read, never re-valued |
| `out_scr(hp)` | int mGU/step | 0..`R_SCR` | D.5 with D.4 (`HP_OK` 7000, `HP_FAIL` 3000) | Net output |

**Output Range:** `margin` from -N x 300 up to +100 at N = 1 (a fully repaired scrubber). It is an analysis formula, not a runtime rule; if Suit moves `R_BREATH` the ladder moves with it and is not duplicated here.
**Example:** (N = 1, `sat` 1000) `hp` 10000 -> `eff` 1000 -> `out` 400 -> margin +100; `hp` 6000 (`PATCH_CAP_BP` [REG:6021-6037]) -> `eff` 750 -> `out` 300 -> margin **0**; `hp` 5000 -> 200 -> -100; `hp` 4000 -> 100 -> -200; `hp` 3000 -> 0 -> -300. So a patched scrubber supports exactly one crew with zero margin, and the part (which restores `hp_cap` to `HP_FULL` and ends the decay clock) buys +100 and the end of the chore. For two crew with one scrubber at `hp` 10000 the deficit is 600 - 400 = 200 mGU/step and the home-ship's 3,000,000 mGU O2 tank lasts 3,000,000 / 200 = 15,000 steps = 25 min; for four crew the deficit is 800 and the tank lasts 3750 steps = 6.25 min. **Those two figures need a `HP_FULL` scrubber, which the MVP cannot reach (no `part_machine_kit` is placed, F-11 scope note).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **At the reachable ceiling, `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000 (`out` 300):** N = 2 with one scrubber has a deficit of 600 - 300 = 300 mGU/step and the tank lasts 10,000 steps = 1,000 s = **16.7 min** (not 25); N = 4 has a deficit of 1200 - 300 = 900 and the tank lasts 3,333 steps = **5.6 min** (not 6.25); `scrubbers_to_hold(N, 6000)` = N and `scrubbers_with_margin(N, 6000)` = N + 1. The ladder rows that need 3 or 4 scrubbers (3,080 W and 3,680 W at 600 W each over the 1,880 W standing load) are beyond both the 2,500 W reactor and the 2,161 W trunk (Power budget under IDB-8): the MVP supports one crew member in steady state and treats N = 2 to 4 as timed on the tank. **Steady-state margin row and chore load** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: at N = 1 the margin falls by 10 mGU/step for every 100 bp below the patch ceiling (the rows above: 6000 -> 0, 5000 -> -100; the Graph's D.4 and D.5 are authoritative), so a patched scrubber at `hp` 6000 holds exactly its hold time `T_hold` 240 s and then runs negative: an **upper bound** of 15 re-patches per hour (`chore_per_hour_max`), not the 6 that a 540 s interval gave; the first 100 bp of decay is a deficit of only 10 mGU/step, which the full tank (3,000,000 mGU) absorbs for 300,000 steps (8.3 h), so a solo player's real re-patch interval is set by how fast the deficit grows and by the player's own margin of safety, not by `T_hold` alone *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `scrubbers_to_hold(4, 10000)` = 3 (margin 0) and `scrubbers_with_margin(4, 10000)` = 4. This chore figure feeds Derelict Authoring's X-DA-9 and is a cross-GDD note. That is why derelict A's FAILED scrubber is the second scrubber a duo needs (a design reason and, since DA-47 / user ruling D72 gave `scr_a` a home slot, also an MVP route: the carry has a destination; the reference carry for the haul numbers stays `htr_a`, Boarding & Salvage C-BS-3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Lever (reported to the Graph owner, not changed here): at `PATCH_CAP_BP` 5500 the patched `eff` is 625, `out` 250, margin -50 at N = 1, which makes the part mandatory even for a solo player.

### F-15 Salvage-reinstall break-even (the launder regression tripwire)

> **WITHDRAWN as a blocking item — retained as a historical note** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. F-15, AC-94 and `T_CYCLE_MIN` are no longer blocking and no longer gate anything. The review found the formula compared the cost of one salvage cycle against the whole patch chore over a horizon, which are not comparable; that its `T_decay_cycle` of 540 s understated the chore load (the life-critical interval is `T_hold` 240 s, see F-14); and that its tripwire is redundant now that IDB-18 closes the launder by rule and the split persistence tests AC-32a to AC-32f are the real control. The heading stays so the formula register (IDB-32) keeps its ids; the body below is kept for the record and is not a requirement.

The patch_launder formula is defined as:

`launder_pays_off <=> T_cycle < floor(H / T_decay_cycle) * (c_patch + w_patch)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_cycle` | s | Interaction's and Assembly's | cut + carry + `cap_port` on the stubs + install | Time cost of one salvage-then-reinstall of a patched module |
| `H` | s | 1800-7200, design horizon | design | How long the module must stay serviceable |
| `T_decay_cycle` | s | 540 for a machine (to FAILED), 440 for a leak subject | Graph [LSG:769]: 240 s hold (`PATCH_DECAY_S`) plus 30 or 20 wear periods of 10 s at `PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100` 100 | Interval between mandatory re-patches |
| `c_patch`, `w_patch` | s | illustrative 8 and 15 | Interaction's channel time and walk (Interaction designed: `interaction-system.md`) | Cost of one re-patch |

**Output Range:** boolean. Under the adopted rule (IDB-18: a reinstall carries `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step`) the launder does not exist, so this check is **no longer the control**; it is a regression tripwire and the Graph owner's fallback arithmetic. It answers one question: if a build or a ruling ever lets a reinstall reset the cap or the clock (`hp_cap` `HP_FULL`, no `patched_at_step`), would cycling pay? The primary control is the persistence rule and its test (AC-32: cap and clock must come back unchanged); this check is the second line. In no case does a cycle launder the damage (`hp` is unchanged) or duty wear.
**Example:** (all times illustrative until Interaction is written) *had the rule been the other way*: for a patched scrubber over `H` 3600 s, `T_decay_cycle` 540 gives floor(3600 / 540) = 6 re-patches x (8 + 15) = 138 s of chore, and a salvage-reinstall in one room at a 35 s illustrative `T_cycle` would be cheaper, so the launder would pay off at the placeholders, at the price of the cut's stub hiss (F-12: the scrubber's one O2 stub, port 101 per IDB-7, leaks 200 mGU/step until capped), the island re-solving without the scrubber and an Attention spike. That is exactly why the rule carries the cap and the clock: a free, repeatable action must not substitute for the finite part. If the Graph owner ever rules that a reinstall resets cap and clock, `T_CYCLE_MIN` 100 s becomes the only control and the placeholders already fail it (35 s against 100 s), so that ruling is not acceptable without a stronger sink and goes to creative-director review.


## Edge Cases

Edge cases are grouped by theme. Diagnostics lines name only the **closed** failure list of IDB-30 (`ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID`, `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`, `ITEM_BOOT_PORT`, `ITEM_BOOT_REPAIR`, `ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR`, `ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS`), the Graph's codes (`GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, `NOT_REPAIRABLE`) or a named CI lint; the Item DB raises no runtime error and rejects no command, so a runtime refusal in a case below is always the owning neighbour's. Specialist proposals that invented other codes (a range code, a non-integer code, a forbidden-field code, a weight-margin code, an over-stack code) are mapped onto these.

**Catalogue boot and ids**

- **If two defs share a `def_id`, or two share a `type_id`**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(class, id)` naming the second occurrence. A catalogue with an ambiguous key cannot be loaded at all, because every save, hash and `SalvageRecord` is keyed by `def_id`.
- **If a `type_id` lies outside its prefix's class range (a `part_` def numbered 1xxx), or a `def_id` prefix disagrees with the declared class**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(class, id)` (F-8). The class ranges are what let the in-memory table be split by class without a lookup.
- **If a shipped def is renumbered (the ledger says `mod_scrubber` is 1001 and the data says 1020), or reuses a retired id**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID`. A `type_id` never reaches a save, so a renumber could not break one, but the ledger keeps the in-memory ids stable for the vertical slice's replication summaries and makes reuse impossible.
- **If `hp_start` is 0 or above `HP_FULL`**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, hp_start)` [IDB-9]. A node installed at `hp` 0 is a DESTROYED node leaking at its maximum [LSG:772].
- **If a JSON number arrives as `40000.5`, `1e9` or any non-integral value in an integer field**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, field)`; an integral float such as `40000.0` is accepted and converted once at boot (`JSON.parse_string` returns numbers as floats, a MEDIUM-confidence engine claim, spike-bound). Zero floats may survive into the table (LS-1 (b)).
- **If a def carries a forbidden key (`price`, `damage`, `recipe`, `appears_as`, `hallucination_*` and the rest of IDB-5's list)**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, field)`. The list is the machine form of the anti-pillars, so a later "just one price field" edit fails the build rather than a review.
- **If a def declares a `port_type_id` that is not in the Graph's LS-4 table, or one illegal for its node kind**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_PORT(def_id, local_index)`; the Graph's own boot would refuse the same table with `GRAPH_BOOT_TYPE_ID(table, id)`. The Item DB reports the def; the Graph reports the table.
- **If the LS-3 or LS-4 mirror in the catalogue differs from the Graph's boot table**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR(table)`. The mirror is read-only; the Graph is the owner.
- **If an alias chain exceeds `ALIAS_DEPTH_MAX` 8, forms a cycle, points at itself, or names a live `def_id`**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS(def_id)`. An alias never creates a def, and a retired string reused for a different def would let an old save silently load the wrong thing.
- **If a def with `mass_g` at or above `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` is authored with `stack_max` above 1, or a CARRIED/HAND def with `stack_max` above 1**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, stack_max)`. A heavy stack would let a player carry an unbounded mass outside the single `carry_ref`.
- **If `repair_part_for` has no answer for one of the five `subject_kind` values, or a module names a `repair_part_id` that is not a part def, or `parts_per_full` is below 1**: boot is refused with `ITEM_BOOT_REPAIR(def_id)`. A FULL repair must never cost zero parts, and M7 may be handed any subject kind.

**Zero, maximum and out-of-range values**

- **If a catalogue edit lowers `stack_max` below a count already held in a save**: the restore **keeps the full held count and deletes nothing**; the holder refuses further pickups of that def until the count is at or below the new maximum. This is the one invariant of IDB-15 that is not asserted at restore (it stays a lint at serialise). (orchestrator ruling: the economy proposal's keep-and-refuse-pickup over the systems proposal's clamp, because a silent clamp destroys items without the player doing anything, against Pillar 5's "loss is not total".)
- **If a module `ItemInstance` is serialised with `condition_bp` 0, or above `cap_bp`, or a `cap_bp` of 0, or a non-zero `patched_at_step` on a `cap_bp` of `HP_FULL`, or a `patched_at_step` of 0 on a `cap_bp` below `HP_FULL`, or a `patched_at_step` above the holder's current `step_no`**: the holder's restore refuses it with the holder's own named failure (the Item DB provides the check, not the code). A `cap_bp` of 9999 is **valid** (a patched module whose recorded cap is any value below `HP_FULL`; there is no tie to the current `PATCH_CAP_BP`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "neither `HP_FULL` nor at most `PATCH_CAP_BP`" form refused every saved patched module after a `PATCH_CAP_BP` retune)*. A module at `hp` 0, DESTROYED or FAILED-at-`hp`-0 (legal while the Graph's warning gate is closed), yields a part, never a module (F-5; cutting a FAILED `hp`-0 node scraps it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*, so a zero-condition module instance can only be corruption; a non-canonical zero would give one state two byte forms and desync `state_hash`.
- **If `mass_g` or a `WEIGHT_*` threshold is edited between builds, and a def now falls in a different `weight_class`**: nothing migrates and nothing bumps `catalogue_version`. Mass and class are never persisted on an instance (IDB-15), so the next read re-derives them; a boundary case is caught earlier by the `WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM` lint (F-1).
- **If an instance's `count` would exceed `stack_max` on a merge**: the merge is refused by the holder (Suit) and the surplus stays where it was; the Item DB supplies the cap and nothing else. A pickup refused for a full stack leaves the item in the world, so scarcity never silently deletes salvage.
- **If a FULL repair targets a subject already at `hp_cap` = `HP_FULL` with `hp` = `hp_cap`**: it consumes nothing and Interaction resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` [IDB-20 (1); IA-18 statement 7]. A part must change something to be spent.

**Simultaneous rules**

- **If two players cut the same node on the same tick**: the first applied M6 yields the module; the second M6 sees SALVAGED and is rejected under validate-then-apply [KRN:CR-9], so `salvage_yield` runs once on the applied record and the module cannot be duplicated. The yield is computed from the M6 return value, never from a pre-read of the node (F-5).
- **If two players start a FULL repair on one subject, each with one part, on the same step**: commands dispatch sequentially, so the first begin is Applied (its part RESERVED at the begin tick, then PAID at its first productive step) and the second seat's begin is `BUSY` at `validate` and makes no reservation, so the second player's visible stock never falls (Interaction IA-34; registry `full_job_charge`). Each player's part is spent only if that player's first step is applied (IDB-20 (7)).
- **If a FULL repair channel is interrupted (stagger, pod hop) after its first productive step**: the part stays spent and the `hp` already applied stays; the Item DB gives no refund. Interaction's `FULL_JOB_GRACE_S` (60 s, IA-18 statement 6) waives the charge when the same seat re-issues on the same subject inside the window; after it, or for another seat, the second attempt costs `parts_per_full` again.
- **If a PATCH step is issued inside a paid FULL job**: it would re-cap `hp_cap` to `PATCH_CAP_BP` and waste the part [LSG:186]; a FULL job must issue only `kind = FULL` steps (IDB-20 (2)), and the acceptance test asserts it.
- **If a player tries `begin_patch` or `begin_cut` while holding `carry_ref`**: Interaction rejects it `BUSY` under PC-8 [PCI:131, 168]; the player must `drop` first. This is why `part_hull_plate` is STOWED with `stack_max` 1: a stowed part never sets `carry_ref`, so the part itself can never be what blocks the full repair that consumes it.

**Degenerate strategies**

- **If a player salvages a patched module and reinstalls it to shed the patch ceiling and decay clock**: it sheds neither. The module reinstalls with its record's `hp`, `hp_cap` at `PATCH_CAP_BP` and the original `patched_at_step`, so the cap stays and the clock keeps its origin (IDB-18, settled by the Graph's LS-18 M5/M6 amendment) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Time in transit counts toward the hold: a module carried or shelved longer than `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 s reinstalls already past its hold and starts decaying at the next wear period. The only way to lift the cap is a FULL repair and its part. The cut still costs the hissing stubs, the island re-solving, a flat-speed carry and an Attention spike, so there is no reason to cycle; the split persistence tests AC-32a to AC-32f guard the regression (the `T_CYCLE_MIN` tripwire is withdrawn, F-15).
- **If a player deliberately destroys a spare module (overload, dropped into a breach) to harvest its part**: a DESTROYED node salvages into exactly `scrap_count` (1) of its `repair_part_id` and no module, so one module becomes one part at most; the conversion costs a module, which is itself finite salvage. Part conservation (IDB-29 (2)) holds: parts in existence never exceed authored loose parts plus DESTROYED module nodes. The conversion is not value-aware: a destroyed lamp yields the same scarce `part_cable_splice` as a destroyed breaker, so destruction is a (costly) parts source that F-11 prints as `supply_max` (whether to make scrap tier-aware, or to rule that fixtures cannot be destroyed on purpose, is Open Question 39) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a player salvages the running SCRUBBER or the live BATTERY**: it is allowed with no special case [LSG:773]; the island re-solves, the stubs hiss, the item is delivered whole. A reinstalled store starts empty (IDB-18), so a battery carried home is dark until recharged. The world shows the cost.
- **If a player hoards glowsticks or parts**: the holder caps each stack at `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` 4 and the part `stack_max`; a stack that is full refuses the pickup. Light and permanent fixes stay a decision, not a stockpile, and the stowed mass is a body cue only.
- **If a player tries to run with a carried module**: not an Item DB rule; the Controller forbids it (PC-8) and mass never changes the 1 px/tick carry speed or the carry noise (F-13). A request for mass-scaled speed is a registry conflict, not a tuning change.
- **If a player tries to run while the hull plate is stowed** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: `run` is refused for as long as any unreserved HEAVY STOWED instance is held (IDB-13, D18; Suit's `stowed_heavy`, SE-41, excludes units a FULL job has RESERVED) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the holder walks at `V_WALK`. The gait returns on the tick the plate leaves the stowed stock (the shed of States row 14, or the reservation at the begin tick of the FULL job that consumes it, F-4). A DOWNED seat keeps its stowed stock (States row 15) and, on a revive, still cannot run. Picking a plate up from the floor re-disables run on the pickup tick. The rule reads the def class, so it is unaffected by the glowsticks and parts in the same pack.

**Save, restore and catalogue edits**

- **If a save names a `def_id` that was removed with no `replaced_by`**: the Graph's restore fails with `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF` [LSG:832] for a node, or the holder's own named failure for an instance, with live state untouched. Never a silent drop (IDB-27). Diagnostics: the failing `def_id` and the save's `catalogue_version`.
- **If a removed def has `replaced_by`, or a renamed def has `renamed_from`**: restore maps the instance or node to the successor and keeps `condition_bp` / `hp`. Aliases are honoured only at restore (IDB-4, IDB-27).
- **If a save was written under a higher `catalogue_version` than the running catalogue**: Save/Load's pre-check does not refuse and the holder's restore decides (Save/Load SL-14 [SL:219]: "a header `catalogue_version` above or below the running catalogue's is never a pre-check refusal; the load proceeds and each holder's `restore()` decides; an unknown `def_id` stays the owning block's named failure (`GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF`); a `renamed_from` alias honoured only at restore is the Item DB's to offer"; AC-SL-96e [SL:1220]: `catalogue_version` 9 in the header against 7 in the running catalogue); the Item DB offers the integer and states that pre-release saves are disposable [LSG:832].
- **If the catalogue edits `req_w` (or another snapshotted-at-install field) of `mod_scrubber` and a save holds an installed scrubber**: the saved node keeps its old value until it is salvaged and reinstalled (IDB-6); no bump. **If it edits `waste_permille`, `R_SCR`, `BATT_CELL` or `cap_ml`** and the Graph reads those live, the change applies to existing saves at the next restore, a possible mid-game rebalance. The Graph ruled "read live through `def_id`" (its Item DB Interactions row; Open Question 8 closed). The one live-read edit with a consequence is a lowered `stock_cap`: stock above the new cap moves to `discarded_*` and is logged at restore (IDB-6, Open Question 31) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a catalogue retune lowers `stock_cap` below the stock a saved node holds** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: on restore the excess above the live cap moves to the section's `discarded_*` counter (conserved) and is logged; it is never a restore refusal and never silent (IDB-6 "Restore against a retuned catalogue"; Open Question 31; AC-105, BLOCKED-ON the Graph).
- **If `PATCH_CAP_BP` is retuned after a save holds a patched module** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the saved `cap_bp` (for example 6000 against a new 5500) restores and reinstalls unchanged; the invariant is `cap_bp < HP_FULL`, with no tie to the constant; the next PATCH takes `min(hp_cap, PATCH_CAP_BP)`, and a PATCH on `hp` at or above the new cap is refused `ABOVE_PATCH_CAP` with `hp` never clamped down (AC-106).
- **If the crew dies while carrying a module**: MVP death is restart from the last autosave (user decision; Save/Load owns it, SL-7), so the restore replays every holder block and no item-loss rule exists in the Item DB. The module is wherever the autosave had it.
- **If a section holding loose items or installed modules is discarded**: `discard_section` (M4 [LSG:183]) discards them with the section and destroys their mass into `discarded_*` [LSG:799]; the Item DB defines no refund. It is the loss sink of "ship, not knowledge".

**Salvage, ports and hardpoints (checkpoint-born)**

- **If the player salvages `mod_o2_tank`**: its single fluid stub (103, cap 1200) leaks 600 mGU/step until `cap_port` (F-12), about a DESTROYED duct's 500 [LSG:772], and the tank's stock is discarded into the section's `discarded_*` counters (IDB-18). With the old cap 3000 it would have bled 1500, three times a burst duct; F-12 refuses that value.
- **If the player salvages `mod_reactor_cell` and its fuel stub (402) is capped late**: the stub leaks 1 mL/step (cap 2); at the earlier cap 1 it would floor to 0 and the fuel line would be cut silently, which F-12 refuses. Its heat-jacket port (301, cap 40) is also a fluid stub and bleeds 20 mL/step of coolant until `cap_port`, like a pump stub (F-12).
- **If a SEAM record is the subject of a FULL repair request**: `repair_part_for` returns `NONE` for subject_kind 4, so Interaction offers PATCH only; a seam is restored by V9 re-weld (Assembly's), never for free. The Graph confirmed that a FULL on `subject_kind` SEAM 4 is not offered at MVP (LS-18 M7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a DESTROYED duct, cable or pipe edge is the subject**: `NOT_REPAIRABLE` [LSG:771]; no part is consumed and no def exists to salvage (edges are structure). The loop is cut for the rest of the run unless the Graph defines a replacement path (Open Questions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a DESTROYED edge permanently cuts a loop, which contradicts G-0 and "Loss Is Not Total". Authoring guarantee requested, not guaranteed here: no maintained edge reaches DESTROYED while the player keeps it patched. That guarantee cannot be absolute, because Derelict Authoring's closed-valve staging already lets `d_cryo_exposed` reach DESTROYED at minute 20.8 with no tell (DA-16, ASK-G1; the Graph owner decides between a literal, a gated and a replaced-route branch; dead faults are DA-17), so this GDD cross-references DA-16 and DA-17 and records the replacement-route question as Open Question 44; until it is answered a DESTROYED edge is a content defect in the slice.)*
