- **If a LOAD hardpoint accepts node kind 13 with no subtype filter**: `mod_heater` and `mod_lamp` have the same port signature, so `compatible_defs` (F-6) offers both and a heater could be installed in a lamp socket. The content lint reports an unfiltered LOAD hardpoint; Layout's LOAD hardpoints carry `accepted_subtypes` (DA-12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was an ask of Layout)*.
- **If `mod_door_motor` is FAILED, or unserved**: it "does not hold" [LSG:798]; the door's state follows the Graph and Interaction. The def contributes no heat (`waste_permille` 0) to the thermal baseline, so a dead door motor changes no temperature [LSG:700].
- **If a module with `emits` and the default `lantern_on_states` is FAILED**: it is dark; `{0 OK, 1 DEGRADED}` excludes FAILED [LSG:125]. A dead reactor or a failed scrubber does not glow, so the room's light never lies about the machine (scr_a on A's woken bus stays dark, DA-24; Boarding AC-BS-48). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **If a player asks for a patch kit**: no def exists; a PATCH consumes no item [REG:6033; LSG:222]. The former Graph wording "patch kits are the Item DB's finite consumable" has been removed by the Graph *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: no longer a routed defect)*.
- **If `curio_data_core` appears in an authored placement in the MVP**: the lint `PLACEMENT_NO_CONSUMER` warns; the item is carryable but has no consumer until the Knowledge Codex (vertical slice). A carry trip with no payoff would break Pillar 1's honesty.
- **If `salvageable` is false on a def**: the Graph does not enforce it [LSG:185]; Interaction's `begin_cut` `validate` reads it. No MVP def sets it.
- **If a player asks about `tool_suppressant`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: there is no such item at MVP. It is not in the roster, the start kit or any placement; a placement naming it fails `PLACEMENT_DEF_UNKNOWN`, and a save naming it fails the owning block's `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF` (no save can, because it never shipped). Its id is held by the dormant ledger entry (IDB-4).

**Hallucination interplay**

- **If the sprite or inspect card of a salvaged scrubber shows a turning rotor while the module is FAILED (false system state, class b)**: the lie is the module-body channel's; the instance's `condition_bp`, the node's `hp` and the Graph's output are unchanged, and the consequence channel (breath, visor fog, lamp brightness from `served_pm`) stays true [LSG:44]. The def holds no believed value to be corrupted.
- **If a phantom crate is dressed as a data core (class d, vertical slice) and picked up**: the carry body cue reports the junk def's true `mass_g` (a different class by F-10); the player can disprove the lie by weight before the trip home. A junk def whose class equals its lure's fails the lint and is not releasable.
- **If a hallucination shows a crewmate carrying a module as a horror (class c)**: `carry_ref`, the instance and its mass are unchanged; the human channel (voice, beam colour and shape) is never faked [CON:217-219]. The Item DB adds no field a lie could attach to.
- **If a player asks what a held item "really is" under hallucination**: the def's identity is never hidden behind an identify step (Player Fantasy); the only uncertainty is Pillar 4 perception, which has a cheaper falsifier (weight) than the trip home.

**Co-op hooks (later)**

- **If a downed player was carrying a module**: the Controller drops it as a loose item at that position (PCI PC-8); the instance persists as LOOSE_WORLD and a teammate may pick it up. The Item DB holds no ownership of an instance. **If the downed player held stowed stock** (parts, glowsticks, the plate): it stays on the body until revive or restart (States row 15); nothing spills and no `loose[]` entry is made, so a crewmate who needs the downed player's plate cannot take it in the MVP, which is a crew decision in a game where a downed seat dies in 30 s unless the air improves (SE-24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If two players each need a glowstick or a part**: counts are per player in Suit's block; the Item DB defines the stack cap and nothing about sharing or trading. A tool is unique per player (`stack_max` 1) and a loose duplicate of a held tool is an authoring warning.
- **If a replicated summary (vertical slice B) names an item**: it uses `def_id` for anything persisted and `type_id` only for in-memory summaries; an item never replicates a number from its def (mass, class), which each peer derives from its own identical catalogue.


## Dependencies

*Status key: **designed** = a complete GDD exists and its Item DB row was read; **vertical slice, undesigned** = no file yet, fields reserved only. As of 2026-10-27 every MVP neighbour is designed, so their rows are binding contracts; only the Vertical Slice systems are undesigned. Every dependent row states only what that consumer must supply to, or take from, the Item DB — not a guess at its internal state. (Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the stale "in flight (draft; Core Rules written)" status and the "provisional until the dependent's own GDD repeats it" paragraph are removed; the repeat-check is the document checklist in Acceptance Criteria.)*

| Life-Support Graph | Item DB consumes | **Hard** | The LS-3 node-kind ids, LS-4 port-type ids and the pure `connectable`, mirrored read-only; the loop ids (LS-2); the bounds that D.13's boot assertion enforces on def numbers; `HP_FULL`, `HP_OK`, `HP_FAIL`, `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000, `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 by name; M5, M6, M7 and their `check_*`; M6's five-field `SalvageRecord` and M5's `hp_cap` and `patched_at_step` arguments, which carry the patch ceiling and clock through reinstall (IDB-18, settled; was a provisional ask, OQ-10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The Graph is also the consumer of the defs (it reads `def_id` at M5 and at restore), so the dependency is mutual *at the data level* and acyclic by boot order (IDB-30) | **designed (complete)**, 261 KB; its Item DB rows are quoted in Interactions |

*Pillar mapping.* Primary P5 (the only faucet, finite parts, wear travels with `hp`) and P1 (a def holds only true values); serves P4(d) (true mass) and P3 (glowstick exposure; carry noise stays the Controller's). P2 has no Item DB mechanic.

*Known cross-system facts used by name and never re-valued* (registry, locked): `TICK_RATE` 60; `TILE_PX` 48; `V_CARRY` 1; `NOISE_PER_TICK` walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1; `HP_FULL` 10000; `HP_OK` 7000; `HP_FAIL` 3000; `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000; `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240; `MAX_PAYLOAD` 4 KiB; `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256; `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4; `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6; `GAS_SCALE` 1000; `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10. This GDD introduces no different value for any of them. **Registry obligations of the later stages** are listed in Cross-References; the `PATCH_CAP_BP` registry entry [REG:6021-6037] now lists this GDD in its `referenced_by` (the former "pending referenced_by" comment is resolved).

## Tuning Knobs

Every knob lives in external data under `assets/data/items/` (IDB-26); none is hardcoded. **Class key.** ★ = cross-system fact proposed for the registry (`status: placeholder`, written from the final text); [PH] = Item DB placeholder, not a Graph or registry fact; MIR = a value another GDD owns, listed only as a pointer to its source of truth and **not** re-valued here. Durations are labelled s (seconds), tick (60 Hz) or step (the Graph's 10 Hz). Masses are grams. "Too high / too low" describe the felt consequence, not the arithmetic.

**Glowstick (the Item DB owns the def properties; Emitter State owns the output curve; Suit owns counts and the start count)**

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` | 150 | s (9000 ticks, F-7) | 60-300 | permanent lighting; restraint (P3) means nothing | the light dies before a sortie leg ends; the stick is a flare, not a lamp | `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM`, Emitter State's exposure ledger |
| ★ `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` | 2 (96 base px at `TILE_PX` 48) | tiles | 1.5-3; strictly below `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3 [PCI:779] | the pool lights the next room and kills the dark | a marker, not a light; barely beyond the chest lamp | `THROW_RANGE_TILES` (Interaction's), Emitter State's reach and area, the lighting spike (art bible 8.12 step 2) |
| ★ `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` | 4 | count | 2-8 | lighting solved by carrying a crate of sticks | frequent refused pickups; rationing too tight | Suit's start count, Derelict Authoring's loose-stick supply, Lighting's pool budget |
| ★ `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` | 400 | permille of total life remaining | 200-1000 (1000 = linear from the throw) | wanes from the start; the "steady" read is lost | the light ends abruptly with no warning dim | `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM`, Emitter State `WANE_LINEAR_25` |
| ★ `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` | 250 | permille of full reach | 100-500 | a lingering ember hard to read as spent; pops out at half reach | fades to nothing, a pop to black | `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM`; exposure scales with area, so 250 is 1/16 of the pool |
| `cons_glowstick.emits.base_pm` (= Emitter State's `GLOWSTICK_BASE_PM`) | 1000 [PH] | permille | 500-1000 | none (it is the ceiling) | a dim pool that does not read against the chest lamp | Emitter State's brightness term and the sixth-slot cut |

| `hp_start` (fresh) | 10000 | bp | 10000 for a fresh def (boot-valid 1-10000, IDB-9; a def default below `HP_OK` 7000 is an authoring warning) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier 7000-10000 range disagreed with IDB-9's boot rule)* | n/a (the ceiling) | fresh modules arrive worn and the patch logic gets muddy | `HP_OK` 7000; authored derelict overrides are Authoring's |
| `duty_wear` (RADIATOR) | false | bool | false in the MVP | the cooler wears out within a session | n/a | D.9 [LSG:505] |
| `salvageable` | true | bool | true for every MVP def | n/a | a "fixed" module breaks V8 | Interaction's `begin_cut` `validate` |

**Locked and Graph-owned values this GDD reads (not Item DB knobs; never re-valued here)**

| Value | Owner and source of truth | How the Item DB depends on it |
|---|---|---|
| `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000 (range 4500-6900) | registry [REG:6021-6037]; build rule [LSG:903] | the cap that makes a part worth a sortie (F-14). Lever, not a change: at 5500 a patched scrubber is at margin -50 for one crew, which makes the part mandatory even solo |
| `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240, `PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100` 100, `LEAK_HP` | `PATCH_DECAY_S`: registry [REG:6007-6019]; `LEAK_HP` 4000: registry [REG:10138] (life-support-graph.md owns it); `PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100` 100: Graph data, not registered [LSG:769] | the decay clock the part ends (F-14's `T_hold`; F-15 is withdrawn) |
| `HP_FULL` 10000, `HP_OK` 7000, `HP_FAIL` 3000 | registry [REG:5863-5917] | `hp_start`, `cap_bp`, the `eff` ladder (F-14) |
| `V_CARRY` 1, `NOISE_PER_TICK` carry 1 / run 4 / walk 1, `TILE_PX` 48, `TICK_RATE` 60 | registry; Controller; pipeline | F-13 (mass never enters), F-7 |
| `R_BREATH` 300 (Suit's), `R_VENT` 600, `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` 500, `INRUSH_PM` 1500 | Graph [LSG:929, 928, 960, 933] | F-12, F-14. **Coupled window** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the shipped roster passes F-12 only while `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` is **500-625** (Graph range 100-1000); outside it the 103 stub is louder than the bound or the 402 stub floors to 0; a Graph retune must signal the Item DB (Open Question 41) |
| `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3, `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 | Controller (Interaction's placeholders) [PCI:779, 611] | the glowstick radius bound and the landing life |

**Interaction notes.** (1) `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G`, `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` and every `mass_g` are coupled by the blocking margin lint: retuning one threshold can fail a roster mass, and the lint names it. (2) `PATCH_CAP_BP`, `R_SCR` and `R_BREATH` are coupled in a way neither the Graph nor the Item DB sees alone (F-14); a change to any one of them should re-run the ladder. (3) A fluid-port cap is now a **loudness** knob (the severed-leak column) as well as a rated flow; raising a cap to make a seam carry more makes the salvage of that module louder. (4) `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S`, `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` and `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` together set how much light a player can buy with salvage; tune them against Emitter State's exposure ledger, not alone. (5) Nothing here may be a price, a rarity or a multiplier on depth: a `grade` effect, when it comes, is a separate def in the same `family`, never a multiplier on a stat (IDB-5).


## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Scope note.* The Item DB is data: it renders nothing and plays nothing. This section states what the player must be able to **see and hear because of the catalogue**, which GDD draws or sounds it, and which art bible sections bind it. No art-director proposal file and no audio proposal exists for this stage (the stage brief lists none), so the art-direction questions are routed to Open Questions rather than invented here. Citations use the keys of the Overview; "ART n.n" is an art bible section.

**What the player sees, and the rule behind it.** (1) *Condition is drawn, never labelled.* A salvaged module shows the wear it came with as scuffs worn to bare metal at rounded corners (Foundry, ART 6.1) and a bolted patch plate that straddles panel lines "on-grid and off-rhythm" after a PATCH (ART 6.1, [ART:617]); permitted materials are cold metals only (ART 6.2, [ART:635]). No bar, number or colour code states condition (IDB-21, G3). (2) *A dead module is a stopped machine.* A FAILED scrubber is dark, its rotor still and no medium moving (ART design principle 2); the catalogue never "refreshes" it (IDB-9, IDB-24), so the sprite family is the same def at a lower `hp`, not a `_dead` variant. (3) *Parts read like the loop they mend*: a duct coupling reads O2, a cable splice reads power, a pipe fitting reads coolant by silhouette and loop hue, hue redundant with shape ([CON:343-345]); the hue is the art-director's token, the shape is the `sprite_family` key. (4) *Tools are silhouettes.* A hand tool is a sprite drawn once per tool, never per dialect (ART 5.1, [ART:512]); its hand-rail glyph is that sprite reduced to the 16 px grid (ART 7.2, [ART:815]), so `glyph_id` names a derived asset, not a hand-made icon. (5) *The carry frame keeps the chest lamp clear*: a carried module rides above the helmet and never covers the chest point (ART 5.1, [ART:512]), and a heavy stowed part (the hull plate) is drawn on the back rig under the same rule; no bare skin, no body shown ([ART:508]). (6) *Sane-palette discipline*: no item, part, tool or glowstick uses flesh or violet, which are reserved for sanity leaks ([CON:333-334]); there is no rarity colour, because there is no rarity (IDB-5, IDB-29). (7) *Footprint*: every def draws in one of the five sprite cells (`M96`, `P48`, `P48x96`, `P96x48`, `T80`; ART 8.2) named by the grammar of ART 8.5.

| Event | Visual feedback | Audio feedback | Rendered or sounded by | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A salvageable module sits in a derelict | Its `sprite_family` at the authored `hp`: worn, scuffed, dark when FAILED; the `salvageable` flag draws nothing extra | Silence from a dead machine; a live one makes its own running sound | Derelict Authoring places it; the Pipeline draws it; Adaptive Audio and Tells sounds a live one | MVP |
| The cut frees the module | The module detaches; its severed ports become `open_end` stubs that hiss and vent (IDB-7 severed-leak column) | The leak hiss the player caused, louder for a tank stub than a pump stub (the F-12 severed-leak column is the loudness ranking) | Graph (leak physics); Interaction (the cut channel); Adaptive Audio and Tells (the hiss) | MVP |
| A heavy part is stowed (the hull plate, `weight_class` HEAVY) | A plate on the back rig, derived from the class (IDB-21), never covering the chest point or the beam; absent when no HEAVY STOWED def is held | No catalogue sound; any stowed-haul noise is Interaction's flat per-tile choice (OQ-4); the Suit's closed-list body cue carries the weight (binding, IDB-21) | Pipeline (sprite); Suit (body cue); Interaction | MVP |
| A module is carried | The carry frame: both hands full, module above the helmet, chest lamp beam unobstructed | The carry footfall is the Controller's `NOISE_PER_TICK` carry stimulus; the Item DB defines no sound and no sound scales with `mass_g` | Controller (gait and noise); Suit (the body cue) | MVP |
| A module is installed at the home-ship | The def's `sprite_family` appears in its hardpoint with the condition it carried; a reinstalled store reads **empty** (dark store, silent gauge on the sane channel, IDB-18) | The install clunk is Assembly's; a running machine begins its loop only if the Graph runs it | Assembly; Pipeline; Adaptive Audio and Tells | MVP |
| PATCH applied | A bolted patch plate appears on the module body; the machine runs DEGRADED if `hp` allows (`PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000). The plate wears a weld bead of one, two or three segments by the patched subject's `hp` (Interaction D.1b `bead_segments`, selected by the Module-Body Presenter, In-World Instruments IW-18); a FULL repair removes plate and bead. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | The repair tool's channel sound (Interaction's), then the machine's own degraded running sound | Interaction; Pipeline | MVP |
| FULL repair spends a part | The patch plate is removed or replaced by a visually distinct "permanent repair" mark **(art-director question, Open Questions; creative-director direction: in the sane state a fully repaired module must not read as capped or patched)**; the part leaves the stowed stack (it was never on the hand rail, which holds tools; reserved from the FULL begin tick, SE-41) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: wording)* | The channel sound; the consume beat is the payoff (Game Feel) | Interaction; Suit; Pipeline | MVP |
| A tool is in hand | The tool sprite at the hand point; the tool layer carries no light (ART 5.1) | The tool's own working sound while its verb channels (Interaction's) | Pipeline; Interaction | MVP |
| A glowstick is thrown | A small arc over `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 to a landing within `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3 | A throw and a landing sound (Interaction and Adaptive Audio designed; the Item DB specifies none) | Interaction (arc); Pipeline | MVP |
| A glowstick burns | An omnidirectional Chemlight `#86B896` pool, radius `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` 2 (96 px, ART 8.3), **steady** then **waning** to `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` 250, never flickering, then out at `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` 150 s (F-7); it counts as exposure like any pool (ART 3.2, [ART:286]) | A faint chemical hiss is optional and, if added, must not tell the player the remaining life (the waning light is the clock) | Emitter State (the pool, curve `WANE_LINEAR_25`); Lighting (the sixth-slot assignment) | MVP |
| A module lantern glows *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | The reactor cell, coolant pump and scrubber carry a node lantern (EM-14) at `base_pm` 300, 150 and 200 while OK or DEGRADED, off when FAILED (the failed scrubber never lights, DA-24); `mod_lamp` follows its switch | The machine's running sound | Emitter State; Lighting | MVP |
| A part or consumable is picked up | The item leaves the world sprite and joins the stowed count; no pickup flash, no rarity glint | A short neutral handling sound (Interaction's); no jingle that signals value | Interaction; Suit | MVP |
| A stowed stack is shed (States row 14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: row added)* | The stack's count falls and its sprite appears on the floor at the holder's feet; if it was the plate, the back-frame plate leaves the sprite and the HEAVY breath layer falls away on the next Suit run | A short neutral set-down sound (Interaction's); no value cue | Interaction; Suit | MVP, pending Suit and Interaction (Open Question 34) |
| The hand rail shows a tool or glowstick | The tool silhouette at 16 px (`glyph_id`), the item's `name_key` as plain technician text | None | HUD and Suit Layer (designed) | MVP |
| A false system state hides a module's true condition (class b) | The sprite shows a turning rotor and no patch plate; the inspect card reads OK | None from the catalogue; the room still thins, which is the sane audio and visual truth | Perception Filter; In-World Instruments (designed) | Vertical slice for the full set; class (b) MVP |
| A phantom crate is dressed as a data core (class d) | A lure sprite over a `junk_` def | The carry gait and the Suit body cue report the true mass | Perception Filter; Suit | Vertical slice |

**Audio, stated plainly.** The catalogue adds **no sound design of its own**. The three sounds that matter are owned elsewhere: the cut hiss (Graph leak plus Adaptive Audio and Tells), the carry footfall (the Controller's carry noise, which is flat per tile and never scales with mass), and the running or silent state of an installed machine (the machine's own state). One rule binds every audio author who touches an item: **a sound must never encode value or hidden condition** — no rare-item chime, no "this one is broken" cue — because that would be a hidden stat leaking through the audio channel (P1) and a free sane-channel substitute for the P4(b) field check. The only item sound that is information is the one the world would make anyway.

**Art bible sections that apply.** 3.2 (steady then waning light) and 3.3 (dialect axes; Foundry is the MVP dialect, IDB-23), 4.1/4.2/4.7 (Chemlight `#86B896`), 5.1 (tools in hand, carry frames), 6.1/6.2 (wear, patch plates, permitted materials), 7.2 (the 16 px glyph grid), 8.2/8.5 (sprite cells and naming), 8.3 (the glowstick pool: radius 2 tiles / 96 px, "provisional - game-designer", now owned here as `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES`). The flickering Tungsten deployable family (flare, torch, decoy; radius 2.5 tiles / 120 px, [ART:395, 650, 1083]) is **not in the MVP roster** and has no def; when added it is a new `cons_` family with its own numbers (Open Questions).

📌 **Asset Spec** — Visual/Audio requirements are defined. After the art bible is approved, run `/asset-spec system:item-and-module-database` to produce per-asset visual descriptions, dimensions, and generation prompts from this section.

## Game Feel

*Ownership note.* The catalogue has no input, no animation and no frame data, so the feel of its things is owned elsewhere: the **carry** by the Controller (`V_CARRY`, PC-8), the **body cue** for mass by Suit & Equipment, the **channelled repair, cut and throw** by the Interaction System, the **glowstick light** by Emitter State. What this GDD owns is the *data that makes those feels honest*: a true mass, a true class, a true condition and a finite part. Every target below is therefore a requirement on a neighbour, tagged with the neighbour, and nothing here is a number the Item DB can tune except where a knob is named. Ticks are 60 Hz; steps are 10 Hz.

### Feel Reference

The target is the moment in **Project Zomboid** when a player drags a heavy generator or a stack of planks across a yard: the cost is the **trip**, not a number — slower, louder, hands busy, every sound behind you suddenly relevant — and the thing keeps the damage it already had. Borrow that, and borrow **Pandorum's** hands-free chest lamp: the beam stays on the road while both arms hold the load. The anti-reference is the **encumbrance meter** (a weight bar that gently scales speed) and the **inventory grid**: weight here is never a readout and never a gradient, and nothing is "managed" in a menu. A second anti-reference is the **loot pop** (a glint, a chime, a rarity colour at pickup): picking a thing up is a quiet, physical act whose meaning arrives later, at the install.

### Input Responsiveness

The Item DB adds no input. The item actions below are the Controller's and Interaction's; the row states the feel requirement the catalogue's data imposes on them. Latencies are proposals for those GDDs to adopt or replace (Interaction designed: `interaction-system.md`).

| Action | Max input-to-response latency | Frame budget (60 Hz) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pickup` a module (sets `carry_ref`) | the gait change begins on the next tick | 1 tick | The slowdown must be visible at once; a delayed gait reads as a bug, not weight |
| `drop` a carried module | the next tick | 1 tick | `drop` is the one mode PC-8 accepts while carrying; it must never be `BUSY` |
| Any other `interact` while carrying | rejected `BUSY` on the same tick | 1 tick | The refusal is immediate and legible (Controller's feedback), never silent |
| `throw_glowstick` | the arc starts on the next tick; lands after `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 | 1 tick, then 30 | The stick counts life from the throw tick (F-7), so a quick re-throw does not waste light |
| Use or start a FULL repair | the channel starts on the next tick if a part is held | 1 tick | The part is spent at the first **productive** step, not at button press (IDB-20) |
| Hand-rail swap to a tool | the next tick | 1 tick | The glyph and the hand sprite change together |

### Animation Feel Targets

Frame counts belong to the art and Controller GDDs; the table fixes only the feel goal each item animation must serve.

| Animation | Startup | Active | Recovery | Feel goal | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lift a module to the carry frame | short (set by the Controller) | the carry gait | none (the carry is a state) | Deliberate, two-handed, effortful but never a stall | The gait is the weight; the chest lamp stays unobstructed [ART:512] |
| Set a module down (`drop`) | short | — | brief settle | Committed, with a hint of relief | Not a throw; a dropped module is LOOSE_WORLD with its condition unchanged (States) |
| Throw a glowstick | quick wind-up | the 30-tick arc | none | Light, underhand, a little desperate | The arc is Interaction's; the light is Emitter State's |
| Weld or patch channel (cutter, welder) | the channel's own wind-up | the linear channel bar | brief | Steady and loud; the sound of work you chose to do | Channel timing is Interaction's; the bar is the linear bar of art bible 7.3 [PCI:478] |
| Spend a repair part (FULL repair beat) | the first productive step | the rest of the channel | a settle | The payoff: a held-breath step, then the patch plate comes off or the machine spools up | See Impact Moments |

### Impact Moments

There is no hit-stop, shake or rumble in this system: a survival-horror economy earns its beats from **consequence**, not punctuation. The moments of peak feedback are all world events the catalogue's data causes.

| Impact type | Duration | Effect description | Configurable? |
|---|---|---|---|
| The cut frees a module | the leak persists until capped | The severed ports hiss (louder for a tank than a pump stub); the player hears what they did | Loudness is the F-12 cap, a knob of this GDD (`SEVER_O2_LOUD_MAX_PM`) |
| Install of a still-dead module | at install | The module is in place and **still dark**: no spark, no recovery, the disappointment is the beat (the world never lies) | No; this is IDB-9 |
| PATCH to `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000 | the channel | The machine starts, DEGRADED, and the room begins to recover; the plate is bolted on | Cap is the Graph's registry value |
| FULL repair consumes a part | at the first productive step | The part leaves the stowed stack; the cap returns to `HP_FULL` and the patch clock ends | `parts_per_full` per def (IDB-19) |
| Glowstick waning | the last `WANE_TICKS` 3600 ticks (60 s) of its 9000 | The pool shrinks smoothly toward the floor and goes out; no flicker | `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` |
| Controller rumble | — | None for item actions; pad feedback, if any, is the Controller's and never encodes mass | No |

### Weight and Responsiveness Profile

- **Weight**: Heavy and deliberate for a module; unremarkable for a part, tool or glowstick. The weight is expressed as a **gait and a cost in sound and exposure**, never as a number, a bar or a pop-up. A 90 kg reactor cell and a 6 kg data core cost the same per tile (F-13); the body cue (Suit) is where their masses differ. The 30 kg hull plate on the hauler's back rig is the other visible weight: it is seen on the back frame and felt as the same closed-list body cue (IDB-21), never as a number.
- **Player control**: High. Even while carrying, the player can steer, aim the chest lamp and `drop` on any tick; the only things they lose are the run and the other `interact` modes. The load commits the player to a path, not to an animation.
- **Snap quality**: Analog for the burn of a glowstick (a smooth waning), binary for installs and part spending (it is done, or it is not).
- **Acceleration model**: Simulation feel. The carry gait is a flat `V_CARRY` 1 px/tick taken the tick `carry_ref` is set (the Controller's ramp, `RUN_RAMP_TICKS`, applies to run only [PCI:296-319]); the catalogue adds no acceleration term of its own.
- **Failure texture**: Fair and readable. A repair fails for a reason the world shows: the part is missing (the stowed stack has none), the subject is DESTROYED (no plate to patch), or the cap is reached (the module runs but remains DEGRADED). A lost module is never a surprise: a dropped or abandoned module is still on the floor with its condition unchanged.

### Feel Acceptance Criteria

Subjective targets stated so a playtester can give a consistent verdict; the formal Visual/Feel evidence rule (retained screenshot plus lead sign-off) is in Acceptance Criteria (AC-33, AC-47, AC-53).

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: every item below was a single-tester, unscored claim that could not fail; each now carries a threshold in the style of the Player Controller's weight protocol, "≥ 80 % of ≥ 8 testers", written 6 of 8 here (75 percent) for the smaller sample the Item DB can afford. Protocol for all of them: a scripted session, eight or more testers, scored against a written key, evidence file `production/qa/evidence/item-db/et-protocol-[date].md` retained.)*

- [ ] After the first salvage, at least 6 of 8 testers say where the home-ship's scrubber came from without prompting (ET-1, provenance is felt).
- [ ] At least 6 of 8 testers who install a FAILED module reach for a patch, not a menu (ET-2).
- [ ] At least 6 of 8 testers explain in their own words why a patched module still needs a part (ET-3, the cap reads as physical, not arbitrary).
- [ ] After 30 minutes at most 1 of 8 testers asks where the shop or the crafting bench is (ET-4).
- [ ] At least 6 of 8 testers pick "heavy" or "committing" over "floaty", "free" or "speed penalty bar" for a carried module on a forced-choice pair; the haul is described as slow to flee and committing.
- [ ] No playtester can read a mass, a condition number or a rarity from any surface (G3, G4), yet can tell a heavy thing from a light one by the body cue once Suit exists.
- [ ] (Vertical slice, ET-5) At least 6 of 8 testers who carried a phantom crate home say "it felt too light" or "I should have checked".

## UI Requirements

**The Item DB has no UI of its own.** It is a static catalogue (IDB-1) with no screen, menu, inventory view, weight readout or condition bar, and it must never acquire one (guard rails G1-G3 and ruling R8: no pack capacity and no weight readout in the MVP; the concept itself is silent on an inventory screen, so this is a decision of this GDD, to be confirmed by the HUD GDD). It supplies exactly **two string keys** to surfaces other GDDs own: `glyph_id` (the tool's 16 px silhouette, ART 7.2) and `name_key` (a plain technician-register localisation key, IDB-5), plus the true `mass_g` and `weight_class` that Suit turns into a body cue rather than text. Every surface below is owned by the GDD in the last column, and each of those GDDs must repeat the clause it takes from this table in its own Interactions row (AC-63, AC-96).

| Information | Display location | Update frequency | Condition | Owner of the surface |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Held tool (cutter, welder, scanner) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Hand rail: the tool's silhouette glyph (`glyph_id`) with its `name_key` on focus | On equip change | A tool is in hand | HUD & Suit Layer (designed, `hud-and-suit-layer.md`) |
| Held glowstick count *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | Hand rail or suit readout: a small integer beside the glyph (the count is Suit's block; `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` 4 is the cap) | On pickup, throw, use | Count above 0 | HUD & Suit Layer; Suit & Equipment (both designed) |
| ~~Suppressant charge~~ *(row retired: no charge-bearing tool at MVP, X6; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | none | none | none | HUD & Suit Layer; Suit (both designed) |
| Carried module | **No glyph or text.** It is the carry frame in the world (module above the helmet) | Continuous | `carry_ref` set | Pipeline and Controller (the sprite) |
| Carried or stowed mass | **No number, bar or word.** Only the Suit's closed-list consequence-channel body cue: **breath** (load layers on the breath cadence, three steps LIGHT / MEDIUM / HEAVY; Suit SE-27, no vignette) | Continuous | Mass above the cue's threshold | Suit & Equipment (designed) |
| A module's condition | **No bar.** It is the module's output in the room and its scuffs and patch plate. The in-world inspect card names the reading only as the instrument label (`name_key`); it never states the module's state in words (BS-6, IA-39) | On inspect | Player inspects a module | In-World Instruments (designed, K-3) |
| Repair part availability | The part appears in the stowed stack and the stack falls at the FULL begin tick (reservation, `full_job_charge`); without the part FULL is simply not offered, there is no `BLOCKED` (Interaction IA-18 statement 1) | On attempt | A FULL job is requested | Interaction System (designed) |
| Item name | Plain `name_key` text from the localisation table, in the manufacturer's register ("scrubber", "pipe fitting"); never a value adjective (AP-6) | On focus or inspect | Any named item | HUD & Suit Layer; localisation (narrative-director owns strings) |
| Whether a hardpoint accepts this module | The Layout lint and the assembly cursor use `compatible_defs`; an incompatible install is simply refused with `PORT_INCOMPAT`, never offered | On install attempt | A module is carried to a hardpoint | Modular Ship Assembly; Ship Section & Layout (designed) |

**What the catalogue forbids any surface to show.** No mass or weight number anywhere (the field check for phantom salvage depends on the body cue being the only mass channel, IDB-21, IDB-22); no condition bar, percent or `hp` value on a def or a card (P1); no rarity, tier or "quality" colour or word (IDB-5, IDB-29); no stack of "total items", pack capacity or inventory grid; no recipe or "craftable" marker; no price. The gamepad and keyboard prompts for pickup, drop, throw and use belong to the Controller's input map and Interaction, not to this table.

**Accessibility notes.** Part hues are redundant with silhouette so the loop is readable without colour ([CON:343-345]); the glowstick's waning must be legible as a shrinking pool and not only a hue change; `name_key` strings must be screen-reader-clean ASCII keys with no embedded markup. The project-wide accessibility standard (`design/accessibility-requirements.md`) is not yet written; any subtitle or caption for the item sounds is Adaptive Audio's (Open Questions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the weight check, the headline mechanic of Pillar 4(d), is audio-first — breath with a load signature, SE-28 — and the MEDIUM class has no visual; only HEAVY has the non-corruptible back-frame (SE-27). Any redundant visual cadence for the load must itself be on the Graph's closed consequence list (for example a visor fog or rime variant), or it can be corrupted and so becomes lie-able and breaks Pillar 1; otherwise the accessibility file records an explicit exception. Open Question 40; the Vertical Slice phantom-salvage story depends on the answer.)*

No bespoke UX spec is authored for this system: it owns no screen. The two hand-rail keys are specified in the HUD & Suit Layer's UX spec when that GDD exists. This is deliberately not a "📌 UX Flag" (the template's flag marks a system with real UI of its own, and this one has none).

## Acceptance Criteria

*How to read this section.* Every criterion is GIVEN / WHEN / THEN, cites the `IDB-` rule and/or `F-` formula it tests, and carries a **tag** and a **gate**. Tags: **(auto)** an automated unit test; **(integration)** an automated multi-system test; **(lint)** a catalogue or content scan run by `tools/ci/item_db_lints`; **(review)** a reviewer or lead reads a sibling GDD or a result against this text; **(manual)** a human plays or inspects it, with a retained screenshot **and** lead sign-off in `production/qa/evidence/` named `item-db-ac-NN-[subject]-[build].png` (the Visual/Feel and UI evidence rule); **(spike)** a time-boxed engine experiment whose result is recorded. Gates follow the project testing standard: every (auto), (integration) and (lint) is **BLOCKING** in CI unless marked ADVISORY; (review) criteria gate this GDD's move from Designed to Approved, not CI; a criterion blocked on a sibling says **BLOCKED-ON** and its sibling, and closes when that sibling **implements** the named clause *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: every MVP sibling GDD is now designed, so BLOCKED-ON no longer means "the GDD is unwritten"; it waits on the sibling's implementation of the clause)*. Test files follow the Godot test root: `tests/unit/item_db/` and `tests/integration/item_db/` (gdUnit4 `[system]_[feature]_test.gd`, functions `test_[scenario]_[expected]`); fixtures are factory functions or constant files under `tests/fixtures/item_db/`, never inline magic numbers (boundary values excepted); all timing is in ticks (60 Hz) or steps (10 Hz). Suggested files: `item_catalog_boot_test.gd` (A), `item_catalog_roster_test.gd` and `item_catalog_ports_test.gd` (B, C), `item_instance_schema_test.gd` and `item_salvage_yield_test.gd` (D), `item_repair_part_test.gd` (E), `item_weight_class_test.gd` (F), `item_glowstick_contract_test.gd` (G), `item_versioning_test.gd` (H), `item_query_api_test.gd` (A), the integration files `item_graph_roundtrip_test.gd`, `item_save_roundtrip_test.gd`, `item_controller_haul_test.gd` and per-neighbour integration files (J), each marked BLOCKED-ON the neighbour's implementation of the named clause. Lints and analysis formulas live in `tools/ci/item_db_lints` with their own unit tests.

*Ids are stable.* The criteria keep the numbers AC-01 to AC-103 that the QA review maps to test files; a withdrawn or merged criterion is marked in place, never renumbered: AC-14 is withdrawn (replaced by AC-80), AC-57 is merged into AC-12, AC-70 is merged into AC-28, and AC-59(c) is dropped as a duplicate of AC-08. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: AC-62 and AC-94 are withdrawn with F-15; AC-32 is split into AC-32a to AC-32f (the id is kept as the group); AC-63, AC-96, AC-102 and the review halves of AC-56, AC-58, AC-101 and AC-40 are moved to a document checklist that does not count toward coverage; new criteria are AC-104 to AC-114.)* **Test infrastructure** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: no `tests/` tree and no gdUnit4 exist yet, and several "unit" criteria need the Graph's type tables. The Foundation `/test-setup` story must scaffold gdUnit4 and CI before any Logic story of this system can be accepted (it blocks them), and the criteria use a shared fixture `graph_tables_stub` (the LS-3 and LS-4 tables as plain data, injected into `ItemCatalog.new(data, graph_tables)`, IDB-26) so a unit test needs no live Graph. Counts and the next free `type_id` are read from `roster_golden.json`, never repeated as literals.

*Rulings applied here (orchestrator rulings on the QA-lead blockers, each also written into the rule it touches).* B2: the pure helpers `part_cost`, `held_mass_g`, `validate_instance` and `glowstick_reach` exist in IDB-28, and the lint formulas are CI tools; F-4's channel behaviour is tested as Interaction's (integration, BLOCKED-ON Interaction). B3: the performance criteria are blocking only as deterministic proxies (object and node counts, byte counts); wall-clock bounds are gated at the spike on the named reference machines RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK (`project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`; release export, `forward_plus`, P99 at most 90 percent of the 16.6 ms / 33.3 ms ceiling) in `tests/performance/item_db/`, pending spike measurement *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* B4/B5: unknown lookups return the sentinel `NO_DEF`, an unknown `kind_id` returns (`NONE`, 0), and `salvage_yield` of ABSENT or SALVAGED returns an empty list. B7: tuning-knob ranges are guidance with an ADVISORY lint; only three structural invariants are boot assertions. B8: a class base (1000, 2000, ...) is reserved. B9: AC-48 names both reasons a 26000 g junk fails. B1 (save-header name): resolved — Save/Load was re-edited after the review round that first asked for a rename and its final text now carries `catalogue_version` (header table SL:159, Item DB row SL:476, Dependencies SL:852, AC-SL-96e SL:1220; its OQ-13 closed at SL:1301), so both GDDs name the field `catalogue_version`; AC-56 is a passed review and AC-101 is no longer blocked.

### A. Catalogue, ids, boot and the query API (IDB-1 to IDB-4, IDB-26, IDB-28, IDB-30; F-1, F-7, F-8)

- **AC-01 (auto, IDB-1, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a clean BOOTING with the Graph's type tables and `GraphTuning` loaded, WHEN the catalogue loads the shipped `assets/data/items/*.json`, THEN it reaches FROZEN with exactly the per-class def counts recorded in `roster_golden.json` (23 defs at this writing: 13 modules, 3 tools, 1 consumable, 5 parts, 1 curio, 0 junk; the dormant `tool_suppressant` is not counted *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the literal lives in the fixture, so adding a def edits one file), registers no state block, no hook and no command with the kernel, and the kernel's registry freeze (CR-6) is unaffected.
- **AC-02 (auto, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the catalogue is asked to load BEFORE the Graph's type tables, WHEN boot runs, THEN boot fails with a BOOTING-order error and serves no def; GIVEN it loads AFTER the Graph tables and BEFORE any block restore, THEN `def_id` validation at restore succeeds.
- **AC-03 (auto, IDB-4, F-8; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a valid fixture catalogue as control (it boots), WHEN each of eight one-def mutations is applied in turn, THEN each is REFUSED with `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(class, id)` naming the offender: (1) two defs share a `type_id`; (2) two share a `def_id`; (3) a `part_` def numbered 1xxx; (4) a `mod_` def numbered 2001; (5) a `type_id` listed in `retired_ids`; (6) the ledger says `mod_scrubber` is 1001 and the data says 1020; (7) a ledger pair is missing from the catalogue with no `retired_ids` entry; (8) a retired `def_id` is re-added with a fresh `type_id`.
- **AC-04 (auto, IDB-3; BLOCKING)** GIVEN every roster `def_id`, WHEN matched against `^(mod|tool|cons|part|curio|junk)_[a-z][a-z0-9]*(_[a-z0-9]+)*$` and `ID_MAX_LEN` 40, THEN every roster id in `roster_golden.json` matches; `mod_o2_tank` is accepted; `Mod_x`, `mod-x` and a 41-character id are refused with `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, "def_id")`.
- **AC-05 (auto, IDB-4; BLOCKING)** GIVEN an alias chain of depth 9, a cycle, a self-alias, and an alias equal to a live `def_id`, WHEN boot runs, THEN each is refused `ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS(def_id)`; a chain of depth `ALIAS_DEPTH_MAX` 8 loads and resolves transitively at restore only.
- **AC-06 (auto, IDB-4, IDB-28; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a fixture catalogue whose defs are supplied in **descending** `type_id` order (so insertion order cannot pass by accident), WHEN `defs_of_class(MOD)` is called, THEN it returns an array ascending by `type_id` (the roster's ids from `roster_golden.json`); and a lint over the query surface asserts that no public query returns a Dictionary or an iteration over one, while the `def_id`-keyed map is used for lookup only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "no query exposes a Dictionary iteration order" was untestable as written)*.
- **AC-07 (auto, IDB-26, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the field `mass_g`, WHEN each value is loaded, THEN `40000.5` is refused (non-integral); `1e9` is refused on range (above 500000), not on integrality; the string `"40000"`, a missing field, `0`, `500001` and any integer above 2^53 (for example 9007199254740993) are refused; `1` and `500000` are accepted; `40000.0` is accepted and stored as the integer 40000 (`typeof` is `TYPE_INT`); every refusal is `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, "mass_g")`; no float survives in the frozen table.
- **AC-08 (auto for the refusal; lint for the shipped files, IDB-5, IDB-21, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a valid fixture, WHEN each key in this exact list is added to one def in turn, THEN the result is `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, key)`: `damage, accuracy, armour, range, durability, dps, xp, unlock, price, value, value_gold, sell, buy, cost, vendor, shop, recipe, ingredients, build_cost, editable, rarity_weight, drop_rate, set_bonus, win, escape, quest, story_flag, lore_text, appears_as, displayed_as, fake_mass, lure_sprite` and the wildcard shapes `craft_*` and `hallucination_*`; the rulings add `speed`, `noise`, `v_carry` and `carry_speed` so that "mass never changes speed or noise" is machine-enforced; the CI lint runs the same scan over the shipped `assets/data/items/*.json`. The list is the single IDB-5 list, and AC-22 reads it rather than naming keys of its own *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `range` and `durability` were forbidden by AC-22 but missing here)*.
- **AC-09 (auto, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the Graph's LS-3 node-kind and LS-4 port-type boot tables, WHEN compared with the catalogue's read-only mirror, THEN they are equal; a mirror that differs by one id or name is refused `ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR(table)`.
- **AC-10 (lint, IDB-22, IDB-26; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the folder `assets/data/items/`, WHEN scanned, THEN it contains exactly `modules.json`, `tools.json`, `consumables.json`, `parts.json`, `curios.json`, `meta.json`, `ids.lock.json`, and no file or key that names a lure, a band or what a def is dressed as.
- **AC-11 (spike, IDB-26; owner technical-director; BLOCKING for the first export build)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "no gate until recorded" could not fail; the spike now has an owner and a pass line: it FAILS if any clause below does not hold, and the first export build is blocked until it is recorded.)* GIVEN a Godot 4.7.2 `template_release` export built with the project's release preset (include filter `assets/data/*.json`, ADR-0007 § 1.6; this AC is the Item DB slice of spike CONTENT-EXP-1) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync: was "the default include filters")*, WHEN the exported build starts, THEN `assets/data/items/*.json` is present and parses through `JSON.new().parse()` (or the filter change is recorded); a deliberately malformed file reports `get_error_line()` and `get_error_message()`; JSON integral numbers arrive as floats, confirming the integrality check; and an integer above 2^53 loses precision, confirming the refusal (the engine reference has no scripting or core module for this domain; confidence MEDIUM). The `var_to_bytes` byte sizes of IDB-15 were measured on 4.7.2 on 2026-10-27 and are recorded here as the first spike result *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **AC-73 (auto, IDB-2, F-8; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a fixture class with 999 defs and then 1000 defs, WHEN boot runs, THEN 999 loads and 1000 is refused `ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID(class, id)`; GIVEN module `type_id`s, THEN 1001 and 1999 are accepted and 1000 (the reserved base) and 2000 are refused (orchestrator ruling B8).
- **AC-74 (auto, IDB-3, IDB-15; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a serialised `ItemInstance`, a `SalvageRecord` and a holder snapshot built from the roster, WHEN the bytes are scanned for the keys `type_id` and `mass_g`, THEN neither appears and the `def_id` string does.
- **AC-84 (auto, IDB-28; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the FROZEN catalogue, WHEN `has("mod_scrubber")`, `has("nope")`, `def_by_type_id(1001)`, `def_by_type_id(9999)`, `def("nope")` and `resolve_alias` of a renamed id are called, THEN the answers are true, false, the scrubber def, `NO_DEF`, `NO_DEF` and the successor id; none throws; `resolve_alias` outside the restore path returns `NO_DEF`.
- **AC-85 (auto, IDB-1, IDB-26; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the FROZEN catalogue (an `ItemCatalog` built with injected data and the `graph_tables_stub`, no static state), WHEN a caller mutates a returned def field, a returned array (`ports`, `verbs`, `defs_of_class`) or a nested element of one, THEN the next `def()` and `defs_of_class()` answers are unchanged (the arrays are read-only copies; `make_read_only` is shallow, so the test mutates a nested element, not only the top level); two catalogues built in one process do not share state; there is no public reload; the observable state log reads UNLOADED, PARSED, VALIDATED, FROZEN in order *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **AC-86 (auto, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** GIVEN every refusal case in AC-03, 05, 07, 08, 15, 35, 87 and 88, WHEN collected, THEN the set of distinct codes returned in the loader's `Array[BootError]` is exactly the members of the **code enum** in the source ({`ITEM_BOOT_TYPE_ID`, `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`, `ITEM_BOOT_PORT`, `ITEM_BOOT_REPAIR`, `ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR`, `ITEM_BOOT_ALIAS`}), asserted against the enum rather than against a logged string; no case calls `assert` or `push_error`; on REFUSED the catalogue is terminal, serves no def, and the kernel reports a BOOTING failure. `ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR` is emitted by AC-09 (and by the stub-mismatch case there), which is what makes the set complete *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text could not pass because no listed AC emitted `ITEM_BOOT_MIRROR`; AC-09 is now in the list)*. (Cases: AC-03, 05, 07, 08, 09, 15, 35, 87, 88.)
- **AC-87 (auto for the invariants, lint for the ranges, F-1, F-7; BLOCKING for (a), ADVISORY for (b))** (a) GIVEN `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` equal to or above `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`, `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` equal to `THROW_RANGE_TILES` (3), or a `WANE_TICKS` below 1, WHEN boot runs, THEN `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD` (the radius case reports `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(cons_glowstick, radius_tiles)`); GIVEN `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` 1.5, 2 and 2.5, THEN `RADIUS_PX` is 72, 96 and 120 (`int(round(x * 48))`, once, no float survives); GIVEN 2.99 (just below `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3), THEN boot accepts it and `RADIUS_PX` is 144, and GIVEN exactly 3 it is refused, so the strict-below boundary is tested from both sides *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: only the refusal half was tested)*; GIVEN `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM` 1000, THEN `f(9000)` is 1000, `f(4500)` is 625 and `f(0)` is 250; `rem` 3599 gives `f` 999 under the default wane and `rem` 3600 gives 1000. (b) GIVEN a knob outside its published guidance range in Tuning Knobs, WHEN the range lint runs, THEN it prints a warning naming the knob and does not fail the build.
- **AC-90 (lint, IDB-30; BLOCKING, BLOCKED-ON Derelict Authoring)** GIVEN any authored placement whose `def_id` is not in the catalogue, WHEN the content lint runs, THEN it fails with `PLACEMENT_DEF_UNKNOWN` (Derelict Authoring's id, a BOOT and CI rule; registry: `content_lint_ids`).

### B. Fields, ports and the Graph-facing numbers (IDB-5 to IDB-9, IDB-31; F-6, F-12)

- **AC-12 (auto, IDB-5, IDB-23, IDB-31; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the roster, WHEN every def is checked, THEN `carry_class` equals its class default; `stack_max` is 1 for every CARRIED and HAND def and for every def with `mass_g` at or above `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`; `dialect` is `foundry`; `grade` is 0; no def carries a dialect stat override; and no family has two defs with different stats (this absorbs AC-57).
- **AC-13 (auto, IDB-9; BLOCKING)** GIVEN every module def, WHEN checked, THEN `hp_start` is in 1 to `HP_FULL` (10000); a def at 0 or 10001 is refused `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD`.
- **AC-14 — withdrawn**, replaced by the golden-table check AC-80 (several listed values are Item DB placeholders, not Graph facts).
- **AC-15 (auto for refusals; lint for the shipped catalogue, IDB-7, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** (a) GIVEN a def with port id 102, a port `(301, 0, 0)` on node kind 20, or a port id absent from LS-4, WHEN boot runs, THEN `ITEM_BOOT_PORT(def_id, local_index)`. (b) GIVEN the shipped catalogue, THEN `mod_reactor_cell` declares 301 and not 302, `mod_radiator` declares 302 and not 301 (both confirmed by the Graph, AC-64 PASSED), `mod_heater` and `mod_lamp` declare exactly ports 202 then 902, and `mod_door_motor` declares exactly port 202.
- **AC-16 (lint, IDB-7, F-12; BLOCKING)** GIVEN every fluid port (loops 1, 3, 4), WHEN F-12 runs, THEN each has a severed leak of at least 1 per step, a D.14 seam-capacity floor (obtained by calling the Graph's `cap_seam` at `Q_MIN`, never a copy) of at least 1, `cap` at or above `rated_flow` **for the ports that have one** (101 = `R_SCR`, 104 = `R_VENT`, 103 = `MAX_CREW` 4 x `R_BREATH`, 402 = the burn rounded up to 1) and, for loop 1, a severed leak at or below `SEVER_O2_LOUD_MAX_PM` x 500 / 1000 (750 mGU/step at the default 1500); ports 301, 302, 303, 304 and 403 print `ratio_pm` `n/a`; the printed table equals the severed-leak column of IDB-7 (scrubber 200, tank 600, vent 300, pump 20 each, reservoir 20, reactor fuel 1, reactor coolant 20, radiator 20, fuel tank 2) and the `ratio_pm` column (101, 103, 104: 700; 402: 1000) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **AC-17 (lint, F-12; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a regression catalogue with `mod_o2_tank` port 103 at cap 3000, or at cap 1000 (below its rated flow 1200), or `mod_reactor_cell` port 402 at cap 1, or `mod_vent` port 104 below a retuned `R_VENT`, or the Graph's `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` retuned to 700 (103 stub 840 above the 750 bound) or to 400 (402 stub floors to 0), WHEN F-12 runs, THEN the build fails naming the port (severed leak above the bound; leak and seam floor 0; cap below rated flow) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the coupled window cases added)*; and GIVEN a catalogue with `SEVER_O2_LOUD_MAX_PM` 1000, THEN the shipped tank's own 600 mGU/step stub fails (the range floor is 1200).
- **AC-18 (lint, IDB-7, IDB-30; BLOCKING; unblocked by Layout LY-20, whose STRUCTURAL lint calls `compatible_defs(hardpoint)`)** GIVEN every Layout hardpoint declaration, WHEN `compatible_defs` runs, THEN every hardpoint has at least one compatible def or the lint prints the distinct reason (`STORE-STORE`, `SRC-SRC`, `SNK-SNK`); a LOAD hardpoint without `accepted_subtypes` is reported (heater and lamp share a port signature).
- **AC-19 (auto, F-6; BLOCKING)** GIVEN ports ordered by `local_index` and results ascending by `type_id`, WHEN `compatible_defs` is called with (1) ports {202, 101, 902}, kinds {20}; (2) ports {303}, kinds {20}; (3) ports {303}, kinds {32}; (4) ports {202, 902}, kinds {13}, no subtypes; (5) the same with subtypes {2}; (6) ports {202}, kinds {13}, subtypes {3}; (7) ports {202}, kinds {13}, no subtypes; (8) ports {902, 202}, kinds {13}; (9) the same as (4) with an empty subtype list, THEN the results are (1) [`mod_scrubber`], (2) [], (3) [`mod_coolant_reservoir`], (4) [`mod_heater`, `mod_lamp`], (5) [`mod_lamp`], (6) [`mod_door_motor`], (7) [`mod_door_motor`], (8) [] (order matters), (9) as (4) (an empty list means no filter).
- **AC-20 (integration, IDB-6; BLOCKING for both cases)** Case A: GIVEN a save with an installed `mod_scrubber` (`req_w` 600, `req_max` 600) and the catalogue then edited so that `req_w` and `req_max` are **both** 700 and the 202 cap is 2100 (`req_w` 700 against `req_max` 600 would fail boot, and a cap of 1800 would fail `POWER_CAP_LINT`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, WHEN restored, THEN the node still holds 600; after salvage and reinstall it holds 700; `catalogue_version` is unchanged. Case B (read-live fields, settled): GIVEN `mod_reactor_cell.waste_permille` edited from 600 to 500, WHEN restored, THEN the Graph's heat term uses 500 (the Graph reads the five fields live through `def_id`, never copied at M5, never persisted; OQ-8 closed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "provisional" and the drop clause removed; case B is no longer BLOCKED-ON the Graph owner)*.
- **AC-75 (auto, IDB-5, IDB-6, IDB-7; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a valid fixture, WHEN fields are set to boundaries, THEN `stack_max` 1 and 99 are accepted and 0 and 100 refused; `req_w` 0 and 5000 accepted, -1 and 5001 refused; `req_max` below `req_w` refused; `waste_permille` 0 and 1000 accepted, 1001 refused; a non-zero `subtype_id` on a node kind other than 13 refused; a ninth port (`MAX_PORTS_PER_DEF` 8) refused; each with the matching `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD` or `ITEM_BOOT_PORT`.
- **AC-76 (auto, IDB-5, IDB-21, IDB-30; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a def at `mass_g` 25000 with `stack_max` 2, a CARRIED def with `stack_max` 2, a HAND def with `stack_max` 2, and a def whose `carry_class` disagrees with its class default, WHEN boot runs, THEN each is refused `ITEM_BOOT_FIELD(def_id, field)`; `part_hull_plate` (30000 g, stack 1) loads.
- **AC-77 (lint, IDB-5, IDB-26; BLOCKING)** GIVEN every def, WHEN scanned, THEN `sprite_family` matches the art bible 8.5 grammar, no string field starts with `res://`, `cell` is one of `M96`, `P48`, `P48x96`, `P96x48`, `T80`, `glyph_id` is present on every tool and on `cons_glowstick` (the hand-rail consumable, ART:815) and absent elsewhere, and `name_key` is a key in the localisation table (until the grammar is defined: a non-empty ASCII snake_case key).
- **AC-78 (integration, IDB-6; BLOCKING, BLOCKED-ON the Graph owner's loader reply)** GIVEN `mod_scrubber` installed through M5 into a headless Graph, WHEN the node is read, THEN `req_w` is 600 and the Graph's `tier` is 0 (the def's `power_tier`, proving the loader's rename); `mod_door_motor` gives LOAD subtype 3 and `tier` 2.
- **AC-79 (auto, IDB-7; BLOCKING)** GIVEN every module def, WHEN its ports are read, THEN each consumer power port `cap` is **at or above** `req_max` x `INRUSH_PM_MAX` / 1000 (the shipped values are the equalities: scrubber 1800, pump 1200, heater 3000, lamp 120, door motor 360) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `req_w` became `req_max`; heater was 1500 and lamp 60; Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "equals" became "at or above", agreeing with the Tuning Knobs row and `POWER_CAP_LINT`, IDB-7 (g); a fixture with `req_max` raised and the cap left fails the lint)*; `mod_reactor_cell` 201 is at or above `out_w_rated` (2500 shipped); `mod_battery_cell` 203 is 3000; `mod_breaker_junction` has three 204 ports of 3000; every non-loop coupling port (901 to 903) has `cap` 0; `mod_reactor_cell` 301 and `mod_radiator` 302 are 40; `local_index` runs 0 to n-1 in declaration order with no gaps.
- **AC-80 (auto, IDB-8; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the golden fixture `tests/fixtures/item_db/idb8_golden.json` (each row tagged GRAPH-SOURCED or PLACEHOLDER), WHEN compared with the catalogue, THEN every row matches: `mod_scrubber` 600 (`req_max` 600) / 400 / 400 / tier 0 (`req_w` / `waste_permille` / `rated_out` = `R_SCR` / `power_tier`); `mod_coolant_pump` `req_max` 400 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the two `req_max` rows)*; `mod_coolant_pump` 400 / 300 / tier 1; `mod_heater` 500 (`req_max` 1000) / 1000 / tier 3; `mod_lamp` 20 (`req_max` 40) / 1000 / tier 3; `mod_door_motor` 120 / waste 0 / tier 2; `mod_reactor_cell` waste 600, `out_w_rated` 2500; `mod_battery_cell` `stock_cap` 3,600,000; `mod_coolant_reservoir` 20,000; `mod_o2_tank` 3,000,000; `mod_fuel_tank` 20,000; every module `hp_start` 10000 and `grade` 0; every module not listed has `req_w` and `waste_permille` 0 and `power_tier` -1; Graph-sourced rows are cross-checked against the Graph's data file when it exists.

### C. The roster: modules, tools, consumable, parts, curio (IDB-10 to IDB-14, IDB-31)

- **AC-21 (auto, IDB-10, IDB-31; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the index words, WHEN resolved, THEN scrubber maps to `mod_scrubber` (node kind 20); reactor cell to `mod_reactor_cell` (10) and, separately, `mod_battery_cell` (11); coolant unit to `mod_coolant_pump`, `mod_radiator` and `mod_coolant_reservoir`; hull plate to `part_hull_plate`; data core to `curio_data_core` (node kind 0); and no composite def exists.
- **AC-22 (auto, IDB-11; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the three MVP tools, WHEN read, THEN none carries any key of the IDB-5 forbidden list (which includes `damage`, `accuracy`, `armour`, `range` and `durability`, so AC-08 and this criterion cannot disagree); every `charges_max` is 0 and `tool_suppressant` is absent from the shipped data *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `tool_cutter` lists verb 8 and the seam-cut verb (`begin_seam_cut`, Assembly), `tool_welder` verbs 1 and 9 (`begin_patch`, `begin_weld`), and `tool_scanner` none (Interaction and Assembly designed).
- **AC-23 (auto, IDB-12, F-7; BLOCKING)** GIVEN `cons_glowstick`, WHEN read, THEN `mass_g` is 200, `stack_max` 4, `use_verb` `throw_glowstick`, `emits` is `{POOL_GLOWSTICK, 1000, CHEMLIGHT}`, and `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S`, `GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES`, `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FROM_PM`, `GLOWSTICK_WANE_FLOOR_PM` are 150, 2, 4, 400 and 250.
- **AC-24 (auto, IDB-13; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the five parts, WHEN read, THEN `part_duct_coupling` is 4001, 800 g, stack 4; `part_cable_splice` 4002, 600 g, stack 4; `part_pipe_fitting` 4003, 1200 g, stack 4; `part_hull_plate` 4004, 30000 g, stack 1; `part_machine_kit` 4005, 2000 g, stack 2; all are STOWED; `weight_class` is LIGHT except the plate, which is HEAVY.
- **AC-25 (lint, IDB-14, IDB-29; ADVISORY)** GIVEN an authored placement of `curio_data_core` in the MVP, WHEN the content lint runs, THEN it reports `PLACEMENT_NO_CONSUMER` as a warning; every other placed def has a consumer.
- **AC-26 (lint, IDB-6, IDB-31; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the roster, WHEN scanned for `emits`, THEN exactly `mod_lamp`, `mod_reactor_cell`, `mod_coolant_pump`, `mod_scrubber` and `cons_glowstick` carry one (five defs); node lanterns use the default `lantern_on_states` {0, 1}; every `shape_id` is a key of Emitter State's shape table (`FIXTURE`, `LANTERN`, `POOL_GLOWSTICK`), with `mod_lamp` keyed `FIXTURE`, `mod_reactor_cell`, `mod_coolant_pump` and `mod_scrubber` keyed `LANTERN` and `cons_glowstick` keyed `POOL_GLOWSTICK`, and every `hue_class` is a token of `hue_class_token_map` (registry; 2D Lighting LO-8), with `mod_lamp` `FIXTURE`, `mod_reactor_cell` `LANTERN_POWER`, `mod_coolant_pump` `LANTERN_COOLANT`, `mod_scrubber` `LANTERN_O2` and `cons_glowstick` `CHEMLIGHT`; no def carries a bare `LANTERN` hue token; and the lantern-bearing machine nodes of the registry entry `mvp_fixture_counts` (home 3, A 2, B 3 = 8 of 20) are exactly the nodes whose def is one of the three lantern defs. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*
- **AC-81 (auto, IDB-31; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the golden fixture `roster_golden.json` (one row per def, copied from the IDB-31 table at approval and signed off by the designer; the **single source for every count and for the next free id**), WHEN compared, THEN each row's `def_id`, `type_id`, node kind and subtype, `mass_g`, `weight_class`, `salvageable` and `repair_part_id` x `parts_per_full` match; every module has `scrap_count` 1; `curio_data_core` has node kind 0, 6000 g, stack 1; the next free module `type_id` equals the fixture's recorded value (1014 at this writing); no def named like a canister, a patch kit or a composite exists.

### D. ItemInstance, holders, yield and the reinstall rule (IDB-15 to IDB-18; F-5)

- **AC-27 (auto, IDB-15; BLOCKING)** GIVEN an `ItemInstance` of every class, WHEN serialised, THEN canonical zeros appear for inapplicable fields (a part instance has `condition_bp` 0, `cap_bp` 0, `patched_at_step` 0, `charge` 0), no `mass_g`, `weight_class` or `res://` path appears, a module instance has `0 < condition_bp <= cap_bp` with `cap_bp` 10000 or any value below `HP_FULL` (patched, no tie to `PATCH_CAP_BP`) and `patched_at_step` 0 exactly when `cap_bp` is 10000, and two encodings of one state are byte-identical (the cross-path proof is AC-104) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **AC-28 (auto, IDB-15; BLOCKING; merges AC-70)** GIVEN the canonical positional encoding of IDB-15 (`var_to_bytes(inst.to_array(), false)`, **not** the instance Dictionary, whose 176 bytes per instance and 2,120 per twelve could never meet any of these limits), WHEN twelve instances of mixed roster ids plus an 8-field holder header are snapshotted, THEN each serialised instance is at most 96 bytes (the `ID_MAX_LEN` 40 bound; 68 to 80 measured for roster ids), the whole holder block is at most 1,024 bytes (25 percent of `MAX_PAYLOAD` 4096; 956 measured on 4.7.2), and every integer is at most 2^53; the two worst cases (twelve of the longest roster id, 1,048 bytes; twelve at `ID_MAX_LEN`, 1,240 bytes) are recorded as ADVISORY until Save/Load answers the share request of Open Question 43 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the budget is re-derived from a measurement of the new encoding; the Dictionary stand-in is withdrawn)*.
- **AC-29 (auto for (a); integration for (b), IDB-15; BLOCKING, (b) BLOCKED-ON Suit)** (a) GIVEN a save holding 4 glowsticks and a catalogue edit lowering `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` to 2, WHEN restored, THEN the Item DB neither asserts, clamps nor deletes; the holder keeps all 4. (b) GIVEN the same state, THEN the holder refuses a further pickup while the count exceeds `stack_max` and the item stays in the world.

### E. Repair parts and consumption (IDB-13, IDB-19, IDB-20; F-3, F-4)

- **AC-35 (auto, IDB-19, F-3; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the full `(subject_kind, kind_id, loop)` space with node and edge kind numbers read from the Graph's LS-3 and LS-5 tables (never retyped), WHEN `repair_part_for` is called, THEN it is total and returns: NODE with a def, that def's pair (AC-36); NODE 50 (HULL_SEGMENT), (`part_hull_plate`, 1); NODE 1, 2, 34, 42, 51, (`NONE`, 0); EDGE DUCT on loop 1, (`part_duct_coupling`, 1); EDGE CABLE on loop 2, (`part_cable_splice`, 1); EDGE PIPE on loops 3 and 4, (`part_pipe_fitting`, 1); EDGE STRUT, (`part_hull_plate`, 1); EDGE VALVE on loop 1, (`part_duct_coupling`, 1), on loops 3 and 4, (`part_pipe_fitting`, 1); EDGE SEAM sub-edge on loops 1, 2, 3, 4, (`part_duct_coupling`, `part_cable_splice`, `part_pipe_fitting`, `part_pipe_fitting`), count 1; EDGE OPENING, (`NONE`, 0); `subject_kind` COMPARTMENT, SEAM (the `SeamRecord`) and OPENING, (`NONE`, 0); an unknown `kind_id`, (`NONE`, 0). "Seam" has two meanings: `subject_kind` 4 (the record) is `NONE`; edge kind 6 (a loop sub-edge) takes the loop's line part. A gap in the table is refused at boot with `ITEM_BOOT_REPAIR`.
- **AC-36 (auto, IDB-19; BLOCKING)** GIVEN each module def, WHEN its repair part is read, THEN `mod_reactor_cell` is (`part_machine_kit`, 2); `mod_breaker_junction`, `mod_lamp` and `mod_door_motor` are (`part_cable_splice`, 1); every other module is (`part_machine_kit`, 1).

- **AC-38 (integration, IDB-20; BLOCKING, BLOCKED-ON Interaction)** GIVEN a FULL job interrupted after its first productive step, WHEN the channel ends, THEN the part stays spent and the raised `hp` stays; and a FULL job never issues a PATCH-kind step.
- **AC-39 (integration, IDB-20, LS-23; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a DESTROYED subject, WHEN a FULL repair is requested of the headless Graph, THEN it answers `NOT_REPAIRABLE` and no part is consumed.
- **AC-40 (document checklist, IDB-20; Interaction is designed — not counted in the coverage figure)** GIVEN the Interaction GDD, WHEN read, THEN it states that `NONE` means no FULL job is offered, that a PATCH consumes no item, that each player's part is spent only when that player's first step applies, and it carries the resume-allowance recommendation as its own constant.
- **AC-41 (lint, IDB-29; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the roster, WHEN scanned for the words "patch kit" as a def, part or consumable, THEN none exists.
- **AC-42 (integration, IDB-29, G-0; BLOCKING, BLOCKED-ON Derelict Authoring's slice; manual backup; parameterised by crew size N)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: G-0 holds for N = 1 only; the test is run for N = 1, where it asserts survivability, and for N = 2, 3 and 4, where it instead asserts the documented single-scrubber deficit of F-14 and that the O2 tank lasts the stated minutes, 25 min at N = 2 and 6.25 min at N = 4.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the stated minutes are now the reachable ceiling, a `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000 scrubber: 16.7 min at N = 2 and 5.6 min at N = 4, F-14; and the harness previously "patched" all 8 faults, which three of them cannot be by a PATCH step.)* GIVEN the MVP slice templates and a headless harness that holds no part (every `part_*` count 0 throughout), WHEN each authored fault of Derelict Authoring's DA-15 table is cleared by **its own zero-part verb** (N = 1), THEN the outcome is: **#1 CLT-LEAK `p_cryo_rad` (`hp` 3800), #2 O2-SHIP `d_cryo_exposed` (`hp` 2000), #4 FUEL-DRIP `pf_feed` (`hp` 3999) and #5 the broken breaker `j_a_main` (`hp` 2000): a PATCH step** (M7 PATCH, `check_repair_step` accepts), each ending not FAILED or DESTROYED (OK or DEGRADED, `hp` at or above `HP_FAIL`) with its failure descriptor cleared or decaying, the breaker reaching `PATCH_CAP_BP` and the breaker then closed; **#3 PWR-OVL `cab_trunk`: latent**, no repair is issued unless the heater is installed, then a PATCH at the cable, with the load removed as the zero-part alternative; **#6 the pump stall `pump_a`: not a repair at all** (STARVE is a status; the node's `hp` 6000 is above `HP_FAIL`): it clears when #5's branch is served, and the harness asserts that **no PATCH is issued** for it; **the reactor SCRAM (S-1): `reset_node` (M11 / V12)**, not a PATCH, and BLOCKED with `STILL_HOT` until the room is cool; **#7 HULL-FATIGUE `seg_b_pump_n` (`hp` 6999): a PATCH is refused `ABOVE_PATCH_CAP`** (M7, `hp` at or above `PATCH_CAP_BP`), so the harness asserts the refusal and records the fault as outside the zero-part survivability claim, whose route is Derelict Authoring's G-0 (`[UNV: Derelict Authoring]`); **#8 the O2 seam: a seam weld/cap (V9), not a PATCH** (`repair_part_for` `NONE`). Across the run no part is consumed; a DESTROYED authored subject is a content defect (M7 rejects it `NOT_REPAIRABLE`). The manual backup is a playthrough with retained session notes and lead sign-off.

### F. Weight, classes and phantom salvage (IDB-21, IDB-22; F-1, F-2, F-10, F-13)

- **AC-43 (auto, F-1; BLOCKING)** GIVEN masses 4999, 5000, 24999 and 25000, WHEN `weight_class` runs, THEN LIGHT, MEDIUM, MEDIUM, HEAVY (the `>=` boundary pinned); `mod_battery_cell` 22000 is MEDIUM and `mod_coolant_pump` 30000 is HEAVY.
- **AC-44 (lint, IDB-21, F-1; BLOCKING)** GIVEN the roster, WHEN the `WEIGHT_CLASS_MARGIN_PM` lint runs, THEN the forbidden bands are the open intervals 4500-5500 and 22500-27500: masses of exactly 4500, 5500, 22500 and 27500 pass; 4501, 5499, 22501 and 27499 fail; 24000 fails; every roster mass passes (the nearest are `tool_welder` 4000, `mod_vent` 6000 and `mod_battery_cell` 22000).
- **AC-45 (auto, F-2; BLOCKING)** GIVEN 4 `cons_glowstick`, 2 `part_pipe_fitting` and 1 `part_machine_kit`, WHEN `held_mass_g` runs, THEN 5200; with a carried `mod_scrubber` added, 45200.
- **AC-46 (integration, IDB-21, IDB-13, F-13; BLOCKING, the plate cases BLOCKED-ON the Player Controller's run-rule amendment)** GIVEN a 30-tile carry of `mod_reactor_cell` (90000 g) and of `curio_data_core` (6000 g), WHEN the Controller simulates both, THEN each takes 1440 ticks and emits 1440 `NOISE_PER_TICK` units; the Controller reads no field from the def, and the def has no speed or noise key (AC-08). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): option (a) of D18. Extended to a stowed plate: (i) GIVEN a holder with `part_hull_plate` stowed and empty hands, WHEN `run` is requested for a 30-tile leg, THEN the gait is `V_WALK` and the leg takes 720 ticks and emits 720 noise units (F-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: every exact tick count below includes the Controller's `RUN_RAMP_TICKS` 6, so an unplated 30-tile empty run is 482 ticks and 1910 units, not 480 and 1920; a tolerance of 2 ticks and 10 units is allowed only where the harness cannot read the ramp)*; (ii) GIVEN the same holder carrying `mod_reactor_cell`, THEN the carry takes 1440 ticks and emits 1440 units, identical to the carry without the plate; (iii) GIVEN the plate is shed (States row 14; **the shed branch is BLOCKED-ON Open Question 34**) or consumed by a FULL job, THEN `run` is available again on the next tick and a 30-tile empty leg takes 482 ticks and emits 1910 units (480 and 1920 before the 6-tick ramp) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (iii-b) GIVEN the plate RESERVED by a FULL job (Suit SE-41), THEN `stowed_heavy` is false and `run` is available (no toggle at the reservation); the plate's pursuit-exposure cost is +238 ticks on that leg (F-13); (iv) GIVEN a holder with a full stack of ordinary stowed parts and no plate (15,200 g, below `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G`), THEN `run` is unaffected, proving the rule keys on class and not on mass.)*
- **AC-47 (auto for (a) and (b); manual for (c), IDB-21; BLOCKING, lead sign-off)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: it was one screenshot walk by one reviewer, which could not fail on a missed label; two automatic oracles are added and the walk is kept as the sign-off.)* (a) GIVEN a carried module, WHEN the Controller simulates 60 ticks, THEN the displacement is exactly 60 px (`V_CARRY` 1) and no def field is read; (b) GIVEN the scenes and string tables of the inspect card, hand rail, pause screen and HUD, WHEN scanned, THEN no label is bound to `mass_g` or `instance_mass_g` and no string contains `kg`, `weight`, `mass`, `heavy` or `lbs` (the closed word list); (c) GIVEN a carried module, WHEN a reviewer walks the closed checklist, THEN each item passes with a screenshot: the slowed gait is seen in the sprite and gait [PCI:98]; the inspect card, hand rail, pause screen and HUD show no mass number, bar or weight word; the chest-lamp beam is unobstructed in the carry frame [ART:512]; and the Suit body cue (when Suit exists) is a closed-list consequence-channel cue. With a stowed `part_hull_plate` and no module carried, the same walk shows the back-frame plate (never over the chest point or beam) and the same closed-list cue (IDB-21 binding clause). Evidence `item-db-ac-47-carry-frame-[build].png` and `item-db-ac-47-hull-plate-back-frame-[build].png`.
- **AC-48 (auto against fixtures, IDB-22, F-10; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a fixture junk def and a fixture lure band file (the MVP ships no junk def), WHEN F-10 runs against a 40000 g HEAVY lure, THEN junk 21000 g gives `tell_pm` 475, MEDIUM differs from HEAVY, `margin_ok` true: PASS; junk 26000 g gives `tell_pm` 350, same class and `margin_ok` false (inside 22500-27500): FAIL on **two** clauses (class and margin); junk 30000 g gives `tell_pm` 250 below `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` 300 and the same class: FAIL; boundary: a 6000 g MEDIUM lure with a 4200 g junk gives `tell_pm` exactly 300, LIGHT differs from MEDIUM, `margin_ok` true (800 from 5000): PASS. **SE-28 precondition** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the same junk and lure evaluated for a seat whose stowed class is HEAVY (the plate on the back) is reported as "masked, comparison not valid" and not as a pass, because Suit's `cue_load` is the max of the carried and stowed classes.
- **AC-49 (review, IDB-22; vertical slice)** GIVEN the junk defs when authored, WHEN reviewed, THEN none carries `appears_as`, `displayed_as`, `fake_mass` or `lure_sprite` and no lure data exists under `assets/data/items/` (the key part is AC-08, the folder part AC-10).
- **AC-92 (auto, Edge Cases; BLOCKING)** GIVEN a save written under `mod_scrubber.mass_g` 40000 and a catalogue with 38000 (and separately a `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` move that changes a def's class), WHEN restored, THEN the instance equals the original, `held_mass_g` uses the new mass, `catalogue_version` is unchanged and no migration runs.

### K. Performance and budget (deterministic proxies block; wall-clock bounds advise)

- **AC-68 (auto BLOCKING; performance gated on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK at the spike, B3)** GIVEN the catalogue built from `roster_golden.json`, WHEN boot-loaded and validated, THEN the **`OBJECT_COUNT` delta** across load plus freeze is **exactly 25** (23 `ItemDef` objects, the shared `NO_DEF` sentinel of IDB-28, and one `ItemCatalog`; ports, verbs and sprite-state arrays are not Objects) with an empty `BootError` list, and returns to the starting value when the catalogue is released *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier wording, "equals the count the loader is specified to create", was circular and could not fail; a loader that allocates one helper object per def now fails with a delta of 48)*, and nothing in the catalogue is a `Node` (it is scene-tree free by construction, IDB-1, so an `OBJECT_NODE_COUNT` check could never change and is not used); a wall-clock bound for load plus validation is gated at the spike on RM-DESKTOP (P99 at most 14.9 ms of the 16.6 ms ceiling) and RM-DECK (at most 30.0 ms of 33.3 ms), release export, startup read-back recorded: pending spike measurement, no figure set until measured *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `OBJECT_NODE_COUNT` can never change for a scene-tree-free catalogue; the 16 ms figure is withdrawn until measured)*.
- **AC-69 (auto BLOCKING; performance gated on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK at the spike, B3)** Split into two query families *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier 1.0 ms total for 10,000 calls was self-contradictory against `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4, and a 10,000-call loop measured 15 to 17 ms on 4.7.2)*. **(a) Non-allocating queries** (`def`, `has`, `weight_class`, `repair_part_for`, `compatible_defs` results are not built per call): GIVEN 10,000 mixed calls, THEN `OBJECT_COUNT` returns to its starting value and no instance id is allocated (blocking). **(b) Allocating queries** (`salvage_yield` and `from_array` build an `ItemInstance`): GIVEN 10,000 calls whose results are released, THEN `OBJECT_COUNT` returns to its starting value after release (blocking). The wall-clock bound for either is gated at the spike on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK (release export, P99 headroom of 10 percent), pending spike measurement; the figure of 15 to 17 ms per 10,000 calls (4.7.2, the developer's machine, which is not a reference machine) is indicative only *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*.
- **AC-70 — merged into AC-28.**
